And that will be the greatest achievement of this COSMOS Planetarium. With this confidence, I wish you all the very best for the journey ahead. I extend my best wishes to all of you. Thank you very much.
Chief Justice of India Justice Surya Kant ji, my colleague in the Union Cabinet Bhupender Yadav ji, Attorney General of India R. Venkataramani ji, National Green Tribunal (NGT) Chairman Justice Prakash Srivastava ji, all distinguished dignitaries present in this hall, ladies and gentlemen. For centuries, India has guided the world on the subject of nature and environment. With that ideological commitment, and based on our cultural experiences, India is now also providing solutions to modern climate challenges, while at the same time calling for collective efforts of humanity. I am happy that this NGT conference is carrying forward that mission. For this event, I extend my congratulations and best wishes to all the officials of NGT and all dignitaries associated with it. Friends, We all know that often the responsibility for carbon emissions has been wrongly placed on developing countries. Many of my companions from the Global South are present here. But the historical truth is that even today, India's per capita carbon emissions are less than half the global average. In developed countries, per capita carbon emissions are six times higher than India's. Six times higher. India presents this fact strongly on global platforms. And I say to every Indian - show your strength too. We have also presented our traditions and their power before the world. On these very subjects, during COP‐21 in Paris, we launched the book Convenient Action - Continuity for Change. We are establishing new benchmarks for environmental protection by taking the world along. Whether it is the vision of One Sun, One World, One Grid, or the International Solar Alliance, CDRI, Global Biofuel Alliance, International Big Cat Alliance, Mission LiFE (Lifestyle for Environment) - each of these platforms is becoming a stage for India's dedicated commitments on climate and environment. Friends, Today's India is moving forward by weaving its ancient ideas of environmental protection into a modern vision. In the past 12 years, India has made extraordinary progress in every sector related to the environment. Our major focus has been on renewable energy, and this has played a big role in environmental protection. India is the only country in the G20 - I say this before the big leaders of the G20 - the only country that has fulfilled all the commitments it made at the COP‐21 summit in Paris, and has done so ahead of schedule. This is the strength of Hindustan that I am talking about. On every parameter - non‐fossil fuel, solar energy capacity, curbing carbon emissions - India's performance is today a subject of positive discussion worldwide. Look at the figures. Twelve years ago, India's solar energy capacity was about 2 gigawatts. Twelve years ago - 2 gigawatts. If there are lawyers here, please note these figures, they may come in handy. Today it has crossed 160 gigawatts. From 2 gigawatts to 160 gigawatts in 12 years. Under the PM Surya Ghar Free Electricity Scheme, we launched the mission to make homes themselves centers of solar power generation. Under this scheme, more than 5 million homes have already installed rooftop solar panels. This is more than the total number of homes in many countries. For this, the government has provided financial assistance of up to 80,000 rupees per household. Under the KUSUM scheme, the government has installed solar pumps in the fields of 3 million farmers. We have also completed the ambitious One Nation, One Grid project. Not only this - LED bulbs in homes have proven successful in saving electricity. Crores of LED bulbs have been distributed to households. And the results of these efforts are before us today. From solar energy alone, India has succeeded in preventing nearly 200 million tonnes of carbon emissions. Friends, Along with solar energy, India has also achieved major accomplishments in other clean energy sectors. In the past 12 years, the country's wind energy capacity has tripled. Hydropower capacity has increased significantly. And now the country is working rapidly on nuclear energy generation. Those who give long speeches on the environment - when I advance nuclear energy work, they will stand in the courts to stop it. A pathway has also been created to bring private players into this sector. Because of these efforts, today India's non‐fossil energy capacity has nearly quadrupled. India is also promoting technologies like coal gasification for cleaner use of coal. Friends, India is now working rapidly in areas like clean mobility and green hydrogen. Just a few days ago, India's first hydrogen train was launched - and it is the world's longest hydrogen train. This marks the beginning of a new era of clean mobility. Friends, In 12 years, the number of electric vehicles in our country has increased nearly 950 times. 950 times. In the year before 2014, fewer than 3,000 EVs were sold in India. Last year, nearly 2.5 million EVs were sold in India. That means India is embracing the opportunity of the EV revolution with full strength, and this is also helping the environment. On our highways and expressways, modern fast‐track systems have reduced waiting at toll booths. This has stopped the fuel wastage that used to occur at toll booths. Friends, Until 2014, in five cities of our country there was less than 250 kilometers of metro network. Lawyers here should note this figure. Before 2014, only five cities had less than 250 kilometers. Today, metros run in more than 20 cities, and India has joined the countries with more than 1,000 kilometers of metro network. Friends, With nearly 100 percent electrification of railways, billions of liters of diesel burning has been avoided. The environment has been protected. Just this month, a 3,000‐kilometer‐long Dedicated Freight Corridor was inaugurated. Every train running on it carries loads equal to hundreds of trucks from one place to another. The operation of double‐stack container freight trains is historic in itself. You can imagine how greatly this helps the environment. Friends, Today, transportation is happening on about 5,000 kilometers of waterways in the country. This is the most environment‐friendly mobility. A standard inland vessel running on these waterways carries loads equal to dozens of trucks in one journey. This too is greatly helping environmental protection. Friends, All these changes in the mobility sector alone are proof that our development is both speedy and sustainable. Friends, Today, with the participation of millions of people, unprecedented work is happening in India on water conservation. I am happy that more than 70,000 Amrit Sarovars have been constructed in the country. Under the Catch the Rain campaign, nearly 15 million water harvesting structures have been built, preserving rainwater. Through the Pradhan Mantri Krishi Sinchai Yojana, more than 20 million farmers today are connected with modern irrigation facilities. Initiatives like Per Drop More Crop are also saving water. Shri Anna - that is, millets - are another great successful example. India is promoting the cultivation of millets, which consume less water. In recent years, millet production has increased in the country, and exports have risen to about 150,000 tonnes. This is creating new opportunities for farmers, while also saving water. And because of this model, the world is joining us. Thanks to India's efforts, 2023 was celebrated as the International Year of Millets. Friends, Under the National Mission on Natural Farming, today about 1.25 million hectares in the country have developed 24,000 clusters of natural farming. Not only this, nearly 3,000 varieties of climate‐resilient crops have also been developed in the country. By promoting agro‐forestry, we are also reducing carbon footprints. Friends, How wide‐ranging our efforts are can be seen in India's forest coverage. This too I mention for my lawyer friends. In the past 12 years, our very dense forest cover has increased by more than 22 percent. This is a very big achievement. Under the campaign Ek Ped Maa Ke Naam (One Tree in the Name of Mother), crores of trees have been planted across the country. And this campaign continues. Friends, Plastic waste has also been a major challenge for the environment. That is why India has banned single‐use plastic. With the mantra of reduce, reuse, and recycle, we have promoted the circular economy. Today, thousands of tonnes of waste are being recycled every day in India. Through the Gobardhan Yojana, cow dung and other organic waste in villages are being converted into useful resources. Our effort is that waste should not be seen as a problem, but transformed into a resource under the Waste to Wealth model. Friends, All these figures are not just India's development statistics - they are India's contributions for the future of humanity. Friends, Our cultural thought is - "Mata bhumih, putro aham prithivyah" - meaning, this earth is our mother and we are her children. With this thinking, India is working to build a responsible and sustainable future. I am confident that the discussions in this conference, and the new ideas and solutions that emerge from it, may become a great support for future generations. Once again, I extend my many best wishes to all of you for this conference. Thank you very much.
Johar! Heartiest greetings to all of you across the country who are connected with this celebration of Janjatiya Gaurav Diwas. On the birth anniversary of Dharti Aaba Bhagwan Birsa Munda, I bow to him with deep reverence. Janjatiya Gaurav Diwas is a national occasion that is linked to the pride of the tribal community and to the inspiration of Bhagwan Birsa Munda. With this same spirit, programmes are being organized across the country today to mark Janjatiya Gaurav Diwas. The nation is proudly remembering its tribal heroes and heroines. Especially in Gadchiroli, Maharashtra, such a massive gathering is taking place. As far as my eyes can see, there are people everywhere. Gadchiroli has a rich tribal cultural heritage. The heroic stories of Raghuji Bhangare, Bhagoji Naik, and Veer Baburao Shedmake are connected to this land. On this occasion, I respectfully pay tribute to all tribal heroes. I also congratulate all the Governors, respected Chief Ministers, Deputy Chief Ministers, and every public representative participating in this event from across the country. Friends, The observance of Janjatiya Gaurav Diwas, which began on , has now become a national celebration. Last year, on the 150th birth anniversary of Bhagwan Birsa Munda, more than two lakh programmes were organised across the country. Crores of citizens participated in these events. There was a time when many commemorative days and celebrations in our country were shaped through a foreign perspective. But today, India is proudly remembering its heritage, its history, and its heroes with indigenous self-respect. This is a symbol of a major transformation in our national consciousness. Friends, Whether it was Tirot Sing's struggle in the Khasi Hills, the Kol movement in Chota Nagpur, or the Santhal revolution led by Sidho and Kanho, the tribal community has always raised its voice against injustice. On the land of Mangarh, the followers of Govind Guru made the supreme sacrifice. Rani Gaidinliu, Tantya Bhil, and many other heroes and heroines inspired the nation through their courage. Great personalities such as Alluri Sitarama Raju and Rani Durgavati also energized tribal movements. Alongside all these stories of courage, Bhagwan Birsa Munda's Ulgulan and his struggle at such a young age continue to inspire crores of Indians even today. Friends, On the occasion of Janjatiya Gaurav Diwas today, more than 170 projects related to tribal development have been inaugurated across different states of the country, and foundation stones have been laid for more than 70 projects. In Mizoram, a museum dedicated to the great freedom fighter Ropuiliani Ji has been inaugurated. In Arunachal Pradesh, a new building of the Tribal Research Institute has also been inaugurated. In addition, among the projects being inaugurated and launched are educational institutions, healthcare facilities, marketing centres for tribal products, and numerous initiatives related to expanding basic amenities in villages. Across different states of the country, projects worth nearly ₹3000 crore have been inaugurated or had their foundation stones laid. I extend my heartfelt congratulations to all of you and to the people of the country for these projects. Friends, Today's programme is taking place at a time when two major achievements of the country are being discussed everywhere. The first achievement is that nearly 50 crore people have overcome extreme poverty and risen out of it. The second achievement is that the country has become free from Naxalism and Maoist terror. Both these achievements are deeply connected with the lives of our tribal brothers and sisters. A few weeks ago, the World Bank report stated that nearly 50 crore people in India have emerged from extreme poverty over the past 12-13 years. This is a very large number, 50 crore people. I consider this one of the greatest achievements of the country since Independence. This achievement belongs to the entire nation. The credit does not belong to any one government. Behind it is the collective strength of society, the effort and hard work of every citizen. The decisions taken by the central government and the schemes launched for tribal welfare in recent years have also played a major role in this achievement. I congratulate all the citizens of the country, especially my tribal brothers and sisters, for this accomplishment. Friends, Such a large number of people rising out of poverty is a major transformation in itself. This transformation is the story of a long journey from resolve to achievement. It has created confidence that India can fulfil its resolutions and that every Indian can achieve great goals. We had taken a pledge that those who had remained deprived of the benefits of development for decades would be given priority. As our resolutions began turning into reality on the ground, a chain of positive changes began to emerge. Our government devoted its full strength to those tribal regions that were among the most backward of the backward, where even the most basic facilities of life had not reached. For the PM-JANMAN Scheme and the Dharti Aaba Janjatiya Gram Utkarsh Abhiyan, we allocated a budget of more than ₹1 lakh crore. Our effort was that the families who received facilities the latest after Independence should now receive them the fastest. Friends, Priority to the deprived, priority to the deprived, this has been our priority. We built more than four crore permanent houses for poor families across the country. Electricity was provided to 18,000 villages where it had not reached even after so many decades of Independence. Through the Ayushman Bharat scheme, free healthcare worth up to ₹5 lakh was provided. More than 1.75 lakh Ayushman Arogya Mandirs were established across the country, allowing healthcare benefits to reach even remote tribal areas. When poor families received toilets and sanitation facilities improved, the risk of many diseases was also reduced. Friends, To defeat poverty, it was equally important to increase income opportunities for poor families. Through the Mudra Yojana, we provided collateral-free loans. Under this scheme, assistance worth lakhs of crores of rupees was provided. The benefits of all these schemes reached the poor, reached Dalits, reached backward communities, and reached our tribal brothers and sisters as well. This created a new confidence within them to move forward. Friends, We also gave special attention to those areas that had remained behind in development for a long time. A special campaign was launched in more than 100 Aspirational Districts and over 500 Aspirational Blocks for development, better facilities, and protection of citizens' rights. A large number of these regions have significant tribal populations. Now, under the PM Krishi Dhan Dhanya Yojana, special attention is also being given to 100 districts that are backward in agriculture. Our effort is to create new opportunities for farmers, poor families, and tribal communities in these regions. These collective efforts, and the mantra of Sabka Saath, Sabka Vikas, have given new confidence to the poor of the country, and crores of poor people have succeeded in overcoming poverty. Friends, The second achievement that I referred to is freedom from Naxalism and Maoism. The people of Gadchiroli, and of tribal regions across the country, know very well that Naxalite violence caused the greatest harm to our tribal communities. In 2014, nearly 125 districts of the country were affected by Maoism and Naxalism. Hundreds of incidents of violence occurred every year. Thousands of innocent people lost their lives in these incidents. A very large number of them were our tribal brothers and sisters. But friends, During that period, the people affected by Maoism and Naxalism, these very brothers and sisters of mine, had been left to their fate by the governments of that era. Their rights had been taken away. Everyone sat in silence and remained quiet. You would be shocked to know that these brothers and sisters from our tribal communities were not even allowed to vote. The governments of that time remained mere spectators and sat with their eyes closed. Maoists and Naxalites would take away small children from poor mothers, and nobody could do anything. Naxalites would abduct the daughters of our homes, and everyone could only watch helplessly in anguish. Friends, Now that Maoism and Naxalism have been defeated, those mothers, the mothers and sisters of my tribal community, are showering countless blessings upon us. The sister who now feels safe is blessing us. The child whose childhood was once snatched away is today joining us as a young person. He is receiving opportunities for education and employment. In Chhattisgarh, areas like Bastar, where people were once afraid to go, I myself celebrated Diwali with our security forces in the heart of that region just a short while ago. This Diwali, people in all former Naxal-affected regions, including Gadchiroli, have received not only the light of lamps but also the light of hope. Friends, Today, our tribal society, our Janjatiya community, is contributing enthusiastically to the progress of the nation. Today, even the leadership of the country is in the hands of a daughter from the tribal community. We are all proud of President Droupadi Murmu ji. Friends, Today is the time for us to draw inspiration from Bhagwan Birsa Munda and devote all our energy to fulfilling the dream of a Developed India. With this call, and while bowing to Bhagwan Birsa Munda, I once again extend my heartfelt greetings to all of you on Janjatiya Gaurav Diwas. And I urge you that every year, 15th November should be eagerly awaited. The birth anniversary of Bhagwan Birsa Munda, and even more than that, Janjatiya Gaurav Diwas, is the pride of the entire nation and the self-respect of the entire nation. Every 15th November, with greater strength than before, with greater enthusiasm than before, and on a greater scale than before, we should continue to celebrate this day. On Janjatiya Gaurav Diwas, we should keep taking new resolutions and dedicate all our strength towards fulfilling those resolutions within a fixed timeframe. What could not be achieved during so many decades after Independence, we must accomplish within a short period. Once again, as I look at the screen and see people gathered at so many places, I can see the enthusiasm and excitement in all of them. This very sight has become the pride, glory, and grandeur of Janjatiya Gaurav Diwas. For such a successful effort, all of you from the tribal community deserve congratulations. I wholeheartedly congratulate all of you. I thank all of you very much. Johar.
Namaskar. You all have organized this Khelo India Samvad program in such a wonderful way. I extend my heartfelt congratulations to all of you. Through technology, I warmly greet my colleagues from the Union Cabinet connected with this program, all Chief Ministers, many ministers from state governments, my colleagues in Parliament, and the lakhs of volunteers of My Bharat. Today, athletes from across the country and distinguished personalities from the sports world have also joined us in this program. You all know, friends, Two days later, on 29th August, it will be National Sports Day. I extend advance greetings of Sports Day to all of you, to the youth across the country, and to all companions connected with the sports world. This day is also an occasion to remember one of Mother India's great sons, Major Dhyan Chand ji. A hundred years ago, the Indian Army's hockey team went on a tour to New Zealand, and Dhyan Chand ji was part of that team. That tour laid the foundation of 100 years of sports relations between India and New Zealand. A few days ago, I visited New Zealand. There too, I remembered him. The relationships that Major Dhyan Chand ji laid the foundation for, today India is continuously working for their prosperity. Friends, On this 15th August from the Red Fort, I placed India's sports vision before the nation. When I speak of sports, it is not limited only to winning medals. It is a campaign to combine India's sports power and India's youth power together in building a developed India. I have always said - jo khelega, woh khilega - "whoever plays, will blossom." And blossoming in sports is not only about winning on the field. Blossoming in sports means blossoming of personality, blossoming of family, and increasing the pride of the nation. Friends, When a player succeeds, that success is not only his or hers. That success builds the identity of his family, increases their prestige. The school and college where he studied, their prestige increases. The identity of his district and state becomes stronger. That is, the world begins to recognize that city or state by the name of that player. And when many such successes make a region shine on the global sports map, the world's perspective of that city also changes. In today's program, players from hundreds of districts of the country are connected. Every district has the strength to shine on the world's sports map. Understanding this strength, we must carry forward this dialogue program. Friends, This time from the Red Fort I also drew the nation's attention to a very big challenge related to sports. This challenge is connected with the Olympics. A bitter truth is that in the Olympics, of all the different sports, India's teams participate in only half of them. That means for a very large part of the medal tally, we do not even compete. And this has been going on for decades. In the 2012 Olympics, India participated in only 13 sports. In more than 20 sports, India had no participation. And in those 20+ sports, many are such that people in the country may hardly have even heard their names. Other countries win medals in those sports, and we do not even participate. To increase our medal tally, we must achieve mastery in those sports too. For example, surfing and sports climbing - we have such a large coastline, such a vast mountainous region, where there can naturally be interest in these sports. We have both the demography and the geography for water sports and winter sports. Yet we are not visible in such sports globally. Therefore, we must explore possibilities in different districts where an ecosystem for a particular sport can be developed. And in this, today's program has a very big role. Friends, When we talk of preparing for the 2036 Olympics, our focus should be that we must prepare today those players who will qualify and compete in different sports in 2036. That is why, from the Red Fort, I announced a major talent hunt campaign to identify players between the ages of 5 and 15. And in this too, we must not only see which child is interested in which sport, but also which child is fit for which sport. We must train the child accordingly. And I assure you all, whatever support is needed from the government, the government will walk shoulder to shoulder with you. Friends, Our government is fully committed to building a modern sports ecosystem of the 21st century in India. Today, from sports universities to modern training centers, a system is being created in the country where at every level - from identifying talent to bringing it to the world stage - support is available. We are also increasing cooperation with other countries of the world to share sports science, training, coaching, and experiences. Friends, How serious the government is about sports, this very event is an example of it. Khelo India Samvad is not just a program to talk about sports. It is a platform of dialogue where every idea can give a new direction to the country's sports vision. And along with this, the youth dialogue happening here is a great medium to connect your ideas and aspirations with the dreams of the nation. In this program, what the youth think about the sports ecosystem, talent development, governance, and developed India, what their suggestions are - these will be heard and included in the policy process. Therefore, I urge you all to make maximum meaningful use of this platform, bring new ideas. These new ideas of youth power will become the strength for achieving a developed India. Friends, We often hear that for a healthy society, for a good family system, for a better work culture, sports‐person spirit is very necessary. And this sports‐person spirit is very difficult to learn without going to the sports field. Sports not only teach us to become champions, but also teach us to become better competitors. To rise, to fall, to rise again after falling - sports bring this spirit within us. Friends, A big example of how sports change life is our para‐athletes. When someone sees para‐athletes on the field, their perspective on life changes. And when they achieve something, along with that athlete, their entire family gets a new life. The parents, brothers, sisters, who saw their child struggling in the early days - for them, the success of that differently‐abled athlete gives their dreams and courage a new height. Friends, I myself have always been inspired by such players. For example, you have all heard the name - in archery, our daughter, an amazing player, you know her name? Sheetal. When she won a medal in the Paralympics, the whole of Jammu & Kashmir, the whole of Hindustan was filled with pride. Similarly, our daughter Avani Lekhara, who has won medals twice in the Paralympics. Whenever I meet daughter Avani, I feel very proud. Whether it is Sumit Antil, Nitesh Kumar, or just recently you must have heard the name of our Jhandu Kumar - such para‐athletes, such companions, inspire us too. Friends, Sports are a guarantee of a better life. Sports make us fit to live a better life, improve our quality of life. We all know about Fauja Singh ji. Last year, at more than 100 years of age, Fauja Singh ji passed away. Love for sports, fitness - how it continues lifelong, Fauja Singh ji was an example of this. His life is a great inspiration that shows how sports and quality of life are connected. Friends, Another very big benefit of sports is that true players develop the willpower to stay away from drugs and intoxication. Today, when such a big challenge of addiction is before the youth, sports play a very meaningful role in this too. Friends, I am happy that today in India sports have emerged as an excellent profession. Society's perspective toward sports has changed. Today you can see, people in our country are participating in triathlons, running 5K and 10K races. Friends are coming together to complete challenges like Hyrox. Someone is celebrating doing a headstand for the first time, someone is playing pickleball and badminton with friends and family. These are small things, but if you look at them from a larger perspective, you see a big change. The change is that sports are now becoming part of our lives. If timetables are being made for study and career, then sports are also getting a place in the daily routine. And this is the change, this is the transformation, because of which trust in India's sports future is increasing. Friends, A sports culture in the country is built only when ordinary people are connected with it. When schools consider sports periods not as co‐curricular, but as part of life. When this happens, the talent pool increases, and children who want to take sports as a career also get support from their families. That is why I say - more important than screens are sports centers. More important than PlayStation is the playground. And this will happen only when all you young companions carry forward this approach. Friends, I have great trust in the capability and success of India's youth power. I want to say to every youth of the country - whether on the sports field or in society, your participation is India's strength. If you love sports, then many avenues are being created to connect with it. Now new sports equipment has come, new technology has come. Training and coaching methods have changed. The needs of organizing sports, of competition, of disciplines have changed. Equipment design, sports technology, sports medicine, nutrition, psychology, analytics - in all these areas new opportunities are being created for youth. You must not miss these opportunities. Therefore, in this dialogue, present your ideas openly. This participation of youth power is the greatest strength of developed India. Once again, I extend my best wishes to all you young companions. Thank you. Vande Mataram.

Chairman and Editor-in-Chief of the India Today Group, Shri Arun Puri ji, the agricultural scientists, agri-entrepreneurs, farmer friends, and all distinguished guests, ladies and gentlemen who have come from across the country for this programme. First of all, I wholeheartedly congratulate the India Today Group for this innovative initiative. When the country launched the cleanliness campaign, the India Today Group participated in it very enthusiastically. Now you have organised this Kisan Mela, and dedicated two full days to it. This is a very positive beginning. I congratulate all of you. And Arun ji speaks such excellent Hindi, so double congratulations for that. Friends, This event is taking place at a time when the country is in a festive mood. Two or three days ago, we celebrated the festival of Diwali, and now the spirit of the Chhath Mahaparva is all around us. The festival of Chhath is deeply connected with agricultural life, and in our country, most festivals are linked with farming and agriculture. Bihu, Pongal, Onam, Baisakhi, Sarhul, Pola, many such festivals are connected with agriculture. In other words, farming is our culture, our tradition, and the foundation of our social life. Friends, From the Red Fort, I had spoken about the seven streams of strength for a Viksit India, and among these, agriculture is a principal stream. Building a Viksit India is simply not possible without developing agriculture, without making farming smarter, and without empowering farmers. That is why the importance of such farmer conferences becomes even greater. Friends, You can understand the relationship between farming and the country's progress through two important facts. Recently, a World Bank study on poverty was released, and it is being discussed across the world. It states that within just 12-13 years, 50 crore poor people in India have defeated poverty. It also states that poverty has declined very rapidly in villages. This means that a large number of small farmers and agricultural labourers have overcome poverty. Now look at the coincidence. During the past decade, India's agricultural growth rate has remained above 4 percent. This is the first decade after Independence in which the country's agriculture sector has achieved an average growth rate of more than 4 percent, for the first time since Independence. And during this same period, a large number of poor people in villages have defeated poverty. But unfortunately, people trapped in angles and agendas of news coverage fail to notice this achievement of the country. Friends, Those engaged in farming and experts connected with agriculture know how necessary continuous change in agricultural policies and methods is. Those who governed for decades became satisfied merely because the country's farmers had filled the grain warehouses of the nation. But what did farmers receive after that? Merely filling grain warehouses is not enough. Over time, the world changed, markets changed, people's needs changed, eating habits changed, the weather changed, and technology changed. Along with these changes, we needed to empower our farmers in a new way. But earlier, even this was not given attention. Friends, Over the past decade, the country took steps to match this pace, and the results are before all of us. Today, India exports agricultural products worth more than four lakh crore rupees every year, and this money is increasing the income of our farmers. Today, India is number one in the world in milk production. Today, India is number one in pulse production. India is number one in jute production. India has also become number one in rice production. It is number two in the world in wheat production. It is number two in the production of cotton, fruits, and vegetables. It is number two in fish production, and India is also the world's largest producer of millets. These are not just statistics. They show how much the capability of India's farmers has grown. Friends, One more thing is necessary for the prosperity of India's farmers. It is necessary that they do not remain dependent only on traditional crops such as wheat, rice, and sugarcane. I repeat, farmer brothers should not remain dependent only on wheat, rice, and sugarcane. Therefore, the government is also placing emphasis on other crops and other activities related to agriculture. Let me remind the India Today Group of something. The word "revolution", kranti, is something the India Today Group is particularly fond of. Therefore, I am now going to talk about a few revolutions. Friends, There was a time when the country took a historic step towards self-reliance in food grain production through the Green Revolution. After that, the White Revolution made India the world's largest milk producer. Today, I will give you some more figures that show how this journey is now moving forward with remarkable momentum. Over the past decade, India's milk production has increased by nearly 70 percent, 70 percent. Egg production has also increased by nearly 90 percent, 90 percent. And if we talk about the Blue Revolution, India's fish production has more than doubled in a decade. New opportunities have emerged in the field of beekeeping through the Sweet Revolution. Honey production has increased from around 80 thousand tonnes to 150 thousand tonnes. In horticulture as well, India's production has increased from 280 million tonnes to more than 350 million tonnes. Friends, When we connect all these revolutions with a broader vision, multiple sources of income are created for the country's farmers, and this is very essential for the future of agriculture. Let me give you another example. Another revolution, the solar power revolution. This revolution has transformed the food provider into an energy provider. Under the PM-KUSUM Scheme, nearly 12 lakh solar pumps have already been distributed. This is reducing farmers' electricity bills, and farmers have also begun earning income by installing solar plants on their fields. Along with production, we have also focused on productivity and on farmers' profits. Friends, Recently, I completed 25 years as the head of government. When I was the Chief Minister of Gujarat, I was continuously working to modernise agriculture even then. At that time, I started large agricultural festivals. I remember that in May and June, in temperatures of 40-45 degrees Celsius, hundreds of agricultural scientists, agricultural experts, and progressive farmers would come together. We would prepare Kisan Raths and travel in them. We would go to villages and to fields. We would visit farmers' fields and interact directly with them. What type of soil the area had, what the availability of water was, which crops could perform well there, what kind of farming was required, every such issue would be discussed directly with farmers. Our effort was that the knowledge present in scientists' laboratories should directly reach farmers' fields. In other words, we moved forward with the concept of Lab to Land, and Gujarat's farmers benefited greatly from it. Friends, The experience from my Gujarat days has been extremely useful after coming to the central government. In 2014, there were only a few hundred soil-testing laboratories in the country. Today, their number has increased to more than 8,400. Just as pathology laboratories are expanding in medical science, today soil-testing laboratories are expanding. Today, there are more than 750 Krishi Vigyan Kendras across the country. We are also prioritising the protection of animals from diseases. To protect livestock from Foot and Mouth Disease, more than eight crore farmers' animals have received free vaccinations. During COVID, we provided free vaccines, and everyone remembers that. But this is Modi; he provides free vaccines even to animals. Friends, Often, some people with old ways of thinking believe that the use of large-scale technology will not be possible in our villages. The Namo Drone Didis have proved such people wrong. Once, while speaking with all the Drone Didis through video conferencing, I was talking to a daughter from a village, perhaps in Jharkhand. She told me, "Now my status has changed." I asked, "Why?" She said, "The entire village calls me a pilot, and even my younger brothers-in-law call me 'Pilot Bhabhi'." Just imagine how much self-respect increases. At present, the Namo Drone Didis are using drones for spraying fertilisers and pesticides. Now the scope of their use is also being expanded. Friends, Today's India is also expanding the role of engineering in agriculture. Our agricultural scientists, engineers, and youth are together developing such tools and machines that are reducing the cost of farming. In some places, machines are being developed that are making sowing and harvesting easier. And I would especially like to urge the people from industry present here, those engaged in manufacturing equipment for farmers, as well as our startup founders, that whatever equipment you build from now on should be powered by solar energy, because we are taking solar power to every farm. Just as the whole world has now rushed towards electric vehicles, I am in favour of making every type of agricultural tool solar-powered. And I am also making considerable efforts in that direction. But I would urge all MSMEs and small-scale industries to focus on this. This is going to become a very large area of revolution. Not only that, sensor-based equipment equipped with AI technology is now being developed that can provide precise information to farmers and can be affordable even for the smallest farmers. Arrangements are now being developed for soil testing through mobile phones. You simply place a little soil on the mobile-based device, and your phone immediately provides a report identifying which crop your soil is suitable for, how much fertilizer is required, and what type of fertilizer is needed. In other words, just see how rapidly change is taking place. Today, from agricultural engineering to agri-startups, India is full of opportunities. We must transform our food providers into agri-preneurs, that is, agricultural entrepreneurs. Work is now progressing rapidly across the country on how value addition can take place right where production happens. Friends, In the past few years, small farmers, working together with the government, have built a large network of 10,000 FPOs, that is, Farmer Producer Organizations. These are turning groups of small farmers into value creators and connecting them with the food-processing business. I clearly remember one incident from my days in Gujarat. In Gujarat, we introduced the Jyotigram Yojana. We separated agricultural feeders from domestic feeders and began supplying as much electricity to farmers as possible. This greatly benefited farmers. There are some villages near Gandhinagar where most people cultivate chillies. But farmers there faced a problem. If production increased, prices would fall, and if production declined, earnings would decline. They suffered in both situations. If too many chillies were produced, they could not sell them. But after the arrival of electricity through the Jyotigram Yojana, they started converting chillies into red chilli powder. They packaged that powder, got advertisements made, printed photographs of personalities on the packaging, and began selling chilli powder on a large scale. Through this value addition, they became highly successful earners. Earlier, they could not even obtain a proper price for their chillies, but now their products became brands. This shows what becomes possible when attention is given to every small aspect. Efforts to connect small farmers with export markets are also progressing rapidly. Friends, Our policy and our intent are absolutely clear. It is our priority that farming should not become a burden on farmers. To ensure affordable fertilizers for farmers, the country has spent ₹16 lakh crore over the last 12 years. Friends, this is an enormous figure. Sixteen lakh crore rupees is more than the annual budget of many countries. Amid the war currently taking place around Iran and the Gulf countries, a bag of DAP fertilizer that costs ₹5,000 is being made available to Indian farmers for only ₹1,300. A bag of urea that costs ₹3,000 in the international market is being provided to Indian farmers for less than ₹300. Friends, I would like to mention one more subject, and I have spoken about this before as well. For your network, what I am about to say today is exclusive. Friends, When the war situation in West Asia was escalating, the price of crude oil rose from 70 dollars to 122 dollars per barrel within just a few weeks. At that time, India also faced a major challenge. India was in a situation where diesel prices should have been increased by at least ₹50 per litre. An increase of ₹50 per litre had become necessary. At that time, the government took a decision in the interest of farmers. We decided that the full burden of the shock in international markets would not be passed on to citizens and farmers. The government increased diesel prices by only ₹7 per litre, whereas the required increase was ₹50. The government itself absorbed a burden of ₹43 per litre. In neighbouring countries as well, diesel prices had increased by 40 to 50 percent. But that did not happen in India. Since then and up to today, the government has been bearing a loss of ₹14,000 crore every month. Every month. Yet we did not allow harm to come to the country's farmers. When the welfare of farmers is the highest priority, when there is a spirit of Citizen Devo Bhava, such sensitive decisions are taken. Friends, Amid so many discussions about the national interest, I am sure the people of Aaj Tak must be wondering that no negative headline has emerged so far. So perhaps now we should discuss something that can become a headline. Until now, I have been talking about the country, and perhaps that has not attracted as much media attention. So now let me talk about controversy. Friends, Nowadays, some people pull the Constitution out of their pockets. The country understands very well what they are doing. They seem to think that the nation's Constitution and democracy are sitting in their pocket. Even while not being in power, they remain intoxicated with the mindset of power. But when these very people were in power, what were their actions like? Let me narrate a small incident. Friends, You may remember that there was a time when people in the media used to rank the best-performing states. In one such ranking related to the economy, Gujarat was ranked number one, and at that time I was the Chief Minister of Gujarat. The institution that gave Gujarat the number one ranking was named after a former Prime Minister of India. That hint is enough; you can find the name yourselves. This ranking was based on research conducted by some of the country's most respected economists. But the people sitting in power in Delhi did not like Gujarat receiving that recognition. They formally instructed those conducting the research that things could not continue this way. They said that from then onward, no report could be published without their approval first. Friends, That was the situation then, and today those very people go around delivering lectures on tolerance and democracy. And when exactly did this happen? What happened after that? You can research and find those details yourselves. Let me now move towards concluding my remarks. Friends, Without doubt, the achievements of the country's farmers over the past years have been remarkable. To build a Viksit India, we now have to expand our efforts even further. We must create a future where farmers are prosperous, villages are prosperous, and agriculture is modern and profitable. I am confident that the deliberations taking place here will benefit the country. The government will also work upon the best suggestions and solutions that emerge from here. And since I have come among you today, I would certainly like to make one appeal. At the beginning, Anjana ji said in her introductory remarks that farmers are connected to two things: the food provider and Mother Earth. I want to ask my farmer brothers and sisters: are we really protecting this Mother Earth of ours? Ask yourselves honestly. The way we are destroying Mother Earth through excessive use of chemicals, and we are her children. We are killing this mother before our own eyes. If we do not wake up in time, if we do not make efforts to save Mother Earth, then the mother who nourishes us today may one day leave our hands. Such a situation could arise. That is why the government has emphasized natural farming. Those who have experimented with natural farming have achieved great success. A single cow can provide sufficient manure for natural farming on 30 acres of land. No chemical inputs are required. Many such subjects must have emerged during your discussions today. Therefore, I appeal to all farmers that we should move towards chemical-free farming. I am not talking about organic farming; I am talking about natural farming, and every farmer can adopt it easily. We can take steps to save our soil by becoming free from chemicals. One challenge with our farmer brothers and sisters is that until they see something with their own eyes, they do not accept it. No matter how many long speeches are delivered, farmers do not simply accept something. They believe only after seeing it. Otherwise, all that stays in their minds is that they must apply the yellow powder, use the green bottle, use the green container, bring the red container, and if the neighbour uses two bags, they will use two bags too; if the neighbour uses four sacks, they will use four sacks too. This is the responsibility of all of us, and especially of progressive farmers, to free them from this mindset. We must help them move towards chemical-free natural farming. The first task is to save our Mother Earth. This mother is capable of providing everything we need. She will never let us feel any shortage, provided we protect her. We are the children of Mother Earth. It is our duty to fulfil this responsibility as her sons and daughters, as the children of Mother Earth. With this confidence, I extend my best wishes to all of you. I also congratulate the India Today Group. You have undertaken a task that will greatly help the nation in the years ahead. Thank you very much.
Greetings to My Brothers and Sisters of Bengaluru! Governor of Karnataka, Sudha Yadav Ji, Chief Minister D. K. Shivakumar Ji, Minister in the State Government, Krishna Byre Gowda Ji, and my colleague in the Lok Sabha, who have sent a brilliant young leader from Bengaluru, Tejasvi Surya Ji, other distinguished dignitaries present here, ladies and gentlemen. This is the first occasion on which I have had the opportunity to share the stage at a programme with our Governor, Sudha Yadav Ji. Perhaps the people of Karnataka are not yet fully familiar with her. But I would certainly like to speak about her life. What does service to the nation mean? What does dedication to the nation mean? We can learn this from the life of Sudha Ji and her family. When the Kargil war was being fought, her husband was martyred in the war. Even after that, in the memory of her husband, she has continued to uphold her resolve to serve Mother India and has been working with a spirit of service to this day. Having served in Parliament for many years, she guided the nation on several important issues. And today, from the land of Karnataka, she is contributing towards the happiness, peace and prosperity of the people of Karnataka. I extend my very best wishes to Sudha Ji. First of all, I respectfully pay my obeisance to Annamma Tai, the presiding deity of Bengaluru, and to Nada Prabhu Kempegowda Ji. This day is going to give a new impetus to the development journey of Bengaluru and Karnataka. Projects worth more than Rs. 15,000 crore, related to connectivity and energy security, are being launched from here today. From projects related to metro and railways to initiatives for energy security, all these projects will give new strength to Karnataka's infrastructure. Friends, Infrastructure plays a very important role in the development journey of any country. Better connectivity creates new opportunities for progress, employment and industries. And when these opportunities for development reach every section of society, the fight against poverty also becomes stronger. As a result, around 50 crore citizens of the country have come out of extreme poverty in recent years. The projects being launched today will also take this development journey forward. They will give a new impetus to the development of a Viksit Karnataka and, through a Viksit Karnataka, to the creation of a Viksit Bharat. They will also further strengthen Bengaluru's identity as a tech city. I extend my very best wishes to the people of Karnataka for all these projects. Friends, Just a short while ago, the new Pink Line of Bengaluru Metro was dedicated to the people of Bengaluru. With this new metro line, connectivity to IIM Bengaluru and several educational institutions will become even better. In addition, many areas that are known as major business, technology and commercial centres of Bengaluru will also be connected by the metro. This means that lakhs of people in Bengaluru will not only have greater convenience in commuting, but will also save both money and time. Friends, Yesterday was the death anniversary of the late Anant Kumar Ji. He had a special attachment to this region. Seeing this metro line today, his soul too must have felt delighted. I pay my heartfelt tributes to him. Friends, Bengaluru is India's Silicon Valley. It accounts for more than one-third of the country's IT exports. This city is a major engine of India's innovation and economic growth. Therefore, the rapid development of the entire Bengaluru region and the expansion of modern public transport are major priorities of the Central Government. With this vision, the Central Government is supporting the expansion of Namma Metro. In 2014, Namma Metro was only seventeen kilometres long. Today, its total network has grown to more than 100 kilometres. We are continuously scoring centuries. Shivakumar Ji, I was telling you: you may have heard a few days ago that India scored a century in Grandmasters Chess. India now has 100 chess grandmasters, and they have emerged during this very period. Friends, After Delhi-NCR and the Mumbai Metropolitan Region, Bengaluru has become the third city in the country to have a metro network of over 100 kilometres. This success of Namma Metro is not limited to just one project. It is the result of the country's new approach towards ease of living and ease of travel. It is the outcome of the country's commitment to improving the lives of middle-class families. Friends, Today, Indian Railways is leaving behind the old mindset of the era of slavery. The country's Railways is now creating a new heritage, a new legacy. Our railway stations, our railway engines and our railway coaches - everything now bears India's own imprint and identity. Vande Bharat trains and Amrit Bharat Stations are a glimpse of the railway services of Viksit Bharat. More than sixty railway stations in Karnataka, including Chamarajanagar, Chikkamagaluru, Sakleshpur and Subrahmanya Road stations, have also now become Amrit Bharat Stations. These stations reflect both sensitivity and modernity. Special facilities are available for every passenger, including senior citizens and persons with disabilities. An effort is being made to provide the same facilities at railway stations to poor and middle-class families that were earlier available at airports. Friends, Over the past 10-12 years, India's railway budget has increased several times over. During this period, as Tejasvi Ji has just mentioned, Karnataka's railway budget has also increased ninefold. Even today, Karnataka has received four Amrit Stations and two projects relating to doubling and electrification. A few days ago, Karnataka received its first Vande Bharat Sleeper train, and today Karnataka has received a Vande Bharat train connecting it with Goa. Through this train, people in Karnataka, including those in Mangaluru and along the entire coastal route, will get a new experience of travelling by a semi-high-speed train. Friends, Along with connectivity, energy security also plays a major role in the creation of Viksit Bharat. The ongoing war around Iran and the Gulf countries has shown how important energy security is. This has also been a challenging time for India. However, the steps taken over the past decade towards energy security have helped us face this crisis with strength. We have increased our capacity to produce petrol, diesel and LPG; developed arrangements for storing crude oil; expanded solar, hydro and wind energy; achieved almost 100 per cent electrification of the Railways; and strengthened public transport such as the metro. The three projects whose foundation stones have been laid or which have been inaugurated in Karnataka today will also give new strength to India's energy security. The LPG project in Mangaluru will increase the country's LPG storage capacity by 80,000 metric tonnes. The Devanagundi Marketing Terminal will make the supply of petrol, diesel and aviation fuel more organised and reliable. Meanwhile, the CBG plant in Bengaluru will produce around 13 tonnes of biogas every day. This will improve waste management and also save the money spent on importing natural gas. Friends, An important aspect of energy security is electricity generation. Today, India is moving forward with the goal of leadership in new technologies, from chips to ships, and from AI and data centres to quantum computing. Continuous efforts are also being made to ensure that the country has adequate electricity for this purpose. Today, India's renewable energy capacity has crossed 300 gigawatts. To achieve clean energy, India is also increasing the number of its nuclear power plants. Friends, Because of the schemes of the Central Government, today the citizens of India are becoming producers of electricity along with being consumers of electricity. Under the PM Surya Ghar Muft Bijli Yojana, more than 60 lakh families across the country have installed solar plants on their rooftops. These include more than 40,000 families in Karnataka as well. The Central Government has provided assistance of around Rs. 80,000 to each family. To every family. Similarly, under the PM-KUSUM scheme, arrangements are being made for irrigation through more than 8 lakh solar pumps for farmers in Karnataka. This means that the food providers of Karnataka are now also becoming energy providers. These schemes are reducing electricity expenses, while also creating new opportunities for income and employment. Friends, When a country moves forward with a mission, when every citizen becomes a part of development, and when the resources contributed by taxpayers are properly invested in development, the results are also encouraging and meaningful. In 2014, we began our work with the resolve of Sabka Saath, Sabka Vikas. Today, as a result of these very efforts, the middle class is expanding in the country, and a new Aspirational Class is emerging. This is a section of society whose aspirations are growing. It wants a refrigerator and a washing machine for its home. It wants a scooter or a motorcycle for its family. It wants to provide better education, nutrition and healthcare facilities to its children. It wants to spend on tourism and have new experiences. These growing aspirations are creating new demand in the country. From automobiles to electronics, and from tourism to the services sector, new industries, factories and employment opportunities are emerging. In other words, this neo-middle class is becoming a major engine of growth for India's economy. The interests of this neo-middle class, which is present in large numbers in Karnataka and Bengaluru as well, are among the priorities of the Central Government. Friends, The Central Government is continuously working to increase the savings of the middle class and put more money in their hands. We have exempted annual incomes of up to Rs. 12 lakh from income tax. By changing the TDS rules, we have provided relief to senior citizens and those earning rental income. Through the next-generation GST reforms, we have made everyday goods cheaper. By removing GST on personal health and life insurance, we have made insurance more affordable. Friends, Our effort is to increase the savings of the middle class, strengthen its purchasing power and enable it to fulfil its family's dreams while also contributing to the country's economic progress. The event being held in Karnataka today will also give a new impetus to this development journey. I extend my very best wishes to all of you for the new projects. Thank you very much to all of you!
Guru Pyari Saadh Sangat Ji, Waheguru Ji Ka Khalsa, Waheguru Ji Ki Fateh! Revered saints of the Sikh community, Lieutenant Governor of Delhi Taranjit Singh Sandhu ji, Chief Minister of Delhi Sister Rekha Gupta ji, my colleagues in Parliament, other ministers of the Delhi Government, all distinguished guests, ladies and gentlemen, In this sacred atmosphere, I respectfully bow to Guru Nanak Dev Ji. I extend lakh-lakh greetings to all the people of the country on the occasion of Prakash Parv. On this holy occasion of Guru Purab, I sincerely thank Taranjit Singh Sandhu ji for giving me the opportunity to participate in this Shabad Kirtan. Friends, Every word of Gurbani and every note of Kirtan is not merely music. It is a form of spiritual practice in itself. It is like diving into an ocean of knowledge. Therefore, whenever I receive an invitation to such an event, whenever I get an opportunity, I make it a point to be present in the Sangat and Kirtan. Friends, Guru Nanak Dev Ji used to say: Kirat Karo, Vand Chhako, Naam Japo. These teachings have been a natural part of my life. Bowing my head at Gurdwaras, immersing myself in Gurbani and experiencing spiritual bliss, finding joy in service, and then partaking in Langar, this has been a continuous part of my journey from childhood to my days as an organisational worker. Many times, I have had the good fortune of bowing my head at Sri Harmandir Sahib. Old colleagues know that I spent a considerable amount of time in Punjab as well, and that period remains one of the most special memories of my life. Brothers and sisters, By Guru's grace, I have continued to receive the company of Sikh saints. At the Prime Minister's residence too, I have had the privilege of hosting many Sikh saints and Gyanis. Even today, the presence of our saints at this gathering is creating a unique spiritual energy. On this occasion, I bow to all the saints and Gyanis. I extend my greetings to all Sikh brothers and sisters. Friends, This year's Prakash Parv is accompanied by another sacred remembrance. This year marks 350 years since the martyrdom of Sri Guru Tegh Bahadur Ji. It was here on the soil of Delhi that Guru Sahib sacrificed his life for the protection of the identity of this nation, for faith, belief, and values. Three hundred and fifty years have passed since that event, yet the inspiration of that sacrifice remains deeply embedded in the soil of India and in every particle of this nation. The people of this country will always remain grateful to him. Friends, The spirit of sacrificing everything for the nation and its culture has always been synonymous with the Sikh tradition. The martyrdom of Bhai Mati Das Ji, Bhai Sati Das Ji, and Bhai Dayala Ji reminds us of this. This spirit continued uninterrupted even during the struggle for Independence. As our Lieutenant Governor explained in detail, Sardar Teja Singh Samundri Ji himself is a shining example of this tradition. He played an important role in the Gurdwara Reform Movement. He dedicated his entire life to service. This year also marks one hundred years since his martyrdom. On this occasion, I respectfully pay tribute to him as well. Friends, India endured centuries of slavery. During the most difficult periods in our history, our Gurus kept the flame of protecting the nation burning. Today, when great national resolutions stand before us, when the goal of a Developed India lies ahead of us, this journey can be completed only through the inspiration of our Gurus and the strength of their ideals. Therefore, it is our responsibility to keep Sikh traditions and the heritage of Sikhi shining before society like a beacon of light. Friends, Over the past twelve years, we have sincerely tried to fulfil this responsibility. We celebrated the 350th Prakash Parv of Guru Gobind Singh Ji. We celebrated the 550th Prakash Parv of Guru Nanak Dev Ji. Numerous ceremonies were organised across India and abroad. Commemorative coins and postage stamps were issued. Special trains were operated. Programmes dedicated to Gurbani were organised throughout the country. Similarly, on the 400th Prakash Parv of Guru Tegh Bahadur Ji, a national event was held at the Red Fort. With the same spirit, we are currently commemorating the 350th martyrdom anniversary of Guru Tegh Bahadur Ji. Friends, One month from now, on 26 December, the country will observe another Veer Baal Diwas. We began observing this day nationally because it is our responsibility to take to the younger generation the immortal saga of the martyrdom of the Sahibzadas, the resolve of Mata Gujri, and that unparalleled courage and valour. When our children learn about the circumstances of those times, when they understand how the Sahibzadas and the Gurus made the supreme sacrifice and how our faith was protected through their sacrifices, that knowledge and inspiration will create youth dedicated to the nation. Our efforts to honour the Gurus are not limited to India. Just a few days ago, I was in the Philippines. There too, I visited the Khalsa Diwan Gurdwara, the oldest Gurdwara in the Philippines. The Government of India is also supporting the redevelopment of the Khalsa Diwan Gurdwara. Our effort is that the light of our Gurus should reach every person. With their blessings, India should continue to reach new heights. Friends, In our Ardas, we say: Nanak Naam Chardi Kala, Tere Bhaane Sarbat Da Bhala. This teaching of wishing well for all comes to us from Gurbani. And the thought of working for everyone's welfare arises only when we begin to regard everyone as our own. Friends, Today, conflicts are taking place in many parts of the world. Many innocent people and families are facing violence and unrest. In such times, the spirit of Sarbat Da Bhala, Sarbat Da Bhala, becomes even more important, so that there may be peace everywhere, every family may remain safe, and every individual may have the opportunity to live with dignity. Friends, The greatest test of the values given to us by Guru Sahib comes in our everyday lives. Today, one such challenge before our society is the problem of drugs. Because when a young person falls into the grip of addiction, the dreams of an entire family are shattered along with him. Friends, Every young person in the country should be free from addiction. They should fulfil their dreams, study well, progress in life, and bring honour to their family and the nation. In this effort, the role of families is extremely important. Our Sangat, our Gurdwaras, and our social institutions can together guide young people and give them the courage to choose the right path. I am confident that with Guru Sahib's blessings and the collective efforts of society, we will be able to provide a safe and bright future for our younger generation. With this sentiment, I once again extend lakh-lakh greetings to all of you on the sacred Prakash Parv of Sri Guru Nanak Dev Ji Maharaj. Waheguru Ji Ka Khalsa, Waheguru Ji Ki Fateh! Come, join me in raising the Jaikara - Bole So Nihal! Sat Sri Akal! Thank you very much!
Victory to Lord Shri Ram! Victory to Lord Shri Ram! Victory to Lord Shri Ram! On this sacred occasion of Vijayadashami, I bow at the feet of Lord Shri Ram, Mother Janaki, and Bajrang Bali. In this holy atmosphere, I extend my heartfelt greetings to all countrymen on Vijayadashami. Friends, Today, every corner of India is immersed in devotion to Lord Ram. Village after village, city after city, the nation is absorbed in the spirit of Ramleela. The staging of Ramleelas across the country, the tradition of keeping alive the ideals of Lord Shri Ram, the proclamation of the end of tyranny and terror, and the faith in the eternal victory of truth over falsehood, for us, this is not merely a festival. It is the life force of India. Therefore, today, when every citizen of the nation is proclaiming the victory of righteousness over unrighteousness, come, once again, repeat after me: Victory to Lord Shri Ram! Victory to Lord Shri Ram! Victory to Lord Shri Ram! Friends, This nation, its culture, and its ancestors have worshipped Lord Shri Ram for thousands of years. This faith exists not only because Shri Ram brought an end to the demon king Ravana. This faith also exists because Lord Ram gave us ideals that are unparalleled in the history of humanity. Friends, Lord Shri Ram faced countless difficulties in his life, yet he never abandoned the path of truth and righteousness. Such was his character: "Prasannatam ya na gata abhishekatah tatha, na mamle vanavasa duhkhatoh." This means that Lord Ram neither became elated upon receiving the kingdom nor became distressed by the sorrow of exile in the forest. Despite enduring every adversity, he upheld righteousness and established truth. When Lord Ram went into the forest, he embraced Nishadraj and honoured Mother Shabari. He established the highest standards of compassion, kindness, love, and harmony. And when Lord Ram returned and became the king of Ayodhya, he established such a Ram Rajya that even today it is considered the benchmark of good governance. Ram Rajya means a system where there is no discrimination against anyone, where everyone receives justice, where everyone gets equal opportunities, where the purpose of power is not to rule but to serve the welfare of the people. That is why Goswami Tulsidas ji wrote: "Daihik daivik bhautik tapa, Ram raj nahin kahuhi vyapa." This is the ideal of Maryada Purushottam Lord Shri Ram. Therefore, we must always regard the people as divine and move forward with the mantra of Citizen First. We must place service to the poor and the deprived above everything else. Friends, We receive a very great lesson from the life of Lord Ram. No matter how great the crisis may be, no matter how difficult the challenge, we must not sit waiting for others. We must recognise our own strength. We must trust our own resources. We must turn our resolve into power. And we must find solutions through our own hard work. Lord Ram taught us that difficulties on the path cannot stop our journey. The ocean stood before him. Lanka was far away. But the resolve was unwavering. Therefore, Lord Ram awakened the collective strength of the people. He made public participation his support. He united the available resources. And he made a goal that seemed impossible. This is the fundamental mantra of success. Faith in our own strength. Confidence in our own capability. Proper utilisation of our own resources. And unwavering commitment to our goal. Today, we too must move forward with this same mantra. Before us stands the goal of a Developed India. Before us stands the goal of a Self-Reliant India. Through the power of the people and for the welfare of the people, we must fulfil every aspiration of our citizens. And for this too, we must draw inspiration from Lord Ram. Friends, A nation progresses only when it possesses self-respect. And heritage plays a very important role in this self-respect. Therefore, today the country is committed to preserving its heritage and identity. For centuries, our treasures were looted and taken abroad. But today, we are bringing those treasures back. In the past twelve years, more than six hundred and fifty ancient idols have been brought back to India. Similarly, India is giving new global recognition to its cultural centres. In recent years, many of our sites have been added to the UNESCO World Heritage List. Last year, even Diwali was included by UNESCO in the list of Intangible Cultural Heritage of Humanity. This is the result of the collective willpower of millions of Indians. We must continue to work with the same dedication. Friends, Vijayadashami is the festival of victory. It is the festival of triumph. This festival gives us the courage to believe that no matter how powerful the enemy may be, when we stand with truth and humanity, victory ultimately belongs to us. This spirit of Vijayadashami has always been India's guiding philosophy. Our sages inspired us: "Samano mantrah samitih samani, samanam manah saha chittamesham." This means that our thoughts should be united, our councils should be united, and all institutions of the nation, all political parties, should stand together in the national interest. We must move forward with this spirit. We must unite against the enemies of the nation. Those forces that attack the honour and dignity of the country, those who divide society, and those who harbour hatred towards our culture, are enemies of the nation. On this auspicious occasion of Vijayadashami, we must once again renew our resolve. We must identify the enemies of Mother India. We must defeat them. And, taking the pledge of Nation First, everyone must come together. I am confident that the vision we expressed during the Pran Pratishtha ceremony of the Ayodhya Temple, "From Ram to Nation" and "From Divinity to National Duty," will certainly be fulfilled. I once again extend my heartfelt greetings to all countrymen on Dussehra. Jai Siya Ram! Jai Siya Ram! Jai Siya Ram! Bharat Mata Ki Jai! Bharat Mata Ki Jai! Thank you very much.

There was no permanent building. Resources were limited. But there was one thing: firm determination and an unwavering spirit of service.
