Friends, From temple to metro, these experiences of Tamil Nadu reflect the spirit of today's India. India draws inspiration from its cultural strength, advances through scientific progress, and creates new opportunities through modern infrastructure. Our heritage and our aspirations for the future are moving forward together. Friends, A few days ago, with your blessings and the love of the people of the country, I crossed an important milestone of twenty-five years as Head of the Government. In October 2001, I took oath as Chief Minister. Since then, the people of the country have given me the opportunity to serve first as Chief Minister and then as Prime Minister. During these twenty-five years, my faith in two strengths of democracy has continuously grown stronger. First - the people of the country. Second - cooperative federalism. I have always followed the mantra that the development of the nation comes through the development of the states. Friends, As Chief Minister, I personally understood the expectations of states. Therefore, after becoming Prime Minister, I not only tried to fulfil those aspirations, but also made every effort to ensure that every state could achieve its own aspirational goals. Friends, As Chief Minister, I had the opportunity to interact many times with Karunanidhi ji and Amma Jayalalithaa ji. And after becoming Prime Minister, I had the opportunity to work with Jayalalithaa ji, O. Panneerselvam ji, E. Palaniswami ji, and M. K. Stalin ji. Governments changed, but my commitment to the development of Tamil Nadu and service to its people always remained unwavering. And today, with that same commitment, we are moving this journey of Tamil Nadu's development forward together with Chief Minister Joseph Vijay ji. Friends, Today, urban development is one of our major priorities in Tamil Nadu. I have always believed that urbanisation is not a challenge, but an opportunity. And public transport plays a very important role in this. When transportation is good, it improves people's ease of living. We all know that merely widening roads is not enough to create better transportation. In modern cities, we need holistic infrastructure. And therefore, over the past decade, we have undertaken a massive expansion of metro networks across the country. In 2014, metro services operated in only five cities of the country. Today, metro services operate in twenty-five cities across the nation. In 2014, India's metro network was about 250 kilometres. Two hundred and fifty kilometres. Today, this network has crossed 1,100 kilometres. I am pleased that with this expansion of Chennai Metro, this network is growing even further. This work has been carried out so that ordinary citizens living in Chennai, working families, students, and senior citizens can all travel with greater ease and complete their journeys in less time. Friends, We have to strengthen India by empowering our states. Therefore, the central government is promoting manufacturing in states like Tamil Nadu. Every Indian takes pride in Tamil Nadu's manufacturing sector, its small industries, and every individual working in them. Today, initiatives of the central government, whether Make in India or Vocal for Local, are also benefiting Tamil Nadu. Friends, Today, from components to technology and finished products, the central government is strengthening the entire product chain in every state. As a result of these efforts, today we are the world's second-largest mobile manufacturer. Since 2014, our electronics exports have increased elevenfold. Our defence exports have increased fiftyfold. And our engineering exports have also set new records. Friends, Because of this growth in manufacturing, millions of direct and indirect jobs have been created for the youth of the country. And in this manufacturing growth of India, the people of Tamil Nadu have made a highly commendable contribution. Today, we are laying the foundation stone for an advanced lubricant base oil project. This will create new employment opportunities here and will also greatly help in reducing the country's imports. To build a strong manufacturing sector, we need good roads. We need railways and ports for the transportation of raw materials. And when such infrastructure is built, it creates a large number of employment opportunities at different levels. When construction takes place, engineers get work, technicians get work, workers get work, everyone gets employment. Employment opportunities are also created in factories connected with cement, steel, and equipment, as well as in smaller companies. And when a project is completed, improved connectivity helps industries and services move forward. That is why the rail and road projects being inaugurated and launched today are going to be extremely important for the progress of Tamil Nadu. Today, port infrastructure is also being strengthened across the country so that the shipping sector can reach new heights. Tamil Nadu has a very important role in this as well. In this direction, the central government has recently taken a significant decision. With an investment of about seventeen thousand crore rupees, the VOC Port at Thoothukudi is being developed as a world-class mega container and green energy hub. Once this project is completed, even the world's ultra-large container vessels will be able to dock here easily. This will help transform Tamil Nadu into a global hub for industry and transportation. Over the past few years, both the speed and efficiency of Indian ports have improved. Because of increased speed, the turnaround time of ships at ports has been reduced by half. As a result, Indian products are now reaching global markets faster than before. In our vision for the ocean economy, our fishermen play a very important role. Therefore, through the PM Matsya Sampada Yojana and several other schemes, projects worth thousands of crores of rupees have been approved for Tamil Nadu. Fishing harbours are being upgraded here. Fishing landing centres are being developed. Focus is being placed on cold chain facilities. Assistance is also being provided for purchasing transport vehicles to keep fish fresh. Friends, Whether it is modernising the Chennai Fishing Harbour for our fisherman brothers or developing the Seaweed Park in Tamil Nadu, the central government is supporting these efforts as well. Through the Kisan Credit Card scheme, fishermen are now also able to access working capital more easily. Today, fish production in the country has grown rapidly. Last year, our seafood exports reached their highest levels ever, both in quantity and value terms. This has improved the lives of our fishing families and people living in coastal communities. Friends, We want to build a Tamil Nadu that keeps reaching new heights. And for this, whatever support is needed from the Centre, we stand with you with full commitment. Let us together realise the resolve of a developed India through a developed Tamil Nadu. With this spirit, once again, I extend my heartfelt congratulations and best wishes to all of you for these projects. Nandri, Vanakkam!!! (Thank You, Namastey).
My dear countrymen, Namaskar! I welcome you all once again to this new episode of 'Mann Ki Baat'. All of you know that this is a platform where we bring to the fore new initiatives and discuss the innovative ideas of our fellow countrymen. Many of these ideas are the result of people investing their boundless energy into them. Friends, Some time ago, someone wrote on social media, "On the auspicious occasion of Ashadhi Ekadashi, I am delighted to announce the launch of my initiative to make history interesting." They further explained that the idea is quite simple: visit the website and select any year. There you can easily find out which ruler ruled over what part of India at that time. I found this quite interesting too and thought of learning more about it. I discovered that this work has been done by Shriman Krishna Seshadri, a resident of Mumbai. Interestingly, Krishna ji has kept the website's name very simple: The effort he has put in should reach all of you; that is why I thought, why not speak directly to Shri Krishna ji! Friends, Krishna ji is joining us on the phone. Prime Minister: Namaste, Krishna ji. Krishna Seshadri: Namaste, Sir, Namaste. Prime Minister: My friend, I've learned that you neither belong to the tech world nor are you a history teacher; yet, you utilized technology to create a website dedicated to history. I am very curious - how did this all come about? Krishna Seshadri: Thank you, Sir; it is a great privilege to speak with you. To tell you a little about myself: I was an engineer, then I pursued an MBA and entered the field of marketing. However, throughout all this, I maintained a deep interest in history - something I've had since childhood. So, I always wanted to do something on history - how to make it interesting and I felt there was immense scope for improvement and change in how we teach history... we could present history to reach people as a story. Prime Minister: Krishna-ji, what aspect of Indian history did you feel needed to be known by the maximum number of people? Krishna Seshadri: Sir, the way Indian history is taught in our schools and colleges often focuses on the perspective of a single empire or kingdom, with those specific empires receiving disproportionate attention. Consequently, we remain unaware of many other great empires - such as the Cholas, the Pallavas, the Karkota dynasty of Kashmir, or the Ahoms, who preserved their culture and value system in Assam for at least 600 years. So, I decided to create a site that introduces people to these great empires. Prime Minister: Krishna-ji, you work so hard; let me ask you a hypothetical question. If you had the opportunity to converse with any three figures from Indian history, who would you choose, and why? Krishna Seshadri: Yes, Sir. First, I would speak to Rajaraja Chola or Rajendra Chola - who was one of the most powerful and significant kings of the Chola Empire Sir. So, I would want to understand the vision behind the establishment of massive Shiva temples like the Brihadisvara Temple or Gangaikonda Cholapuram. Where did that vision and planning come from? How did the thought occur to them to create something that would inspire people for the next one or two thousand years? So, that is what I would like to know from them. And, the second personality - who, in my opinion, is a highly underrated personality in Indian history - Sir, his name is Hemu. We usually read only two or three lines about him in Indian history. Krishna Seshadri: We often don't know the fact that Hemu was a common man - an ordinary individual and a merchant - who went on to build an empire; he was not just an empire-builder but also a military thinker and a military genius who fought 23 battles in his career and won 22 of them. I am very interested in understanding his thought process and the journey that took him from being an ordinary man to such a great king, Sir. The third figure is a very interesting personality - a woman named Tribhuvan Mahadevi. Her story belongs to the Bhaumakara dynasty of Odisha. Around 850 AD, the neighbouring Pala Empire invaded her kingdom, and her husband - the king - was martyred in the battle. Consequently, the empire shrank, and chieftains and leaders began abandoning it; however, Tribhuvan Mahadevi took charge of the administration. She became one of the first independent rulers in Indian history. She not only stabilized the government but also made it powerful enough to rule over Kalinga and the surrounding region for the next hundred years. So, these are the three interesting historical personalities I would like to meet, Sir. Prime Minister: Krishna-ji, it seems like you are re-living history now; you've been completely immersed in it. See, I have also viewed your site with great interest, and what I found most fascinating was the tab allowing people to submit corrections and suggestions. Being open to such constructive feedback is a significant thing in itself. Have you started receiving suggestions from people? And how do you act upon them? Krishna Seshadri: Yes, Sir, we receive many suggestions from people. Suggestions generally fall into two categories. One type involves suggesting corrections - such as pointing out which king ruled a specific region or nation at a particular time, or clarifying when a specific battle took place. We receive other suggestions - such as adding specific features to the website - and we consider them all. For instance, if someone suggests that a particular king ruled a specific region during a certain era, we take that suggestion and cross-reference it with our sources. Someone suggested to us that while we had showcased India as a whole, it would be even better to display information similarly at the regional level; so, we implemented that feature. And then another suggestion led us to create a 'trump card' style feature - compiling data and statistics for various empires - to make the content more engaging for children. Someone also suggested consolidating these resources so they could be easily downloaded and shared, a feature we are currently implementing. It's important for us to know that we are bound to make mistakes - we aren't the best. That is precisely why we consciously included a 'correction' tab; we want this to be a collaborative platform where contributions help us build a site that makes history an enjoyable subject for years to come. Prime Minister: Krishna-ji, you have truly accomplished something remarkable. Okay, apart from history, you must be having a lot of other hobbies... or do you engage in other activities as well! Krishna Seshadri: Well, actually, Sir, I'm a bit of a boring person. Prime Minister: Krishna Seshadri: I'm deeply interested in technology and history, and now that the two have come together, I spend my entire day immersed in that. Prime Minister: Well, Krishna ji, I extend my very best wishes to you. You have made a good start, and your approach is such that people will connect with it - I am fully confident of that. My best wishes are with you. Friends, This initiative by Krishna ji will help not only history students but all of us to learn about our past in an engaging way. My dear countrymen, From morning till evening, lakhs of women across the streets of our cities and towns are engaged in their work. My dear countrymen, Namaskar. I am getting an opportunity to meet you once again for the September edition of 'Mann Ki Baat'. I welcome all the people of the country. In 'Mann Ki Baat', we provide a platform for the positive efforts of our countrymen... discuss the nation's achievements. One such major, historic achievement of India is set to mark its 10th anniversary in just a day - this achievement is the 'Surgical Strike'. Ten years ago, on September 28-29, 2016, the Indian Army carried out surgical strikes on terrorist hideouts in Pakistan. The world was astonished to see the manner in which our brave soldiers crossed the Line of Control (LoC) to take action against terrorism. The surgical strike boldly demonstrated to the entire world a new example of India's counter-terrorism policy. The 'Surgical Strike' was our decisive response to the long-standing pain India had endured due to terrorist attacks. Over the past decade - from the Balakot airstrike to 'Operation Sindoor' - India has repeatedly sent a clear message: if terrorists dare to cast an evil eye on India, they will be met with a befitting response. Friends, India has long suffered wounds inflicted by terrorist attacks. There was a time when serial blasts had become a common occurrence in the country. It was in this very month of September - specifically on - that many innocent lives were lost in the serial blasts in Delhi. Just a few weeks later, the massive 26/11 attack took place in Mumbai. Friends, in the past too, there used to be demands for action against Pakistan following every terror attack, but such calls were often not given due attention; time to pass by was simply awaited. Today, India pursues a zero-tolerance policy against terrorism, and we are witnessing the results of that. Friends, This year also marks 25 years since the 9/11 terrorist attacks. Among the innocent lives lost at the World Trade Center in the US, the highest number of victims - after Americans - was Indians. I recall that after becoming Prime Minister in 2014, when I visited the US, I had reached New York to pay my homage to those innocent citizens. Today, once again, I extend my condolences to the bereaved families on behalf of crores of Indian citizens. My dear countrymen, A grand picture of the nation is currently emerging. This picture includes you, your family and even your neighbours. I am referring to India's census. The first phase of this exercise is nearly complete across all states and union territories; data of over 32 crore families has already been recorded. Work is currently underway also in the remote, snow-clad regions of Ladakh, Himachal Pradesh, Jammu & Kashmir, and Uttarakhand. Many inspiring experiences have emerged from these areas. In Uttarakhand, the census process began in the village of Mana in Chamoli; Mana is known as the country's first village. The process there started with 83-year-old Purnima Rawat ji. In Bagori village, Uttarkashi, Hardev Singh Rana ji - who runs a homestay - entered his details himself using digital means. Friends, The census is a national responsibility for all of us. On 15th August also, I had urged my countrymen to take time out for that. The facility to self-register your census details is very convenient. You can register information about yourself and your family from your mobile itself. Along with Hindi, this facility is available in 16 languages. Friends, To do this, you need to visit the census online portal. The portal is se.census.gov.in... log in using your mobile number, enter your location, fill in the details regarding your family and submit the information. The entire process takes about 15 to 20 minutes. And for that you do not need to upload any document. After submitting the information, you will receive a special ID. You can share this ID with the census enumerator when they visit your home. After the information you provided is verified, it will become part of the census record. Friends, So far, more than 2 crore 60 lakh people have already entered their details on the census portal via mobile - completing the self-enumeration. It will take a little of your time, but it is important for the nation, as accurate information about you and your family helps create a precise picture of the country's future. My dear countrymen, Today - the date 27th of September - has become even more special for a special reason. My dear countrymen, We began this year with a grand celebration of national pride - This festival was the "Somnath Swabhiman Parv". A thousand years ago, in 1026, Mahmud of Ghazni had demolished the Somnath Temple for the first time. However, those who came to destroy Somnath and erase India's identity have no existence at all today, whereas the Somnath Temple stands in all its grandeur by the vast sea, bestowing the blessing of immortality upon India. That is why we celebrated the 'Somnath Swabhiman Parv' this year with immense pride. Friends, Why I am bringing this up again in 'Mann Ki Baat' today - there is a special reason for that. In 1026 AD when Somnath was destroyed by Ghazni; the same year, the famous Sun Temple in Modhera, Gujarat, was also attacked and destroyed. This means that a thousand years have passed since the destruction of the Modhera temple as well, whereas the temple then had been built just a year ago. King Bhimdev had got it built in 1025. Friends, Imagine that era; a massive invasion had taken place, a major center of faith like the Somnath Temple had been vandalised, and attacks were occurring at multiple locations - any society or administration could have had its morale shattered. Yet, King Bhimdev The First not only confronted the forces of Ghazni but also had the Modhera Sun Temple rebuilt within a short time. It is because of this very resilience that the Somnath Temple, too, was rebuilt every time despite facing repeated destruction. Friends, The attacks on the Modhera Sun Temple did not cease in later periods either. Alauddin Khilji later destroyed this temple, the magnificent architectural marvel once again. However, friends, we, the people of India, do not worship the Sun solely through architecture and temples; for us, Sun worship also signifies light, energy, science, and the inspiration for continuity. That is why Modhera is known today not just for its Sun Temple; this village in Gujarat has become the country's first 'Surya Gram' 'Solar Village.' Modhera now runs entirely on solar energy, setting an example of sustainability for the nation. Several attractions and amenities for tourists have also been introduced in Modhera. The Modhera Dance Festival is held there in January, following Makar Sankranti, featuring magnificent performances of India's cultural and classical dances. I would urge all of you to visit Modhera at least once and witness this glorious Indian heritage. Friends, Our connection with the past remains very much alive today. This is driven by the dedication and hard work of individuals who are deepening this bond. A unique initiative of this kind is underway in Manapparai, Tiruchirappalli, Tamil Nadu. A. Selvaraju ji, associated with the Department of Tamil Studies at a college, is spearheading this effort. During a lecture, he learned about some ancient sculptures from a student. Following a discussion on the subject, he planned a field visit. During this visit, he discovered several sculptures and ancient temples at various locations. This is a fascinating way to connect with the past; this way, we can understand our heritage even better. Similar initiatives can be undertaken in other parts of the country as well... they offer us the opportunity to observe and comprehend our history more closely. My dear countrymen, When we protect the environment, we do not merely save plants or animals; we strengthen that entire ecosystem. There was a time when elephants used to inhabit this area, but as human interference on forest land increased, the elephants moved away. Three years ago, this land was cleared of encroachment, and the results have been remarkable - the elephants have returned here again. Herds of elephants are once again being spotted in areas where they had not been active for years. Friends, another wonderful initiative has taken place in Assam itself - this effort is to save a tiny creature, the Pygmy Hog. Once, its very existence was under threat; at that time, a conservation program was launched with just six Pygmy Hogs, and they were reintroduced into the wild. Grasslands were also curated for their living. Today, thanks to this program, the Pygmy Hog population has risen to over 190. Friends, Our relationship with nature is not limited merely to its preservation; often, when we care for nature, it becomes a source of sustenance for our own lives. In the Sundarbans of West Bengal, some women are setting an inspiring example of this very bond. These women from Jharkhali are creating mangrove forests. Most of them are women who lost their husbands to tiger attacks. After losing their husbands, they found a means of livelihood within the Sundarbans itself. The mangroves they have planted are now serving as a natural protective shield for their villages, while also providing them with an income. Friends, let us conserve the environment, respect the balance of nature, and safeguard its diversity. Every such effort of ours is a responsibility we owe to future generations. My dear countrymen, Millions of young people across the country have recently taken a significant resolve against substance abuse and drugs. They have vowed to stay away from drugs themselves and have also taken up the responsibility of spreading this message to others - all under the 'Nasha Mukt Yuva Abhiyan'. This campaign will run for 100 weeks... it includes volunteers from 'MY Bharat'. Our youth associated with the NSS are part of this; schools, colleges, and universities have joined in; and numerous social and spiritual organizations are also involved. In just the last few days, thousands of pledge ceremonies and awareness programs have taken place across the country. Friends, there is one aspect of this campaign that I particularly appreciate: the 'Nasha-Mukti Ka Sandesh.' Our youth are propagating this message in their own language and in their own way. I had urged young people in 'Mann Ki Baat' as well, to join this campaign using their creativity, and I am delighted that thousands of youth have stepped forward in response to this call. Various competitions - such as anthem - slogan writing, and street plays - have been organized on the 'MY Bharat' platform. Similarly, young people are sharing creative content on social media using the hashtag '#NashaMuktYuva'; in essence, the message, the medium, and the driving energy behind it all come from the youth themselves. In the coming days, activities such as health check-ups and meditation camps will also be conducted as part of this campaign. Friends, Several of our festivals are approaching soon. This time amidst the festive celebrations, let us also look around us. If any young person is drifting towards a harmful habit, talk to them. If someone is trying to break free from addiction, support them. Let us all work together to advance the resolve of a drug-free youth and a Viksit Bharat. My dear countrymen, I announced a talent hunt campaign for sports from the Red Fort this time. To ensure India continues to perform excellently, it is essential to reach out to villages and towns to discover sporting talent. At the same time, there are many individuals who have dedicated themselves to this very cause. Their sole passion is to discover talent and shape it. In our Punjab, Rajinder Singh ji is doing exactly this work. Rajinder Singh ji is one such individual who also carries the title 'Senior' as part of his name. He was part of the team that won the gold medal in hockey at the 1980 Olympics. He shone at the 1982 World Cup. In 2001, India won the Men's Junior World Cup title for the first time, whereas in 2003, the country clinched the Senior Asia Cup title. He played the role of the coach in both these tournaments. Now, he is pursuing his passion through the Round Glass Hockey Academy. Thanks to his efforts, new hockey astro-turfs are being laid in villages. Rajinder ji has a very clear focus: identifying children in villages that lack access to excellent coaching facilities. Through such initiatives, many players are securing spots at India's junior and senior levels. No matter how much Rajinder Singh ji's dedication is lauded, it's not enough. My dear countrymen, *Amla*, the Indian gooseberry is very familiar to all of us. Pickles and preserves i.e. *murabba* made from it are used in most households. Many people like *Amla* juice and *Chyawanprash* becomes an essential part of our daily routine during winter. Our elders have long spoken about the qualities of *Amla* across generations. Friends, this very *Amla* is now becoming a source of new possibilities in Pratapgarh, Uttar Pradesh. The bond between Pratapgarh and *Amla* is very old; farmers here have been growing it for generations. *Amla* has given Pratapgarh a strong identity, and now, this long-standing identity is bringing new opportunities for local farmers and women. The 'One District One Product' (ODOP) has boosted local entrepreneurs involved in the *Amla* trade there. Today, around 100 units related to *Amla* processing are operational in Pratapgarh, with women connected with self-help groups playing a significant role. One such group is from Devgarh Kamasi village; women associated with the Savitri Phule Self-Help Group are producing *Amla* candy, preserves, and juice. Access to new markets through the ODOP scheme has also led to an increase in their income. Similarly, there is a 'Vindhyavasini Self-Help Group' in a village. The women of this group are producing many such items like Amla pickle and digestive powder. These women received machinery for processing and access to loan facilities. They also got the opportunity to participate in local trade fairs, and in no time, their income saw a significant rise. Friends, Every region of our country certainly possesses some such unique specialty - be it a specific fruit, a traditional art form, or a unique skill. The 'One District, One Product' campaign aims to promote these very specialties. Our endeavour should be to ensure that the identity of our villages and small towns is not confined merely to local markets; these products should reach other parts of the country and even global markets. This also strengthens the 'Vocal for Local' campaign... it inspires the nation to become self-reliant. Even during the upcoming festive season, we must never forget the 'Vocal for Local' mantra. My dear countrymen, The month of September is dedicated to the fight against malnutrition. During the Nutrition Month, individuals and organizations from various sectors come together for this noble cause. Several commendable initiatives have been launched this year to combat malnutrition. Chhattisgarh, for instance, has started the 'Har Ghar Munga - Ghar Ghar Munga' campaign... through this, people are being encouraged to include the nutritious *Munga* i.e. Moringa in their diet. Gujarat has also launched a 'Vangi Spardha' - a competition featuring dishes made from millets. This initiative promotes affordable and nutritious local cuisine. So far, more than two lakh people have participated in this contest. Maharashtra is also making commendable efforts in this direction; teachers, Anganwadi workers, and parents are joining awareness campaigns there. In Mizoram as well, people have come together this year to launch a campaign for the upkeep of 'Poshan Vatikas'. This aligns with the Mizo tradition of 'Talo-Mad-Yana,' which signifies selfless service and always being at the forefront of helping others. Such efforts are truly praiseworthy. I am also proud of everyone associated with Anganwadi centres who has been part of such initiatives. These Anganwadi centres are now digitally connected via the 'Poshan Tracker,' with approximately nine crore beneficiaries registered on the platform. This has enabled better care for children, pregnant women and breastfeeding mothers. We must continue this fight against malnutrition with the same spirit; we have to make our society completely free from malnutrition. Friends, All of us have celebrated Ganesha Utsav this month with great devotion and enthusiasm. Ganpati Bappa was welcomed across the country with deep reverence. This sacred occasion fills the entire atmosphere with joy and exuberance. Friends, Vighnharta Bhagwan Shri Ganesha symbolizes knowledge, wisdom, and the auspicious beginning of any endeavour. Deep reverence for Him exists not only in India; take the example of Shriman Panthoun Thirkhanon ji - he hails from Thailand. There, Lord Shri Ganesha is worshipped as 'Phra Phikanet.' When he was 19 years old, his father gifted him a photograph of Bhagwan Shri Ganesha. It was then that a profound sense of devotion awakened within him. He has spent over four decades studying traditions associated with Bhagwan Shri Ganesha. He has travelled across several Asian countries, including India and Nepal. He has held a special affection for Maharashtra. After learning about the *Ashtavinayak* tradition, he has visited the region numerous times. Through his efforts, the *Ashtavinayak* yatra has gained popularity among devotees in Thailand as well. In 2002, he established the Ganesha Himal Museum in Chiang Mai, northern Thailand. Today, it houses more than 10,000 idols of Ganesha. The museum also has *Ashtavinayak* idols for devotees who are unable to travel to India. Worship rituals for Ganesha ji are conducted here every Sunday morning around 10 o'clock. Also, on special occasions connected with Ganesha, celebrations are held. This demonstrates how India's faith, art, and cultural traditions are gaining popularity in other countries as well. I wish that the blessings of Ganpati Bappa remain with everyone always. Friends, After every episode of 'Mann Ki Baat,' I receive messages from you on myriad topics. Do continue to share such inspiring stories. When you appreciate the initiatives taking place around you, the enthusiasm of those involved grows even further. Learning about these efforts through 'Mann Ki Baat' inspires people in other parts of the country as well. May this journey continue to move forward with your support. Celebrate the upcoming festivals with great joy and enthusiasm with your family and friends. I extend my best wishes to all of you once again. Thank you very much. Namaskar.
My Greetings to Everyone! Hon'ble Governor of Karnataka, Sudha Yadav Ji, Sarkaryavah of the Rashtriya Swayamsevak Sangh and our guide, Shri Dattatreya Hosabale Ji, President of S-VYASA, H. R. Nagendra Ji, Vice-President, Dr. R. Nagarathna Ji, Chancellor, H. R. Dayananda Swami Ji, Vice-Chancellor, Manjunath N. K. Ji, Member of Parliament, C. N. Manjunath Ji, Mayor of Miryang City, South Korea, other distinguished dignitaries present here, ladies and gentlemen! Today, here in Bengaluru, I have the opportunity to experience the extraordinary energy of yoga. This land of Karnataka has been a centre of yoga for centuries. Many great yoga teachers and scholars were born here and have also enriched the tradition of yoga. We all know that the legendary Kannada cinema actor, Dr. Rajkumar Ji, was also among those who practised yoga and contributed to the world of art as well as to the field of yoga. Friends, Amidst this great legacy of Karnataka, the 50-year journey of Prashanti Kutiram is an extraordinary and unprecedented saga of service and dedication, which every Indian should know about. How an ordinary yoga centre transformed into such a large movement is, in itself, a source of inspiration. Friends, On this occasion, several new facilities have also been launched. I extend my best wishes to the S-VYASA family for these initiatives as well. I also congratulate all of you and the millions of practitioners associated with Prashanti Kutiram on its Golden Jubilee. Friends, Along with the Golden Jubilee of Prashanti Kutiram, we are also celebrating the birth centenary of the revered H. V. Seshadri Ji this year. I pay my respectful tributes to him. Seshadri Ji devoted his entire life to advancing the mission of 'Nation Building through Individual Development'. He understood that if the country was to be taken to the pinnacle of success, it required dedicated, capable and disciplined citizens. Seshadri Ji devoted himself wholeheartedly to preparing such individuals. The goals he pursued while serving in the Rashtriya Swayamsevak Sangh continue to inspire crores of Swayamsevaks even today. Friends, Whenever we remember him, the first image that comes to mind is his simple personality. Dattaji has just described his simplicity in such wonderful words. Such a towering personality, such vast experience, and yet such humility in his conduct! To remain in the background while encouraging others to move forward, to bring people together and prepare them for the nation - this was the great discipline of his life. During conversations, he would often cite an example. He used to say that we should learn from the conduct of a mother. Just as a mother stays awake throughout the night to serve her child, nurtures the child and works selflessly for the child, if we extend this very sentiment to society, imagine how much society would benefit. Friends, With the same selflessness as a mother, Seshadri Ji remained devoted to serving society. And I have been fortunate to receive his affection and guidance continuously. The memories of the time spent with him and his words remain invaluable to me even today. Friends, There is another thing that Seshadri Ji used to say which I can never forget. He would say that if we give to society with both our hands, society gives back to us with millions of hands. I have personally witnessed this principle bearing fruit time and again in my own life. While serving the people, the Janata Janardan, the people have always showered their blessings upon me, and this is one of the greatest assets of my life. Friends, We also see the same spirit of service in Seshadri Ji's family. Dr. Nagendra Ji and Dr. Nagarathna Ji are carrying forward his mission through their work. I appreciate all their efforts and extend my congratulations to them as well. Friends, When the country was shackled by the chains of colonial rule, millions of freedom fighters were fighting for independence on one hand, while on the other, great sages of the nation like Swami Vivekananda were awakening India's spiritual consciousness and showing the path of yoga to the youth of India. Friends, The inspiration drawn from sages like Swami Vivekananda gave rise to institutions such as Prashanti Kutiram. Here, an initiative was undertaken not only to connect yoga with the masses, but also to conduct new research by putting it to the test of modern science. This institution has not merely produced yoga practitioners; it has also prepared trainers and philosophers for society. These teachers, doctors and researchers are serving people across the country and the world through yoga. S-VYASA, which evolved from Prashanti Kutiram, has become an important institution in the fields of yoga education, scientific research and healthcare services. Along with yoga, you have also developed a holistic wellness ecosystem comprising facilities related to Ayurveda, naturopathy and modern medical science. The research work undertaken in areas such as stress-related challenges and lifestyle diseases has served the entire world. I commend Prashanti Kutiram for these efforts. Friends, Over the past 12 years, India has been presenting its culture, identity and knowledge systems to the world with complete confidence, and I say this with a sense of responsibility, with complete confidence. One outcome of this is that, at India's initiative, the entire world now celebrates International Yoga Day. On the occasion of Yoga Day, programmes related to yoga are organised across the world, from Australia to Africa and America. Crores of people participate in them. Today, in many countries across the world, yoga has reached our schools, institutions and workplaces. For instance, in Saudi Arabia, yoga has been recognised as a sporting activity. The Sports Ministry there has entered into an agreement with India's Ministry of AYUSH to promote yoga. We are continuously witnessing examples of foreign nationals promoting yoga. And I have personally experienced this. Wherever I travel across the world, during lunch or dinner, when I sit with foreign guests or Heads of State, there is hardly any lunch or dinner where yoga does not come up in the conversation. Earlier, I had to explain to them that I am not a yoga expert, but a practitioner. I then tell them about its various benefits, and they ask numerous questions. I have met many world leaders who tell me that their wives visit India once a year to learn yoga and teach yoga there. Some tell me that their daughters are learning yoga, while others say that their daughters have dedicated themselves to yoga. In the families of some of the world's most prominent Heads of State, there is someone or the other associated with yoga. This shows how far India has reached through yoga. It seems that yoga has become a gateway to understanding India. I have also seen foreign nationals, whether from the Royal Family of Qatar or from elsewhere, who are completely dedicated to yoga. India has also honoured many such individuals with the Padma Shri, thereby enhancing their prestige. Friends, As people across the world become more familiar with yoga, their curiosity to know India also grows. A new attraction develops towards our culture and traditional knowledge systems. This opens up a new avenue of engagement between India and the world. And all this has been made possible through our collective efforts. If I talk about Ayurveda, more than 150 Ayurveda training courses have been recognised in different countries across the world. Memorandums of Understanding (MoUs) have been signed with 28 countries to facilitate cooperation in the expansion of Ayurveda. Friends, Our endeavour is to ensure that the benefits of Ayurveda and yoga reach every citizen of the country. Today, holistic healthcare has emerged as a new global movement. As a result, there is a natural attraction towards Ayurveda and naturopathy. And friends, When there is such excellence and enthusiasm all around, alongside promoting and propagating yoga, we have also made several changes to rules as per the requirements. In India, Yogasana has also been recognised as a competitive sport. New opportunities have been created for Yogasana in events such as Khelo India and the National Games. These changes are very significant for India. Just a few days ago, an international yoga competition was held in Ahmedabad. And I was amazed. When I visited Uzbekistan, they had secured the number one position in the world, and they had gathered all the winners and gold medallists there, who were citizens of that country. This shows that wherever I go, I see and meet people, yoga has become a subject of discussion everywhere. This has also opened up avenues for those associated with yoga to participate in competitive events. Friends, Just as with yoga, the Government of India is continuously promoting Ayurveda as well. There are many things in our country that do not receive the attention they deserve, even from well-informed people, including those who have an interest in these subjects. After so many years of independence, for the first time, a separate Ministry of AYUSH was established in the country. We have encouraged research related to Ayurveda and patents based on Ayurveda. And today, we are witnessing the results of these efforts. Exports of AYUSH and herbal products from India are continuously increasing. The economy associated with the AYUSH sector has grown to several lakh crore rupees. Our turmeric which Indian families have been using for generations in their food habits and household traditions, is now witnessing growing demand across the world. India is exporting turmeric in quantities that were once beyond imagination. Its exports have been continuously increasing. We have also established the National Turmeric Board to give a new impetus to the production, quality and exports of turmeric. Friends, We must also share the benefits of our ancient sciences and modern research with humanity and also realise the opportunities associated with these fields for our youth. With this objective, the Government has introduced the facility of an e-Ayush Visa. This is making it easier for people from abroad to come to India for treatment. The World Health Organization's (WHO) Global Centre for Traditional Medicine has been established in Jamnagar. Through this centre, research and international cooperation in traditional medicine are being promoted. Friends, Today, 140 crore citizens of the country are working continuously with a shared goal. And that goal is to build a Viksit Bharat! The latest World Bank report has given a new impetus to this mission. The World Bank report states that over the past 12-13 years, nearly 50 crore Indians - this is a very significant figure - have been lifted out of extreme poverty. New opportunities for a better life now lie ahead of these millions of families. The possibilities of adopting a healthy lifestyle and paying attention to their family's health have increased. Our endeavour is to make a healthy lifestyle a priority for every family. Yoga can play a very significant role in this, and I always say that yoga liberates us from diseases, and yoga also liberates us from indulgence. Moreover, it is the most effective health remedy that comes at zero cost. Prashanti Kutiram is part of this mission, and several new initiatives are also being undertaken by the institution. It has also been extending its support. Their contribution has been very valuable to this work. Therefore, I would like you to start preparing a roadmap for new goals from now onwards. Since I have come here, I will also entrust Dr. Nagendra Ji with a few tasks before I leave. Dr. Nagendra Ji has, in fact, written to me in a letter just a few days ago that he feels younger than ever before. And therefore, his youthful energy will benefit Prashanti Kutiram as well as the nation. However, I would like you to ensure that when Prashanti Kutiram completes 75 years, the country would have become a Viksit Bharat. So, keeping this vision of a Viksit Bharat in mind, start planning now for what your next 25 years should look like, and dedicate yourselves to this mission, so that the path you follow also contributes to strengthening the nation, and your journey towards the 75th year of Prashanti Kutiram, your next 25-year journey, also becomes a source of strength for a Viksit Bharat. I urge you all to think in this direction. With this conviction, I once again extend my congratulations to all of you on the Golden Jubilee celebrations. I also once again bow in reverence at the feet of the revered Seshadri Ji. Thank you very much to all of you!
