Prime Minister - Ok Roshan ji, it is a matter of happiness that people are getting so many types of help from the ownership scheme. When the ownership scheme was brought, Roshan was saying something. Roshan - Yes Sir, people are getting a lot of benefit due to the ownership scheme sir. Some people in our village have taken loans to open shops. Earlier, they could not do anything, they could not get loans on the basis of their farming, they could not get loans on the basis of their houses, but due to the ownership card, everyone is getting loans easily, because of this people are doing their small businesses and farming too, so their income has doubled, Sir, and they are easily raising their families and children, and are living a happy life, Sir. Prime Minister - Well, Roshan ji, you have described that other people of your village are also taking advantage of this, and I would also like that all the people of the village should take advantage of these arrangements, and you have also built a house, improved farming, and your income has also doubled, and when a house is built, there is a concrete roof, then the status also increases in the village, so does yours. Roshan - Yes, Sir, all the credit for this goes to you, Sir, I want to thank you very much, Sir. Prime Minister - Well, I wish you all the best, Sir, and best wishes to the people of Nagpur as well. Roshan - Thank you Sir, thank you, thank you Sir. Prime Minister - Who is it now? Program Coordinator - Now the most respected Prime Minister will interact with another beneficiary ownership property card holder Smt. Gajendra Sangeeta ji from Rayagada district of Odisha. Sangita - My salutations to the Honorable Prime Minister. Prime Minister - Hello Sangeeta ji. Sangeeta - Hello. Prime Minister - Sangeeta ji, tell me what work you do. Sangeeta - I do sewing work, I do tailoring. Prime Minister - Yes and how many people in the family have responsibilities? Sangeeta - There are four people in my family, two children and my husband. One daughter is studying in the final year of M.Com, the second son is working in Andhra Pradesh in Kadapa, and my husband is also working in a private company. Prime Minister - Well, Sangeeta ji, getting the property rights of the house, getting the papers is not like that, let's say after the government papers, another paper has come, has it brought a big change in your life? Sangeeta - Yes sir, there has been a big change. Earlier there was no paper, there was no permanent paper, sir, the permanent paper that we got also increased our confidence that we are living in the village and we are also feeling very good about it. Prime Minister - What have you done now that you have got the papers. Sangeeta - Yes, we have just received the papers. I have applied for Pradhan Mantri Awas Yojana but it did not happen. I also do small household chores. Prime Minister - Have you taken any loan from the bank yet? Sangita - Yes Sir, I have not taken it yet, I am thinking of taking it now. Prime Minister - But have you contacted the bank, do you want to take a loan? Sangita - Yes Sir, I am thinking of taking a loan now. Prime Minister - So what will you do with the loan? Sangita - I want to expand my business a bit by taking a loan, I want to expand my tailoring business a bit, Sir. Prime Minister - So I will focus more on my business. Sangita - Yes, it can be useful for my children's education too, if some money is saved. Prime Minister - Come on Sangeeta ji, expand your work and your home, best wishes to you for this from now onwards. Through the ownership scheme, your biggest worry has ended. You have received the papers of your house and you are also a member of the self-help group. What were you saying Sangeeta ji, Sangeeta ji you were saying something. Sangeeta - It was 60 years. We did not have any permanent papers Sir, we just got it through the ownership scheme, I am very grateful to you Sir. Prime Minister - Well, your blessings are my biggest strength. See, you also work in the Self Help Group and our government is also continuously helping the women SHGs. See, the ownership scheme is going to transform the entire village. Come on, more people are waiting for us, now who is left, brother, which way to go. Program Coordinator - Jammu Kashmir. Now the most respected Prime Minister will interact with another ownership beneficiary and property card holder Shri Virendra Kumar ji of Samba district of Jammu and Kashmir. Prime Minister - Hello Virendra ji. Virendra - Hello. Prime Minister - Virendra ji, please tell me about yourself. Virendra - Prime Minister ji, I am a farmer and I and my family are very happy that I got the property card. We were living on this land for many generations and now after getting its documents, my heart feels proud. Prime Minister ji, that is why I am very grateful to you. Prime Minister - Well, earlier there were no cards and documents and other villagers may not have them either. Virendra - Sir, none of the people in our village had any papers. Many generations were living in this village for more than 100 years, but there were no papers. Now, everyone in the village is happy with the papers that we have received under the ownership scheme. Prime Minister - Well, we have received the property card, what difference has it made in your life? Virendra - I have received the property card, but there was a dispute regarding one of my lands. Because of this property card, that dispute regarding one of my lands has also been resolved. Now, because of this property card, I can take a loan from the bank by mortgaging my land and can repair my house and strengthen the financial condition of my family. Prime Minister - Well, in your village, have others also taken any benefit due to the ownership scheme? Has there been any change there as well? Virendra - Yes, sir, there has definitely been a change, Prime Minister. Whatever property cards we have received in our village under the ownership scheme, now the ownership rights of every villager have been clearly decided. As all the disputes related to land and property have been resolved to a great extent, so the villagers can take loans by keeping their land and property as collateral in the bank and are also adopting many other types of schemes, so on behalf of the villagers, I thank you from the bottom of my heart. Prime Minister - Virendra ji, it was nice to talk to you all, I am happy. Yes Sir, and it is a matter of great happiness for me that you have not just considered the card received under the ownership scheme as a paper for the house, you are also making it a way of your progress. I wish you all the best. It is cold weather, all the people of Jammu and Kashmir should take care of their health, many congratulations to you. Virendra - Thank you Sir. Program Coordinator - Now I would like to humbly request the most respected Prime Minister to address him. Namaskar! Today is a very historic day for the villages of the country, for the rural economy of the country. Honorable Governors of many states are associated with this program. Chief Ministers of Odisha, Chhattisgarh, Madhya Pradesh, Rajasthan, UP, Maharashtra and Gujarat have also joined us. Lieutenant Governor of Jammu and Kashmir, Lieutenant Governor of Ladakh are also with us. My colleagues from the Union Cabinet are present in different parts of the country, in different programs. Ministers of state governments are also present, MPs and MLAs are also present, all other public representatives are also present. All the colleagues associated with thousands of Gram Panchayats, lakhs of beneficiaries of the Swamitva Yojana, this is such a wide and huge program in itself and you all have joined it with great enthusiasm, I congratulate all of you. Friends, Swamitva Yojana was started five years ago, so that the people living in the villages could be given legal proof of their house. Somewhere it is called Gharauni, somewhere it is called Adhikar Abhilekh, somewhere it is called Property Card, somewhere it is called Malmatta Patra, somewhere it is called Residential Land Lease. The names are different in different states, but these are certificates of ownership. In the last 5 years, these ownership cards have been given to about 1.5 crore people. Now today, in this program, more than 65 lakh families have received these ownership cards. That is, under the ownership scheme, about 2.25 crore people of the village have received permanent legal documents of their houses. I congratulate all of them, give my best wishes. And because of today's program, those who have now received their government papers related to the land, how can they take advantage of it, you will definitely get ideas from the conversation I just had. Friends, In the 21st century world, there are so many challenges like climate change, water shortage, health crisis, epidemic. But there is another very big challenge in front of the world. This challenge is of property rights, authorized papers of property. Many years ago, the United Nations did a study on land property in many countries of the world. This study revealed that in many countries of the world, people do not have permanent legal documents of property. The United Nations has clearly stated that if poverty is to be reduced, then it is very important for people to have property rights. A leading economist of the world has written an entire book on the challenge of property rights. And in this book, he says that the little property that people have in villages is dead capital. That is, this property is a kind of dead property. Because the villagers, the poor people, cannot do any transaction in exchange for that property. It cannot help in increasing the family's income. Friends, India was also not untouched by this big challenge facing the world. Our situation was also the same. You also know that despite having property worth lakhs and crores of rupees in the villages of India, it was not worth that much. The reason was that people often did not have legal documents of their houses, so there were disputes regarding the ownership of the house. In many places, powerful people would occupy the houses. Without legal documents, even the banks would stay four steps away from such property. This was going on for decades. It would have been good if the previous governments had taken some concrete steps in this direction, but they did not do much in this direction. Therefore, when our government was formed in 2014, we decided to deal with this challenge of property papers, no sensitive government could leave the people of its village in such trouble. And we want everyone's development, we also want everyone's trust, our minister Rajiv Ranjan ji explained it very well. Therefore, we started the Swamitva Yojana. We decided that with the help of drones, mapping of the land of houses will be done in every village of the country, villagers will be given papers of their residential property. Today, when we are seeing the benefits of this scheme, it gives us satisfaction that we were able to work for the poor of the village. I was just talking to the beneficiaries of the Swamitva Yojana. How this scheme has changed their lives, how they are now getting help from banks on their property. You had property, you lived there but there were no papers, the government should have solved that problem and that is why we took up the work and are doing it and I was seeing in their talks the satisfaction on their faces, the happiness, the confidence, the dreams of doing something new, how enjoyable this conversation felt to me, I consider this a great blessing. Brothers and sisters, There are more than 6 lakh villages in our country. Nearly half of these villages have been surveyed by drones. After getting legal documents, lakhs of people have taken loans from banks on the basis of their houses and their properties. With this money, they have started their own small business in the village. Many of these are small and medium farmer families. For them, these property cards have become a big guarantee of economic security. Our Dalit, backward and tribal families were the most troubled and affected by illegal encroachments and long disputes in the court. Now, with the availability of legal proof, they are getting relief from this crisis. It is estimated that after the property cards are made in all the villages, the way for economic activity worth more than Rs 100 lakh crore will open. You can imagine how much capital is going to be added to the country's economy. Friends, Today our government is trying to bring Gram Swaraj on the ground with full honesty. With the ownership scheme, the planning and implementation of village development are now improving a lot. Today we will have clear maps, we will know the populated areas, so the planning of development work will also be accurate and we will also get relief from the wastage and obstacles that used to happen due to wrong planning. There are many disputes about which land belongs to the Panchayat and which land is pasture. Now, by getting property rights, the problems of the Gram Panchayats will also be solved and they will also be able to become economically strong. There are incidents of fire in the village, floods, landslides, and many such disasters. By getting property cards, disaster management will be better, it will be easy to get a proper claim in case of disaster. Friends, We also know that there are many disputes about the land of farmers. There are difficulties in getting land documents. One has to go to the Patwari again and again, and have to make rounds of the Tehsil. This also opens the way for corruption. To reduce these problems, land records are being digitized. Ownership and Bhu-Aadhaar - these two systems are going to become the basis for the development of villages. Land has also been given a special identity through Bhu-Aadhaar. About 23 crore Bhu-Aadhaar numbers have been issued. With this, it is easy to know which plot belongs to whom. In the last 7-8 years, about 98 percent of land records have been digitized. Maps of most lands are now available digitally. Friends, Mahatma Gandhi used to say- India lives in villages, the soul of India is in villages. The realization of this sentiment of Pujya Bapu has been done in the last decade. Most of the more than 2.5 crore families who have received electricity in the last 10 years are from villages. Most of the more than 10 crore families who have received toilets in the last 10 years are also from villages. Most of the 10 crore sisters who have received Ujjwala gas connections live in villages. More than 12 crore families who have received tap water in the last five years are also from villages. Most of the more than 50 crore people who have opened bank accounts are also from villages. More than 1.5 lakh Ayushman Arogya Mandirs have been built in the last decade, and they are mostly in villages, serving the health of the people of the villages. For so many decades after independence, our villages and crores of people in the villages were deprived of such basic facilities. The families of our Dalit, backward and tribal society were the most deprived. Now these families have benefited the most from all these facilities. Friends, Unprecedented efforts have been made in the last decade to ensure good roads in villages. In the year 2000, when Atal Bihari Vajpayee was the Prime Minister, the Pradhan Mantri Gram Sadak Yojana was started. Since then, about 8.25 lakh kilometers of roads have been built in villages. 8.25 lakh kilometers in so many years.... Now you see, in 10 years, we have built almost 4.75 lakh kilometers, that is, almost half of the roads in the last 10 years. Now we are also running the Vibrant Village program to increase connectivity to remote villages located on the border. And friends, Not only roads, providing internet to villages has also been our priority. Before 2014, less than 100 panchayats of the country were connected with broadband fiber connection. In the last 10 years, we have connected more than 2 lakh panchayats with broadband fiber connection. Before 2014, there were less than one lakh Common Service Centers in the villages of the country. In the last 10 years, our government has built more than 5 lakh new Common Service Centres. And these are not just figures, with these figures, facilities have reached the villages, modernity has reached. The facilities that people used to see in cities earlier, are now available in the villages. This is not only increasing facilities in the villages, but also economic strength. Friends, 2025 has also started with big decisions for the villages, for the farmers. The government has decided to continue the Pradhan Mantri Fasal Bima Yojana. Under this, till now, farmers have received claim amounts of about 1.75 lakh crore rupees, insurance money has been received. Another decision has also been taken regarding DAP fertilizer, whose prices have increased a lot in the world. The government has again made arrangements of thousands of crores of rupees, so that farmers keep getting cheap fertilizers. In the last decade, about 12 lakh crore rupees have been spent to provide cheap fertilizers to the farmers. This is almost double the amount compared to the decade before 2014. Under the Pradhan Mantri Kisan Samman Nidhi, about three and a half lakh crore rupees have reached the accounts of farmers so far. This shows the commitment of the Central Government towards farmer welfare. Friends, Women power has a very big role in building a developed India. Therefore, in the last decade, we have kept the empowerment of mothers and daughters at the center of every major scheme. Schemes like Bank Sakhi and Bima Sakhi have given new opportunities to women in villages. The Lakhpati Didi Yojana has made more than 1.25 crore women in the country Lakhpati Didis. The Swamitva Yojana has also strengthened the property rights of women. In many states, the names of wives have also been included in the property cards along with the husband. At some places, the first name is the wife's, while at other places it is the second name, but it has been done with the participation of both. Most of the houses given to the poor under the PM Awas Yojana have also been registered in the name of women. And it is such a pleasant coincidence that the drones of the Swamitva Yojana are also helping in giving property rights to women today. Drones are doing the mapping work in the Swamitva Yojana. With the Namo Drone Didi Yojana, the sisters of the village are becoming drone pilots. They are helping in farming with drones and are earning extra income. Friends, With the Swamitva Yojana, our government has given such a capability to the people of the village that can completely transform the rural life of India. If our villages and our poor are empowered, then our journey towards developed India will also be pleasant. Due to the steps taken in the interest of villages and the poor in the last decade, 25 crore people have defeated poverty. I am confident that with schemes like Swamitva, we will be able to make villages strong centers of development. Once again, best wishes to all of you. Thank you!
Your Highness, Excellencies, Namaskar! I extend a warm welcome to all of you to the extended family of BRICS. Today, this hall brings together continents, cultures, and diverse development journeys. It is a testament to the growing strength, appeal, and unprecedented trust in BRICS. Friends, Under India's Presidency, we have focused all our efforts on these four pillars: Resilience, Innovation, Cooperation, and Sustainability. I am pleased that, with all your cooperation and support, we have been able to translate our shared vision into win-win cooperation. Today, the number of conflicts and tensions in the world is continuously increasing, and this is having an increasingly negative and far-reaching impact on the lives of ordinary people. Pandemics, climate disasters, and supply-chain disruptions have shown that in today's interconnected world, no crisis remains confined to a single region. Therefore, within the BRICS group, we have placed special emphasis on strengthening Resilience. It is equally important to identify challenges in time, be prepared for them, and take prompt action. With this approach, we have agreed on a BRICS Integrated Early Warning System for the prevention of and response to infectious diseases. Early Warning Data Integration Guidelines have been prepared for disaster management. The BRICS Logistics Supply Chain Cooperation Framework will help make our supply chains more reliable and resilient. Friends, Under Innovation, the BRICS Incubator Network will connect our start-ups and incubators with one another. To promote innovative and scalable solutions, we have proposed the BRICS Startup Innovation Fund. The BRICS Network on Digital Agriculture will create new opportunities for farmers. Through this initiative, AI, geospatial technology, and Digital Public Infrastructure will be connected to the real needs of farmers. The weaponization of technology and critical minerals can hinder our shared progress. Therefore, we have emphasised inclusivity in the adoption of technology. Friends, India has advanced friendship, partnership, and cooperation through the BRICS platform. Our endeavour has been to ensure that BRICS cooperation does not remain limited to governments alone. We have sought to make our entrepreneurs, farmers, researchers, women, youth, and ordinary citizens active participants in it. Through BRICS CONNECT, we have promoted skills, employability, women in the workforce, social security, and capacity building. To connect small enterprises with knowledge, finance, and new markets, we have initiated the BRICS MSME Cooperation Portal. The BRICS Urban Mobility Hub has been established to facilitate the sharing of best practices among our cities. Friends, In Indian civilisation, nature has been accorded the status of a mother. For centuries, we have believed that development and the environment are not alternatives to each other, but are complementary. Within BRICS as well, we have emphasised the need to strike a balance between human development and the environment. This is the foundation of Sustainability. It is also our responsibility towards future generations. In this direction, we have made progress on several fronts. For Smart Grids and Energy Storage, we have established the BRICS Digital Centre of Excellence. This will help make our clean energy systems more reliable, efficient, and accessible. The Centres of Excellence for Agro-Ecology and Regenerative Agriculture will connect traditional knowledge with modern science. The principles developed for community-based climate adaptation will make indigenous knowledge an important foundation for climate action. Friends, Building on these four pillars, through BRICS we have sought to promote inclusive global growth. We believe that the benefits of solutions developed within BRICS should not remain confined to BRICS alone. They should be made adaptable and accessible to meet the needs of the Global South. Friends, From my personal experience, I can say that the Global South countries' confidence in BRICS has grown, because within BRICS: Their voices are heard. Their experiences are respected. And solutions are developed not for them, but together with them. The strength of BRICS lies in our diversity and cooperation. We may be rooted in different realities, but together, we rise through cooperation, and blossom for humanity. May our diversity remain our identity, our cooperation become our inspiration, and our progress serve the cause of all humanity. Thank you very much.
Your Excellency, President Marcos, Your Majesty, Excellencies, Namaskar! It is a great pleasure to reconnect once again with our ASEAN family. I warmly congratulate and thank President Marcos for his successful ASEAN Chairmanship and his valuable contribution as India's Country Coordinator. This is my thirteenth interaction with ASEAN leaders in this format. Over the past few years, our Act East Policy has given new momentum and greater depth to India-ASEAN relations. Our partnership has now expanded beyond our shared culture and heritage, acquiring new dimensions in areas such as digital technology, defence, and space. Since 2014, we have undertaken more than 100 initiatives to strengthen people-to-people ties, launched nearly 250 Quick Impact Projects, and provided over 10,000 skill-building scholarships. Today, direct flights between India and nine ASEAN countries, along with growing tourism, are further evidence of our close ties. Friends, Over the past few years, the world has faced one crisis after another. Today, what the world needs most is peace, stability, and predictability. In this context, this year's theme, "Navigating Our Future, Together," holds special significance. Together, India and ASEAN represent nearly one-fourth of the world's population. That is why it is essential to ensure that our future is shaped not by competition, but by cooperation. We have both the capabilities and the shared resolve to achieve this. Together, we can make this region a symbol of peace and stability. Friends, This is my third visit to an ASEAN country this year, reflecting the special importance ASEAN holds for India. We firmly believe that India's growth and ASEAN's prosperity are deeply interconnected. In the coming years, both the ASEAN Community Vision 2045 and Viksit Bharat 2047 can help us turn our shared aspirations into reality. Over the past twelve years, we have given fresh momentum to our partnership. Now, together, we must give a new direction to the future of our region. India's commitment to ASEAN unity and centrality in this partnership has been, and will remain, unwavering. I am confident that today's meeting will take our Comprehensive Strategic Partnership to new heights and contribute to building a secure, inclusive, and prosperous Indo-Pacific. I look forward to hearing your views and suggestions. Thank you very much.
Your Highness, Excellencies, Namaskar! It gives me immense pleasure to welcome and greet you all to India. India's BRICS Chairship is guided by a people-centric and humanity-first approach. Resilience, Innovation, Cooperation, and Sustainability have been our key priorities. With these priorities in mind, we have placed special emphasis on making BRICS cooperation more closely connected with the lives of ordinary people. With this vision in mind, more than 350 meetings were organised. We had the opportunity to welcome your teams in nearly 30 cities across India. I sincerely thank each one of you for your active contribution and support in making India's BRICS Chairship a success. Friends, This year's BRICS Summit marks a historic milestone. We are celebrating the 20th anniversary of BRICS. In human life, the age of twenty marks the beginning of a new phase of maturity and responsibility. It is a stage when dreams are accompanied by responsibilities, and capabilities are channelled in new directions. Today, BRICS too stands at a 'coming of age' moment. Over the past twenty years, BRICS has established a distinct identity for itself on the global stage. We respect each other's perspectives and move forward through consensus. We strive to move ahead with an approach that is not against anyone but instead seeks to take everyone along. It is now time for us to translate our unity and consensus within BRICS into concrete outcomes on the global stage. For the next twenty years, we must work on a new and ambitious agenda. We must begin by strengthening our own working systems. The BRICS Chairship changes every year, but this should not interrupt our institutional momentum. I propose the establishment of a BRICS Continuity and Implementation Mechanism. Through the Troika, this mechanism will ensure systematic follow-up on all initiatives and outcomes. We can create a secure digital repository in which, for every decision, the nodal point, timeline, and implementation status can be recorded. Friends, While strengthening BRICS, we must also chart the course for global reform. The current structure of global governance resembles a pyramid. At the top, power and decision-making are concentrated, while at the bottom are countries that are affected by these decisions but have little say in shaping them. The Global South is today in the front row of global crises, but in the back row of decision-making. We must transform this "pyramid of privilege" into a "platform of partnership", where every country has a voice, every member has a stake, and the advancement of humanity remains at the heart of every endeavour. With this objective, I propose that we work together to formulate ten proposals for global governance reform, which we could seek to develop into a BRICS Reform Roadmap by the next Summit. In preparing this roadmap, we must keep three key aspects in mind: Representation, Responsiveness, and Rule-making. First, with regard to Representation, I would like to say that reform of the UN Security Council can no longer be delayed. The text-based negotiations on this issue must be linked to a clearly defined timeline and concrete outcomes. Similarly, in financial institutions, voting power, leadership positions, and policy priorities should reflect today's economic realities. Countries that drive global economic growth should also have an appropriate role in shaping global economic governance. Second, Responsiveness. Global institutions will earn the trust of humanity only when they are able to provide effective solutions to global challenges. We must focus on the speed, scale, and last-mile impact of our collective response. Seafarers make a very significant contribution to global trade, yet they often find themselves without adequate support during times of difficulty. I propose whether we can establish a Seafarers Emergency Support Network? This network could connect the Maritime authorities and missions of the countries concerned. It could facilitate distress alerts, medical assistance, communication with families, and support for evacuation. And third, I would like to speak about rule-making. Ensuring equal participation in shaping the global rules of the future is of utmost importance. AI, cyberspace, outer space, biotechnology, and critical technologies are shaping not only the opportunities of the future, but also the rules that will govern the future. We must work to transform the Global South from rule-takers into rule-shapers. Through BRICS, we can provide young diplomats and policymakers from the countries of the Global South with negotiation skills, practical training, and legal expertise. By a happy coincidence, today is the United Nations Day for South-South Cooperation. It is especially significant that such a decision is being taken on a day dedicated to the Global South. India is ready to take the lead on this issue. Friends, The message of the BRICS Summit being hosted by India should be loud and clear: If our future is shared, then the right to shape that future must also be shared. From voice to impact. From privilege to partnership. This should be the resolve of BRICS at 20. Thank you very much.
Your Excellency, Mr. President, Delegates from both countries, Friends from media, Namaskar! I am very happy to warmly welcome the President of Switzerland to India. First of all, I appreciate his commitment to India-Switzerland relations. In October last year, with his personal support, we implemented a historic Trade and Economic Partnership Agreement between India and the European Free Trade Association (EFTA) countries. This is India's first FTA with any economic bloc in Europe. It is also an ambitious blueprint for investment, innovation, jobs, and shared prosperity. Through this, we aim to achieve the targets of $100 billion in investment in India and 1 million direct jobs over the next 15 years. Friends, The friendship between India and Switzerland goes back centuries. In the 19th century, Swami Vivekananda saw a glimpse of the Himalayas in the Swiss Alps. Mahatma Gandhi also spent time in Switzerland and interacted with its people. The memories of these historic visits continue to resonate in our friendship even today. Today, innovation, infrastructure, and advanced technology are strong pillars of our partnership. And through today's summit, we are going to give our partnership a new momentum and take it to new heights. Friends, We have taken several important decisions to increase market access for Indian exports in Switzerland and further facilitate investment between the two countries. This will help increase exports of Indian agricultural products, pharmaceuticals, textiles, and engineering goods to Switzerland. At the same time, we will attract new Swiss investments into key sectors in India, such as biotechnology, life sciences, banking, insurance, food processing, and sustainability. Through these initiatives, we will work together to develop trusted supply chains and reliable solutions for the world. Friends, Today, we identified new opportunities for cooperation in military exchanges and defence production. We also agreed to further deepen cooperation in nuclear energy. The Varanasi ropeway, Jewar Airport in Noida, and railway and tunnelling projects in mountainous areas are shining examples of our infrastructure cooperation. To further strengthen this cooperation, today we signed an agreement on transport, mobility, and infrastructure. We invite Swiss infrastructure companies to invest in India at a large scale and participate in capacity building, design, and manufacturing. Friends, Science and technology cooperation is also a strong pillar of our partnership. I am very pleased that we will soon launch new joint research projects in priority areas such as health, clean energy, and space. We also welcome the agreement with the University of Zurich to build the capacity of India's science start-ups. Given the growing popularity of Yoga and Ayurveda in Switzerland, we also had a meaningful discussion today on deepening cooperation in these areas. Friends, People-to-people ties are the greatest strength of our partnership. The Migration and Mobility Partnership Agreement and the Young Professionals Agreement signed today will open up many new opportunities in Switzerland for Indian students, researchers, professionals, and young people. Friends, Both India and Switzerland believe in democratic values, the rule of law, dialogue, and diplomacy. We will continue to work together for global peace and stability. Whether it is Ukraine or West Asia, India has consistently supported every meaningful effort towards peace. We also agree that reforms in global institutions are essential to address the growing global challenges. Friends, I am grateful to the President of Switzerland for visiting India with a large business delegation. On October 7, in his presence, we will host the "Prosperity Summit" with India-EFTA business leaders. This summit will serve as an important platform to turn the promise of our trade and investment partnership into concrete outcomes. I also extend my very best wishes to all the business leaders participating in this summit. Your Excellency, In February this year, India hosted the world's largest AI Summit. Next year, Switzerland will take this journey forward through the "Geneva AI Summit." I extend my best wishes to Switzerland for the success of the Geneva AI Summit. I am confident that together, we will continue to work towards making AI responsible, inclusive, and human-centric. Once again, thank you very much for your visit to India and for being a strong supporter of India-Switzerland relations.
Your Excellency, Mr. Prime Minister, Delegates from both countries, Friends from media, Namaskar! It gives me great pleasure to extend a warm welcome to the Prime Minister of Poland in India. During my visit to Poland in 2024, he extended a warm welcome to me and my delegation. During that visit, we elevated our relations to a Strategic Partnership. Building on this strong foundation, our partnership has gained fresh momentum over the past two years. And in today's meeting with the Prime Minister, we took several important decisions to further strengthen our Strategic Partnership. Friends, The friendship between India and Poland is rooted not only in diplomatic ties, but also in our shared heritage of humanity and compassion. During the Second World War, the royal families of Jamnagar and Kolhapur provided refuge in India to thousands of Polish children and families. Even today, the people of Poland remember the Jam Saheb with great respect as "Dobry Maharaja," meaning "Good Maharaja." In 2022, Poland carried forward this same spirit of humanity by helping ensure the safe return of our students from Ukraine. I am pleased that, through the Jam Saheb Youth Exchange Programme, young people from Poland are visiting India and carrying our historic friendship forward to the next generation. Friends, Today, the world is going through a period of uncertainty and change. Even in these challenging times, both India and Poland are emerging as important centres of growth, technology, and innovation. Last year, Poland's GDP crossed the historic milestone of one trillion dollars. I warmly congratulate Prime Minister on this achievement. Meanwhile, India also recorded an impressive growth rate of 7.8% in the last quarter. Our growing economic strength has created new opportunities for the India-Poland partnership. The India-European Union Free Trade Agreement has further expanded these opportunities. Once the agreement comes into effect, it will open up new opportunities for India to export machinery, chemicals, plastics, and engineering goods to Poland. At the same time, we will seek new investments from Poland in sectors such as railways and mobility, advanced manufacturing, food processing, and biotechnology. I am pleased that the Hon'ble Prime Minister has come to India with a large business delegation. We are confident that companies from both countries will work together to develop trusted supply chains for the world and achieve our shared goal of doubling bilateral trade by 2030. Friends, Today, we agreed to deepen our cooperation in defence, space, and nuclear energy. In all these strategic sectors, we invite Polish companies to partner with India in co-development and co-production. We will also develop new partnerships in critical minerals and coal gasification, bringing together Polish expertise. Our ties in IT and cybersecurity are already strong, with several Indian IT companies operating in Poland. Building on this cooperation, we have agreed to establish an India-Poland Startup Bridge. These initiatives will give fresh momentum to our cooperation in AI, cybersecurity, fintech, and other emerging technologies, while creating new opportunities for the youth of both countries. Friends, Cultural and people-to-people ties are the greatest strength of our relationship. We are delighted that Poland has a rich tradition of Sanskrit studies and Indology. We have established an Indian Studies Chair in the city of Kraków. Today, we also decided to further strengthen our cooperation in culture and education, as well as in sports and tourism. Friends, Today, we also held detailed discussions on regional and global developments. Both India and Poland believe in democratic values and the rule of law. We believe that reforms are necessary to make global governance institutions stronger and more effective. Whether in Ukraine or West Asia, India will continue to support every meaningful effort towards peace Your Excellency, Our shared history is deeply rooted in humanity and compassion, while our shared future holds immense possibilities for cooperation in innovation and technology. I am confident that your visit will give new momentum to the India-Poland Strategic Partnership and take it to new heights. Once again, I extend a warm welcome to you and your delegation. Thank you very much.

We are working together on advanced defence equipment, such as the Zorawar tank. In Kakinada, Andhra Pradesh, we are developing one of the world's largest green ammonia projects. Whether it is food, fuel, or supply chains, every sector is being impacted by uncertainty. In this challenging environment, India's economy has maintained its momentum. We recorded 7.8% growth in the last quarter. Today, India's growth story is becoming a source of confidence and new opportunities for the world. This unprecedented cooperation between two of the world's largest democratic powers will also give new impetus to global stability, development, and shared prosperity. India's sustained economic progress and this Agreement have opened new avenues for our economic relations. Today, we have taken several important steps to translate these possibilities into concrete outcomes. Friends, For Belgian companies, we have established an Investment Fast-Track Mechanism in India. We have decided to make the India-Belgium Business Forum a regular mechanism. We will strengthen coordination among the financial, fintech, and regulatory institutions of both countries. Through all these initiatives, we will realise our goal of doubling bilateral trade over the next five years. Friends, Defence and security cooperation is a key pillar of our relationship. Today, we agreed to enhance military exchanges and training cooperation between the two countries. The agreements between our governments and defence industries will open up new opportunities for defence co-development and co-production. We will also further strengthen intelligence sharing and cooperation between our agencies in combating crime. Friends, A clean energy future and reliable supply chains are our shared priorities. The MOU on Renewable Energy signed today will give new momentum to our sustainability cooperation. In critical minerals, we have decided to enhance cooperation in processing and recycling. Belgium's expertise in semiconductors and India's scale are complementary to each other. In this direction, we will work together to connect Belgium's IMEC Institute with India's semiconductor ecosystem. Friends, People-to-people ties are the greatest strength of our relationship. Today, we agreed to strengthen connections between our start-ups and enhance capacity building in the maritime sector. We will also strengthen university and research cooperation. And, to facilitate talent mobility, we will soon conclude a "Migration and Mobility Partnership Agreement". Friends, Today, we also had detailed discussions on regional and global developments. Both India and Belgium believe in the rule of law, dialogue, and diplomacy. Whether it is Ukraine or West Asia, we support all efforts aimed at an early end to conflicts and the restoration of peace. And eliminating terrorism in all its forms and manifestations is our shared commitment. Your Excellency, Last month, Belgium and the Netherlands jointly hosted the Hockey World Cup. Today, we are transforming this historic connection into a new and more comprehensive relationship. I am confident that today's discussions mark the beginning of a new chapter in our bilateral relations. Once again, I extend a warm welcome to you and your delegation to India. Thank you very much.

Friends, Uzbekistan is at the heart of Central Asia, and at the heart of India's engagement with the region. India and Uzbekistan share a deep convergence of perspectives and aspirations. At the heart of both "Yangi Uzbekistan" and "Viksit Bharat 2047" are our youth, innovation, inclusive growth, and modern technology. Guided by this shared vision, we are working for the well-being and prosperity of the people of both countries. This year marks the 15th anniversary of our Strategic Partnership. On this special occasion, we have decided to elevate our relationship to the level of a Comprehensive Strategic Partnership. Today, we have charted an ambitious agenda to take our partnership forward in this new framework with greater momentum and concrete outcomes. Our bilateral trade has crossed one billion dollars. We have now set a target of five billion dollars in bilateral trade by 2030. To achieve this, we will systematically address issues related to market access, connectivity, banking, and payment systems. We will work together to enhance win-win cooperation in infrastructure development. Cooperation in Digital Public Infrastructure, Artificial Intelligence, critical minerals, clean energy, space, and nuclear energy is making our relationship future-ready. Friends, Our strengths in agriculture and healthcare are complementary to each other. By combining Uzbekistan's expertise in horticulture with India's modern agricultural practices, we will contribute to the food security of both countries. Uzbekistan's medicinal herbs and India's expertise in Ayurveda are also a natural combination. The agreement reached today on Traditional Medicine will give further impetus to our joint efforts in this area. Our endeavour is to ensure that our partnership is not confined to our two capitals. Therefore, we are working towards establishing partnerships between our cities and states. These partnerships will help foster sustained direct linkages in areas such as urban management, industry, education, tourism, and culture. The close defence and security cooperation between our two countries reflects the deep mutual trust that underpins our relationship. Going forward, we will promote direct linkages, co-production, and co-development between the defence industries of our two countries. We believe that terrorism, extremism, and separatism pose serious challenges to the entire region. Our shared resolve to adopt a policy of zero tolerance towards them has been, and will continue to be, unwavering. Friends, The deepest roots of our relationship lie in our shared heritage. Today, we placed special emphasis on further strengthening these bonds. We will work together for the preservation of Uzbekistan's Buddhist heritage sites. Promoting exchanges between the youth of our two countries, particularly students and professionals, is a high priority for us. Today, we agreed on a Programme of Cooperation to promote tourism between the two countries. I am pleased that today we are going to announce scholarships to promote the Hindi language in Uzbekistan. Sports is also emerging as an important dimension of our cooperation. Last year, the 20-year-old Uzbek Grandmaster Sindarov won the FIDE World Cup in Goa. He is now set to face India's 20-year-old chess champion, Gukesh, in November. When these two young stars meet across the chessboard, the entire world will be watching this exciting contest. I also extend my very best wishes for the Chess Olympiad to be held in Samarkand next month. In the times ahead, traditional sports such as Kurash and Kabaddi will also bring renewed energy to our sporting partnership. Today's discussions have charted a clear course for the future of our relations. A stronger India-Uzbekistan partnership will be a powerful force for peace, progress and prosperity in Central Asia. Excellency, Once again, I thank you very much for your friendship and for the fruitful discussions we had today. In two days, Uzbekistan will celebrate the 35th anniversary of its Independence. On behalf of the 1.4 billion people of India, I extend my warmest congratulations and best wishes to you and all the citizens of Uzbekistan on this occasion. Thank you very much.
My friend President Macron, Excellencies, Distinguished participants, Namaskar! I am happy to be a part of the first International Space Summit. I thank President Macron for his gracious invitation and for taking this timely initiative. For centuries, space inspired our imagination. Today, it shapes life on Earth, from climate and crops to oceans and communities. The promise of space is infinite. Our responsibility is collective. As orbits become more crowded technologies more powerful, and actors more numerous our choices must be clear: cooperation over confrontation sustainability over short-term gain; and inclusion over exclusion. Friends, India's approach is rooted in Vasudhaiva Kutumbakam, that is One Earth, One Family, One Future. This spirit must now travel beyond Earth. We support a safe, secure, peaceful and sustainable space domain anchored in international law, dialogue and multilateral cooperation. Scientific data must serve the common good, with openness and integrity. India's space journey reflects this philosophy. ISRO, which stands at the heart of this journey, has earned global admiration for its excellence and cost-effective missions. Its capabilities serve farmers and fishermen, weather forecasting, disaster management and navigation. More than 430 satellites from 34 countries have travelled to space aboard Indian rockets. ISRO's technologies and partnerships serve not only India, but the wider world. Behind this global trust is the dedication and hard work of generations of Indian scientists, engineers and technicians. Working day and night, they have built ISRO's global reputation mission by mission, success by success, and given India a place of pride among spacefaring nations. Chandrayan-3 made India the first nation to land near the lunar south pole. Aditya-L1 is unlocking the secrets of the Sun. Last year, an Indian visited the International Space Station. Looking ahead, we aim to establish an Indian space station by 2035 and send an Indian to the Moon by 2040. In all these efforts, our guiding principle has been clear: their benefits must reach all humanity. Alongside ISRO, India's private space sector is also surging in a big way. The energy of enterprise is joining the excellence of ISRO. France has been a trusted partner since the Indian space program began at Thumba in the 1960s. Today, our cooperation spans Earth observation and advanced technologies, uniting scientific excellence with human purpose. We invite France and the world to be co-travellers in India's next space frontier. Friends, Let the Space Summit in Paris send a clear message: we will explore together, innovate together and protect space together, for all humanity, and for generations to come. I convey my best wishes to all the participants. Thank you.

Friends, Our effort is that new employment opportunities also grow rapidly in the private sector. With this vision, the Pradhan Mantri Viksit Bharat Rozgar Yojana has been launched. About 1 lakh crore rupees are being spent on this scheme. With its help, nearly 20 million youth will get the chance to join the workforce for the first time. Under this scheme, the government is giving an incentive of up to 15,000 rupees to youth who get their first job. Employers who create new jobs are also being encouraged by the government. That means the government is working on every front to increase employment in the country. Friends, New opportunities for youth are created only when the country's economy grows, when investment increases, new industries are set up, and new sectors expand. Today India is one of the fastest‐growing major economies. In the first quarter of this financial year, India's growth rate was 7.8 percent. Investment increased by nearly 12 percent. Rating agencies are upgrading India's ratings. And this progress is happening at a time when the world is passing through many uncertainties. Whether it is simplifying tax and compliance, increasing ease of doing business, reforms in education and skilling, opening new sectors for innovation, or ease of living - at every level, the Reform Express must continue at full speed. And as government employees, you will have a very big role in this in the present and coming years. Friends, I said a few days ago - the sun must be ours, the sea must be ours. The boat must be ours, the sailor must be ours. The courage to write victory on the waves must be ours. Today India's youth are moving forward with this courage. Now, while serving in the Government of India, it is your responsibility to understand the aspirations of the country's youth and fulfill your duties accordingly. I urge you again - every decision you take, every signature you put on a file, must advance the aspirations of youth. In your tenure, you must ensure that obstacles in the path of youth are removed, their ideas get opportunities to move forward, innovation increases investment, and investment creates new employment opportunities. Friends, Now you are becoming part of the government system yourselves. Therefore, your effort must be that the difficulties you faced, or that your parents faced, should not be faced by other youth. The hardships your parents endured, the hardships you may have endured - no citizen of our country should have to endure them anymore. A scholarship for a student may depend on your responsibility. A facility for a farmer may be delivered through your department. For some citizen, you may be the face of the government. Your behavior, your sensitivity, your efficiency, your honesty - all these will determine how that citizen experiences the government. Thank you very much.

Today, in the capital of France, Paris, the grand Swaminarayan temple is being inaugurated. This inauguration is a new milestone in the cultural relations between India and France. With the blessings of revered saints and the noble custodians of the Sanatan tradition, today this inauguration platform has become a stage of the spirit of Vasudhaiva Kutumbakam - "the world is one family." By the grace of Lord Swaminarayan and the affection and blessings of our saints, it is my fortune that I always get the opportunity to be connected with such sacred occasions. Even today, though I am not physically present among you in Paris, in mind and heart I feel myself to be right there with you. Friends, On this occasion, I also respectfully remember Param Pujya Pramukh Swami Maharaj. In ancient times, India's cultural and spiritual glory was immense, and he began a campaign for its renaissance. Today its light is spreading across Europe and the entire world. In 2024, the grand temple in Abu Dhabi was inaugurated. Today thousands of people go there for darshan. And now here in Paris, the construction of the Swaminarayan temple - this continuity of creation - is proof of the resolve and strength of our saints. I express gratitude to Pujya Mahant Swami for this sacred work, I bow at his feet. I extend heartfelt congratulations to the BAPS Swaminarayan organization and to millions of Hari devotees on this occasion. Friends, Through BAPS, when temples are consecrated in different countries, India's ancient ideas and human values also travel with them. And India's thought says: "Samanam manah saha chittamesham" - may our minds be united, may our hearts be connected. Therefore, today when such a grand temple has been built in France, it will enrich France's diversity, and it will also energize the cultural relations between India and France. Friends, In recent years, India and France's relations have reached new heights. Together with my friend President Macron, both countries are building a Special Global Strategic Partnership. From technology, AI, and defense to space and climate action, we are moving forward with new speed and new energy. We are also celebrating 2026 as the India-France Year of Innovation. This strategic partnership is special because it rests on the strong foundation of our old cultural and political ties. History is witness - the sacrifices of Indian soldiers in France are still alive in the temple of memory. Those interested in languages also know how French scholars played a crucial role in popularizing Sanskrit. The writings of Romain Rolland on Ramakrishna Paramhansa and Swami Vivekananda brought our societies closer. From Paris to the Puducherry Aurobindo Ashram, "The Mother's" spiritual journey inspired countless Indian and French seekers. Friends, Decades ago, Srila Prabhupada Swami also came to France. Through ISKCON he began a new chapter of spiritual relations between India and France. Today yoga too has become a medium of people‐to‐people connection between India and France. The pictures from in front of the Eiffel Tower on International Yoga Day are examples of yoga's popularity in France. And now, through this grand BAPS temple, a new chapter is being added to our cultural relations. Friends, These days, there are discussions about India-France joint ventures. The Swaminarayan temple has added a new dimension to this possibility. This temple is "Made in India" and "Built in France." Many of its stones were carved in India. India's ancient craftsmanship has joined here with modern engineering in France. In Paris, this grand temple has taken shape. Therefore, I hope this temple will stand for centuries as a symbol of cooperation and possibilities between India and France. Friends, Swaminarayan temples have always been not only centers of devotion but also of service. I have been told that this temple too will become such a center of service projects. I am confident that the voices of Indian culture that will arise from here will benefit people across Europe. In the future, whenever I get the time, I will surely make the effort to come and have darshan in this temple. With these words, once again I extend many congratulations on this auspicious occasion to all the guests present, all families, all Hari devotees, and the entire Indian diaspora. Thank you very much! Merci beaucoup! Jai Swaminarayan!

I congratulate and send my best wishes to every sister who is benefitting from this scheme. Friends, In our homes, mothers and sisters carry many responsibilities. Amidst numerous small and big decisions, a woman takes care of the family in every way. And when she has less worry about essential expenses, her confidence increases even more. The Annapurna Yojana is such an important effort to strengthen the confidence of Bengal's women. Every month, a fixed amount reaches the bank accounts of Bengal's sisters - an amount for which they do not have to ask anyone. This is a great support in their lives. Friends, Today, the fourth installment of the Annapurna Yojana is reaching the accounts of nearly 15 million sisters. That means 3,000 rupees every month. For a mother, this could be the expense of her child's education. For a sister, it could be support for medicine or essential household items. Friends, Our vision is that Bengal's women should have the power to make decisions and the courage to move forward. Annapurna Yojana plays a big role in this. And I know, in this transformation, the most important role has been played by the mothers and sisters of Bengal. You have seen many hardships in your lives. You have waited for many things. Therefore, the first test before our new government was to make the lives of Bengal's women easier, to increase ease of living in their lives. That is why the guiding mantra we adopted was - Less talk, more work. Friends, The Annapurna Yojana began with this very thought. Within just a few days of forming the government, work started on this scheme. And today, money is directly reaching the bank accounts of such a large number of women. No cut money, no middlemen! Our government's goal is that not a single eligible woman should be deprived of the benefits of the Annapurna Yojana. Work is progressing rapidly for this too. Friends, Solving every problem connected with the life cycle of mothers, sisters, and daughters has been the highest priority of the BJP. To ensure nutritious food for the child during pregnancy, we launched the Matru Vandana Yojana, sending money directly to women's bank accounts. To prevent daughters from being eliminated in the womb, we started mass movements like Beti Bachao, Beti Padhao. To ensure daughters do not drop out of school due to lack of toilets, we built millions of toilets. To continue their education, we offered high interest in the Sukanya Samriddhi Yojana. To allow daughters to enter Sainik Schools, we opened their doors. To help daughters start their own work, we launched the Mudra Yojana with collateral‐free loans. To ensure mothers could continue their jobs after childbirth, we increased maternity leave. To provide new earning opportunities in villages, we gave record support to self‐help groups. To free daughters from smoke in the kitchen, we gave Ujjwala gas connections. To free them from the struggle of fetching water, we launched the Har Ghar Nal Se Jal scheme. To free them from darkness, we gave free electricity connections under the Saubhagya scheme. To ensure daughters do not hide their illness due to lack of money, we gave them the strength of the Ayushman card. To give daughters access to banks, we opened millions of accounts under Jan Dhan Yojana. To give daughters rights over property, we ensured joint ownership in PM Awas houses. Friends, One of Bengal's great pains has been that in the past 12 years, many central schemes did not reach here on time. Poor families suffered the most because of this. Now, under the BJP government, these stalled schemes are being advanced rapidly. For example, the PM Awas Yojana. After the BJP government was formed in Bengal, more than 5 million new poor families have been identified to receive permanent houses under PM Awas. The PM Vishwakarma Yojana has also been implemented here. Every pending scheme, every stuck project, is now gaining new speed in Bengal. And in this, the blessings of Bengal's women and people are with the BJP. That is why I say with great confidence - Together we will build a new Bengal. Friends, The nation has taken a pledge for a developed India. I am confident that Bengal's mothers and sisters will continue to give new momentum to this journey of development. With the strength of a developed Bengal, the pledge of a developed India will become even stronger. Friends, Your affection, your blessings, continuously inspire us to serve. Once again, I extend many congratulations to all of you on Annapurna Day. Heartfelt wishes for Vishwakarma Puja! Thank you!

I thought you would sit down after a while because your hands would start hurting. I request the SPG personnel to collect that beautiful picture which this daughter has brought. Otherwise, she will grow tired holding it up. They will collect it from here, my child. Please stay where you are. Let the SPG personnel collect it. After Independence, the needs of small farmers were never truly understood. Nobody paid attention to them. Yet small farmers account for more than 85 percent of all farmers in our country. More than 85 percent. It is our government that understood their concerns and supported them through PM-Kisan Samman Nidhi. Friends, Over the past few years, global uncertainties caused fertiliser prices to surge sharply. But a government dedicated to farmers did not allow that burden to fall upon them. The government spent nearly ₹16 lakh crore on this. To ensure that fertilisers remain affordable for you, we had to spend ₹16 lakh crore from the national treasury, so that the burden would not fall upon our farmers. Friends, Previous governments repeatedly misled farmers on Minimum Support Price. But ours is the government that fulfilled its MSP promise. Over the past twelve years, farmers growing wheat and paddy have received ₹24 lakh crore in MSP payments directly into their accounts. Friends, Our government has provided farmers with a powerful safety net through the PM Fasal Bima Yojana. We launched crop insurance on a large scale. During the past ten to twelve years, farmers have received more than ₹2 lakh crore in crop insurance compensation. Whenever crops suffered losses, over ₹2 lakh crore reached farmers through insurance support. We are continuously trying to reduce farmers' irrigation costs as well. PM-KUSUM is playing an increasingly significant role in this effort. Under this scheme, nearly twelve lakh solar pumps have already been provided. Twelve lakh pumps powered by solar energy have been given to farmers for irrigation. Because of this, the need for electricity and diesel has reduced substantially. If these solar pumps had not been provided, farmers would have had to purchase more than 80 crore litres of diesel. That entire expense has been saved by lakhs of farmers. And besides that, additional electricity has also been generated. If viewed in one way, these five schemes and decisions alone have either directly benefited farmers or saved them the equivalent of ₹50 lakh crore. This is an enormous figure. These ₹50 lakh crore have increased farmers' incomes and strengthened Indian agriculture. Friends, Agriculture is our tradition. Agriculture is our culture. Agriculture is our culture. During the past twelve years, our government has extensively integrated science and technology into farming. We have linked agriculture and animal husbandry with scientific methods. Soil Health Cards are an excellent example of this. Earlier, farmers found it difficult to determine what type of fertilizer their fields required and in what quantity. Soil Health Cards solved this problem. Today, more than 26 crore Soil Health Cards are in the hands of farmers. These have modernised farming practices. Friends, Changing weather patterns are a major challenge for farmers. Therefore, our effort is to continuously provide farmers with new seed varieties that are better suited to changing climatic conditions. Over the past years, around three thousand new seed varieties have been made available to farmers. Friends, Animal husbandry is an important part of our rural economy, and our sisters have a major role in it. During the past decade, we have strongly promoted the use of science and technology in animal husbandry and the dairy sector. When livestock breeds improve, milk production increases. As Shankarbhai was saying earlier, we want one cow to produce as much milk as four cows once did. If animals receive timely vaccination and treatment, losses due to disease are reduced. In other words, healthier animals mean higher incomes for our livestock-rearing brothers and sisters. Friends, You are all familiar with the National Gokul Mission. Through it, indigenous breeds are being preserved and scientific breeding is being promoted. The new research centre established in Banaskantha will further strengthen this mission. Indigenous cattle breeds such as Gir, Sahiwal, and Kankrej, and buffalo breeds such as Mehsana, Murrah, and Jaffarabadi, will be preserved here. At the same time, major work will be done to improve their quality. For the Kankrej breed, this is the first facility of its kind in the country. Consider a farmer who owns a high-quality cow that produces more milk and remains healthy at a lower cost. Naturally, that farmer would want future generations of that cow to retain those same qualities. Modern technologies such as IVF help achieve exactly that. They increase the likelihood that superior traits will pass on to future generations. The direct benefit of this goes to livestock rearers. Friends, Protecting livestock from disease is also one of our priorities. Foot-and-mouth disease affects both animal health and milk production. Therefore, we launched a nationwide free vaccination campaign. So far, nearly 150 crore vaccine doses have been administered to animals. The livestock of more than eight crore farmers have received free vaccinations. This has also reduced the financial burden on livestock rearers. Friends, The impact of these efforts is visible in milk production as well. In 2013-14, a cow or buffalo produced on average less than five litres of milk per day. This was the situation when you sent me to Delhi. Less than five litres a day. Today, the national average has increased to around eight litres per day. India's growth rate in milk productivity is the fastest in the world. India ranks number one globally in milk production. The benefits have reached villages, and the incomes of farming families have increased. Friends, We want livestock rearers to earn not only from milk but also from dung. Infrastructure is being developed for producing biogas, bio-CNG, and organic fertilisers from dung and agricultural residues. Institutions like Banas Dairy have an important role in this. Today, foundation stones have also been laid for two bio-CNG plants. Our objective is clear: healthier livestock, higher milk production, and complete utilisation of dung. We are transforming animal husbandry into not merely a tradition but also a strong means of increasing farmers' incomes and enriching villages. I was interacting with the women here earlier. They told me they supply milk worth ₹2 crore. Milk worth ₹2 crore. Then I asked them how much education they had received. They replied, "Four books," and yet they supply milk worth ₹2 crore. Friends, Over the past years, we have worked with a holistic approach for the prosperity of villages. Just look at the fisheries sector and see how much it has expanded. You will be pleased to know that India's seafood exports have more than doubled. Our fishermen go out to sea, catch fish and shrimp, and sell them. Today, this sector has made enormous progress, and demand across the world has risen sharply. Cooperation in the fisheries sector is also being promoted. Friends, There is another sector that has provided an additional source of income for our farmers. That sector is beekeeping. Over the past twelve years, honey production has doubled. I remember that when I came to Banaskantha, I urged all of you to bring about a Sweet Revolution, a Honey Revolution. Today Banaskantha is setting new records in honey production. When I was interacting with the women earlier, they told me they sell honey worth ₹35 lakh. You will be proud to know that India has become the world's second-largest exporter of natural honey. In this Sweet Revolution, the contribution of our women's self-help groups is also steadily increasing. Friends, A few days ago, on 7th October, I completed twenty-five years as head of government. It feels as though it happened only yesterday. Yes, it feels like yesterday itself, when I first took the oath on . You all gave your Narendra Bhai immense affection and immense love.Today, I felt a little awkward here. Whatever is said in the rest of the country is one thing, but here Shankarbhai said, "Prime Minister Sahib, Prime Minister Sahib." Tell me honestly, how will such an address go down my throat? Here, people habitually say, "Our Narendra Bhai has come." Doesn't it feel better to say that? What joy is there in saying "Prime Minister Sahib"? And when my brothers from Gujarat say "Our Narendra Bhai," my heart overflows with emotion. More than affection, every Gujarati taught me something. They guided me towards the right path and inspired me. At that time, I knew nothing about government offices, government functioning, or files. A person from an ordinary family, whose generations had never even dreamed of becoming the head of a village, was entrusted by you with the responsibility of serving first Gujarat and then the nation. Earlier while speaking with the women, they asked me how ideas such as Jyotigram, bringing water, natural farming, and initiatives for livestock came to me. I told them that there was no special formula. You taught me everything, and I simply went and implemented it. Friends, I have always known only two things: service to the people is service to the nation, and the development of Gujarat leads to the development of India. Everything else, how to work and how to move forward, you taught me. Whatever I have been able to do for the nation belongs to all the citizens of India, and to all of you as well. For this, I will remain indebted to you for my entire life. Until my final breath, I will remain devoted to service. Service, service, and service to the people of the country and to all of you. Friends, The senior citizens present here will still remember the old days. Twenty-five years ago, when I used to come here, there were severe problems of electricity and water. Even then, I used to say that we must save electricity and water. Farmers would protest against me, saying, "Who is Modi to tell us to save water? He won't even give us electricity." I kept arguing with you, and eventually you accepted what I was saying. And today, just look at where we have come from and where we have reached. Those old problems now remain only in memories. Today, I will once again tell you the same thing: we must save water, and we must save electricity as well. Friends, The waters of the Narmada and modern technologies such as drip irrigation have transformed Banaskantha. They have changed both the face and the destiny of Banaskantha. Banaskantha's potatoes are now world-famous. No one can come to a person from Banaskantha and say, "I will turn potatoes into gold," because he will reply, "I am already doing that." We have a cooperative brand like Banas Dairy here, which is expanding the cooperative movement to other parts of the country, including Kashi. I am especially grateful to Banas Dairy because even in my Kashi, people are benefiting from your experience and your service. Now, even the French fries potato processing plant has expanded its capacity. This will make it possible to purchase a larger quantity of potatoes. This is a major facility and support for potato farmers. Friends, A Cluster Development Programme is being implemented for farmers engaged in fruit and vegetable cultivation. Under this programme, twelve clusters have been approved today. These clusters are associated with fruits and vegetables such as tomatoes, potatoes, chillies, grapes, bananas, onions, oranges, pomegranates, and cocoa. Under this initiative, farmers will be supported in establishing new orchards and rejuvenating old ones. People will be trained and guided for the rejuvenation of older orchards, and subsidies will be provided for the entire project. The two clusters approved in North Gujarat will greatly benefit potato farmers of Banaskantha and Sabarkantha. Friends, Today, several other development projects related to Gujarat have also been inaugurated and launched. Gujarat has received two new railway services: the Ahmedabad-Varanasi Sabarmati Express and the Mehsana-Becharaji-Sabarmati MEMU train. Both are highly significant rail connectivity projects. In addition, the Ahmedabad Metro service is also being expanded. Today, the foundation stone has been laid for metro projects connecting GIFT City and the airport. The Ahmedabad-Mehsana Highway is now set to be expanded from four lanes to eight lanes. Friends, Today, more than 4,000 modern Anganwadis are also being inaugurated for the children of Gujarat. Nearly four lakh children and pregnant mothers will benefit from them. Friends, Every such effort is aimed at making India a developed nation. Today, every farmer, every young person, and every daughter and sister in the country is moving forward with the resolve of building a Developed India. But unfortunately, some people do not like this. Some people are unable to accept it. These are people who want to see the country in difficulty. They derive satisfaction from seeing the nation in trouble. Whenever challenges arise for the country, they feel happy. Friends, A recent example of this is the war taking place around Iran and the Gulf region. At a time when India is united in dealing with its consequences, these people were busy spreading rumours. Rumours were spread among farmers that fertilisers would not be available. Artificial queues were created and the atmosphere was disturbed. Women were frightened by being told that cooking gas would no longer be available, asking them how they would cook food and how their children would go to school. Their plan was to create panic and disorder in the country so that they could defame Modi. Friends, I want to remind the farmer brothers and sisters of this country that I had said Modi would stand firmly with you. And you can see that even though the war is still continuing, Modi did not allow any shortage of fertilisers for farmers. We did not allow fertiliser supplies to run short. Friends, Because of this war, fertiliser prices around the world have risen sharply. The chemicals used in producing fertilisers have also become much more expensive. Yet the government did not allow this burden to fall on farmers. A bag of urea that costs around ₹3,000 elsewhere in the world is available to Indian farmers for less than ₹300. The global price of a bag of urea is around ₹3,000, but the government bears that burden and provides it to you for around ₹300. The price of a bag of DAP fertiliser has crossed ₹5,000 globally. But Indian farmers receive the same bag for around ₹1,350. The nation is providing this support so that our farmers become stronger and their lives improve. Friends, In a world full of uncertainty, India's rapid development has become a new source of hope for the entire world. In such a situation, we must continue moving forward on the path of Ek Bharat, Shreshtha Bharat - One India, Great India. And tomorrow, the 31st of October, is a day to strengthen this resolve. Tomorrow is the birth anniversary of Sardar Vallabhbhai Patel and National Unity Day. The entire nation will celebrate National Unity Day. On this day, the Run for Unity is organised across the country. Reports are coming in from all directions about the grand preparations being made for the Run for Unity. I would especially urge all citizens of the country, particularly our young friends, to participate enthusiastically in the Run for Unity tomorrow morning. Friends, Every year, the Unity Festival is celebrated in Ekta Nagar on the morning of 31st October. But this year, keeping the Run for Unity and other programmes in mind, it will be held in the evening instead of the morning. This is a change from previous years. Tomorrow at 5:30 in the evening, a grand Unity Festival will be celebrated in Ekta Nagar. The programme will be organised on a very large scale. You should watch it on television. There will be such a parade on the soil of Gujarat that it will remind you of the Republic Day celebrations in Delhi. Do watch it. There will be parades by police and security forces, and impressive tableaux will be displayed. I urge all citizens of the country to join this programme tomorrow evening as well. From Kashmir to Kanyakumari, from Kutch to Kamakhya, the entire nation should pay tribute to Sardar Patel and reaffirm the resolve of Ek Bharat, Shreshtha Bharat. This time, however, our athletes have returned with one hundred and fourteen medals. Just think about this journey from fourteen to one hundred and fourteen. It is the result of the highest level of your hard work. It is the outcome of your dedication to sports and the ecosystem that has developed around it. This time, with thirty golds, thirty-five silvers, forty-nine bronzes, five new world records, and nine Asian records, you have filled the entire nation with pride. Out of fifty-six women athletes, thirty-four daughters became medal winners. And in four events, all three podium positions belonged to India.
