Honey broke his own previous world record. Himanshu, Ravi, and Jehan showed the world through their performances how physical challenges can be overcome. Friends, Whether it is the personal achievement of an athlete or the collective success of a nation, the world sees only the shine of victory. But the years of hard work behind that moment, the sweat shed day and night, and the unwavering dedication and perseverance required to reach that point are what form the foundation on which the towering structure of success stands. For example, Rinku ji lost her mother just two months before the Asian Para Games. Even amidst such great sorrow, she maintained her courage. And when she won the gold medal, she raised her mother's photograph and dedicated that medal to her. That moment touched the hearts of all Indians. Friends, Your struggles and achievements are an inspiration to every Indian. And in this journey, your path and the nation's path have been very similar. That is why today, your efforts and the efforts of the nation are combining and creating an even greater force together. Friends, After 2014, when the country began its journey towards bigger goals, the situation was very different. Most parents were afraid to send their children into sports and the children themselves were often hesitant. There was disorder in the system, a lack of resources, a lack of support, and a lack of transparency. People used to say that nothing could change in such a system. They had accepted it as a reality. But just as you never accepted defeat before any challenge, the country too kept its eyes fixed only on its goals. We resolved that we would not allow the talent of our youth to go to waste. Therefore, we decided to change the very system that prevented young people like you from moving forward. As a result, a new approach towards sports emerged. Today, if a young athlete has faith in his or her talent, that athlete also believes that the country stands with them. Our para-sports ecosystem itself is one of the biggest examples of this. In sports where India's representation was once extremely limited, our presence is now growing stronger. In archery, athletics, shooting, swimming, table tennis, badminton, judo, powerlifting, and many other sports, new young athletes are emerging. We are discovering new champions. This time, sixty-one of our athletes participated in the Asian Para Games for the first time. Sixty-one completely new athletes. And among those first-time participants, thirty-six returned home with medals. Sandeep even created a world record in his debut itself. This participation of young athletes reflects the India of today and announces the India of the future. Friends, We do not view sports merely as a collection of national and international events. For us, sports are also a powerful medium for character-building and nation-building. It is sports that teach young people how to win after losing. Sports teach discipline and the meaning of team spirit. Friends, With this vision, we are building a modern sports ecosystem for the country. Campaigns are being run at schools and at the grassroots level. A large number of Khelo India Centres have been established. We also launched the TOPS Scheme so that athletes could receive Olympic-level training. Keeping the Olympics in mind, I called upon young people from the Red Fort this time to come forward through the Talent Hunt campaign. Friends, Para-sports are being given equal importance in all these efforts. As a result, athletes like you are emerging with excellence and bringing glory to the nation at major events. We saw this once again in the recently concluded Para Games. In this competition, athletes receiving support under TOPS won forty-one medals, while Khelo India athletes won nineteen medals. This shows that our efforts are moving in the right direction. Friends, Today, modern sports policies in India are creating many new opportunities for young people, even beyond the playing field. Sports infrastructure development, sports equipment production, and the sports broadcasting economy are all expanding rapidly. Friends, Just a few days ago, the government approved the Khelo India Sports Goods Manufacturing Scheme worth more than ₹1,800 crore. Now it is time for our sports goods manufacturing industry to achieve new milestones. When an Indian or foreign athlete steps onto a field anywhere in the world, their sports gear should also be Made in India. Their clothing and footwear should also be Made in India. We want India to produce not only world-class athletes but also world-class sports equipment. We want Indian sports products to reach markets across the world. This will strengthen India's MSME sector and create new opportunities in small towns and rural areas. Friends, After today's event, all of you will also be invited to schools, colleges, and various institutions, where young people will want to listen to you and learn from you. When you speak with them, teach them the secrets of winning on the field, but also tell them about the opportunities connected with sports beyond the field. And you should also explain to them the extensive efforts being made by the country to make life easier for our differently-abled brothers and sisters. Just a few days ago, the Cabinet took an important decision regarding pensions for persons with disabilities. We increased the pension amount. The disability benchmark that was previously 80 percent has been reduced to 40 percent. Earlier, a person had to be eighteen years old to become eligible for a pension. Now, the path for pensions has been opened even for eligible children with disabilities from birth. Similarly, significant efforts have been made to promote Indian Sign Language. A sign language dictionary containing more than ten thousand words has been developed. School textbooks are also being made available in sign language. Through the Accessible India Campaign, public places are being made more accessible for persons with disabilities. Our effort is to ensure that every differently-abled person in the country receives both dignity and equal opportunities, and is able to move forward in life on the strength of their own abilities. Friends, The podium at Aichi-Nagoya is not the final destination of your journey. Keep that in mind. You should use this success as a launching pad for even bigger goals. The next target for India, and for most of you athletes, is L.A. 2028. Avani and Manish have already secured their quota places for Los Angeles. Our goal should be to ensure that as many Indian athletes as possible participate in L.A. 2028 and bring home as many medals as possible. Therefore, celebrate this moment wholeheartedly, but also begin preparing for the next challenge right away. The country stands with you, and so do I. My very best wishes to all of you. Thank you very much.
Bharat Mata Ki Jai. Bharat Mata Ki Jai. Bharat Mata Ki Jai. My dear countrymen, Today, crores of Indians across the country and in every corner of the world are celebrating the sacred festival of lights. People are travelling from far and wide to reach their homes. It is a great festival of meeting and spending time with family and loved ones. And I too have come among you to celebrate Diwali. Whether on the borders or within the country, all of you who defend the nation, all of you who stand guard over the nation, all you brave sons and daughters of Mother India, are my own people, a part of my family. From Kargil in the lap of the Himalayas to Lepcha, from Sir Creek on the seashore to INS Vikrant in the embrace of the sea, every Diwali spent with our brave soldiers has been memorable for me. My brave soldiers, This time, I have come to celebrate Diwali in the forests of Bastar, among all of you. This is the land where Naxalite terror once held sway, and because of your sacrifice, dedication, and perseverance, that very land has now been freed from the scourge of Naxalite terror and has begun a new journey of development. And friends, This Diwali of 2026 is truly special. After forty or fifty years, such a Diwali has arrived for all of you. This is the first Diwali when the country has won its decades-long battle against Naxalism and Maoist terror. This is the first Diwali when every district of the country is free from Naxalism. This is the first Diwali when crores of citizens have overcome the fear of Naxalism. This is an extraordinary achievement. A very large share of the credit belongs to you, to lakhs of brave personnel like you, to our soldiers, sons and daughters, and security personnel. Therefore, today I have come to celebrate Diwali with you. And I have also come to express, on behalf of 140 crore Indians, gratitude for your sacrifice, dedication, and spirit of service. Friends, Our visionary ancestors said: "Abhyantaram bhayam rakshyam, asaram bahyato bhayam." This means that when a nation's internal security is strong, external enemies cannot do much harm. But when internal security weakens, external enemies also begin to raise their heads. Therefore, the role that our armed forces play on the borders is the same role that all of you security personnel play within the country. You have made countless sacrifices for the unity and integrity of the nation. You have shown limitless dedication. The security forces engaged in preserving internal security, our CRPF, CoBRA, BSF, ITBP, every single jawan, the SSB, IRB, the police personnel of the states, the commandos of special forces such as the Bastar Fighters, and our women fighters, this nation is indebted to all of you for your service. Crores and crores of citizens are proud of you. With this feeling of gratitude and affection, I extend my heartfelt Diwali greetings to every one of you, wherever you are standing guard. I also convey my greetings to our soldiers deployed on the borders for the defence of the nation. Friends, This battle against Maoist terror and Naxalism was not easy. There was a time when more than 230 districts across nearly 20 states of the country were affected by Naxalism. After sunset, life would come to a standstill in these areas. It became impossible to step outside one's home. These Naxalites had even snatched away the people's right to vote. Bihar, Maharashtra, Odisha, Uttar Pradesh, Madhya Pradesh, and Chhattisgarh, a very large part of the country was affected by Left-wing extremism. Thousands of villages and crores of people were trapped by it. There were as many as two thousand Naxalite attacks in a single year. Just remember, in 2010, nearby in Dantewada, 76 CRPF personnel were martyred in an encounter with Naxalites. In the same year, another incident in Dantewada claimed the lives of 44 innocent civilians. This land was consecrated by the blood of our brave men. But not a single cry of grief was heard from those privileged leaders living in their palaces. Not even a single tear emerged from their eyes. Such attacks occurred repeatedly. This cycle continued for decades. During this period, more than one thousand security personnel sacrificed their lives in Chhattisgarh alone. Many soldiers lost their arms and legs in IED blasts. If you look at the figures from 2004 to 2013, every year more of our brave soldiers were martyred than the number of Naxalites eliminated. The leaders of Naxalite organisations considered this their victory and celebrated it. And unfortunately, the governments of that time had accepted Naxalism as an incurable disease and simply left it unattended. My tribal families were left to survive on their fate. It had been accepted that this would continue endlessly, bullets would keep flying, soldiers would keep dying, and innocent citizens would keep suffering. Friends, No nation can function with an attitude of "this is how things will continue." To move a nation forward, its enemies must be crushed with determination, courage, and a clear strategy. The political will that was lacking in the country for this task was restored in 2014. You gave this Pradhan Sevak an opportunity to serve Mother India. And after 2014, we saw both the situation and the statistics begin to change. For years, discussions revolved around the number of security personnel being killed compared to the number of Naxalites. But after 47 years, for the first time, not a single CRPF jawan lost his life during duty over the past year. This is the victory of the country's correct policies and firm resolve. This is the victory of your bravery, your courage, and your restraint. I repeat, this is the victory of your bravery, your courage, and your restraint. Bharat Mata Ki Jai. Bharat Mata Ki Jai. Victory to these brave soldiers. Our security forces and police personnel remained steadfast even in the most adverse circumstances. This is the victory of that devotion to duty. Friends, Success requires the right intentions and the right policies. For national security, we took one tough decision after another. Many such steps were also taken that are still not known to the public. Through these decisions and efforts, a major blow was dealt to the funding networks of Naxalites and separatists. The nexus of fake currency was destroyed. This not only broke their financial backbone but also choked their entire supply chain. As a result, the supply of weapons stopped, and restrictions were imposed on the movement and recruitment of Naxalites. Friends, Previous governments had also tied the hands of the security forces fighting on the ground. Naxalite attacks kept taking place, but the security forces did not have the freedom to retaliate decisively. We gave the security forces the freedom to take every necessary decision. We also worked to stop the support that Left-wing extremism used to receive from certain people sitting within the government and the system. Friends, We were pursuing a very large goal. Our goal was that no mother's lap should be left empty, no family should be trapped in a cycle of destruction, no child should pick up weapons and walk the path of violence, and no young person's future should be ruined. Therefore, until this goal was achieved, we continued to work with determination. There was no publicity, no display. Working with sensitivity, we broke the backbone of Maoist terror. Through a spirit of service, we transformed lives in Naxal-affected regions. That is why people who had suffered from Naxalism also stood firmly with the government and the security forces. Even here today, there are citizens present who contributed greatly to this major victory. I express my gratitude to all of them as well. Friends, When we work with the spirit of Nation First, the country's peace and security are ensured, the nation reaches new heights of development, and the participation of every poor and deprived citizen in development also increases. Therefore, over the past twelve years, the country has not only won the fight against Naxalism and separatism, but it has also won a major battle against poverty. Friends, Just before Diwali this year, a piece of news emerged that is being discussed across the country and around the world. A few days ago, the World Bank stated in its report that in India, over the past twelve years, fifty crore people, fifty crore people, have risen out of extreme poverty. What decades of slogans about removing poverty could not achieve, the country accomplished in just twelve years. Just imagine how great an achievement this is. Think about how it became possible. Friends, During Diwali, we create rangoli designs. At first, only a few lines are drawn, and the initial patterns appear small and separate. But as one small part after another comes together, a beautiful and complete picture emerges. Over the past twelve years, something similar has happened in India. We created a rangoli of welfare schemes that continuously empowered the poor. Through the Jan Dhan Yojana, crores of people became connected to the banking system. Through the PM Awas Yojana, crores of families received permanent homes. Under the Mudra Yojana, we provided loans without collateral. Through the SVANidhi Scheme, street vendors also received support and opportunities to expand their businesses. Under Ayushman Bharat, we provided free healthcare to the poor. Crores of families received free food grains every month. Policies were created for children's education. In other words, we ensured free medicine, free education, and free kitchen support for every poor family. Not only that, schemes such as the Swachh Bharat Mission and Har Ghar Jal removed many basic concerns of daily life. As a result, crores of people have come out of poverty today. Families to whom poverty was once passed on as an inheritance are now inheriting the foundation of a better future. Families whose entire lives were spent in anxiety now have the confidence that comes from trust in the government and a renewed sense of self-belief. Friends, A major foundation of this victory against poverty is also linked to the country's fight against Naxalism and separatism. The people of Bastar and all of you know that wherever Naxalites held influence, their first agenda was to stop development and harm the lives of ordinary people. Schools for children were not allowed to function and were often set on fire. Hospitals for the sick were not allowed to be built, and doctors were prevented from entering. Facilities such as medical stores, which help save lives, were not permitted. These Naxalites even prevented children from receiving polio vaccinations. They did not allow mobile towers to be installed. You have seen for years how railway tracks were blown up with bombs and how industries and businesses were prevented from functioning. And the result was that lakhs of poor and innocent citizens, my brothers and sisters, and my young brothers and sisters, remained deprived of opportunities. Their lives never escaped the cycle of poverty. Therefore, when we began the fight against Naxalism, we simultaneously launched special campaigns for the development of Naxal-affected regions. We built more than 15,000 kilometres of roads in areas affected by Left-wing extremism, with complete security arrangements. More than 9,500 mobile towers were installed in these regions. Around 175 Eklavya schools were opened for the education of tribal children. Nearly 50 Industrial Training Institutes were established to connect youth with employment opportunities. Around 50 Skill Development Centres were created. Thousands of post offices, bank branches, and ATMs were opened. The PM Janman Yojana was launched for the development of the most backward tribal regions. Through this, facilities such as water, electricity, and roads reached thousands of remote villages for the first time. Not only this, surrendered Naxalites were also given an opportunity. Special schemes were created for their rehabilitation. Friends, This campaign of "Sabka Saath, Sabka Vikas" has given the country's fight against poverty new energy and new confidence. That is why, on one hand, 50 crore people have come out of extreme poverty, and on the other hand, Naxalite violence has also come to an end in the country. Just look at Bastar itself. Today, the Bastar Olympics is being held in Bastar. Children are no longer picking up guns; they are trying their hands at sports. Friends, Amid these achievements, we must remember one more thing. The Naxalites have been eliminated, but the danger has not completely disappeared. Armed Naxalites have vanished from the forests, but intellectual Naxalites have captured many organisations. Even today, they are provoking people against the Constitution and democracy of the country. These intellectual Naxalites have also infiltrated several political parties and various organisations. We must identify them, defeat them, and secure the nation. Friends, Today's achievements inspire us, but they are not a pause. I say again, they are not a pause. These victories, these successes, are platforms for a new flight. This flight will carry the dreams of the nation, dreams that our generations have cherished for centuries. These will be the dreams of those freedom fighters who made countless sacrifices for independence. These will be the dreams of those brave soldiers who laid down their lives for Mother India on the nation's borders. These will be the dreams of those courageous personnel who sacrificed their lives in the fight against Naxalism and terrorism. Every day, you live by this spirit: "May your glory remain eternal, Mother. May your glory remain eternal, Mother. Whether we live for a few more days or not, May your glory remain eternal, Mother." Now is the time for this spirit of yours to transform into a new confidence. Now is the time to live for the nation and to take the nation to new heights. Friends, Whatever resolve our nation takes, it also possesses the capability to fulfil it. We had promised that we would remove Article 370 from Kashmir, and we fulfilled that promise. We had promised that we would build toilets in every household, and we fulfilled that promise. We had promised to expand the scope of vaccination, and we fulfilled that promise. Therefore, when we speak today about eliminating poverty, when we speak about a drug-free India, a narcotics-free India, when we speak about a Developed India, when we speak about a Self-Reliant India, these goals too will certainly be achieved. We must witness these dreams becoming reality before our very eyes. I have faith in you. I have faith in the youth power of my country. I have faith in the people of my nation. The country will certainly reach these goals. Your sacrifice and hard work will become the strong foundation of the grand edifice of a Developed India. With this faith, I once again extend my heartfelt greetings to all of you on the occasion of Diwali. Thank you all very much. Bharat Mata Ki Jai. Bharat Mata Ki Jai. Bharat Mata Ki Jai. Sardar Patel - Immortal forever, immortal forever. My dear countrymen, Today's event is somewhat different. Until now, we have always held this programme on Unity Day in the morning. This time, there has been a slight change. We have all gathered in the evening. In the morning, lakhs and lakhs of citizens participated in the Run for Unity with a pledge of national unity. We saw the enthusiasm of the people in the Run for Unity. Yesterday evening, I witnessed magnificent presentations at the Bharat Parv Pavilion. And now, on this national occasion, we are present at the feet of Sardar Patel. Friends, This is the world's tallest statue, the Statue of Unity. The participation of the entire nation in its construction, the unique aura of this place, and the extraordinary energy and inspiration of Unity Day fill one's heart with pride and gratitude upon coming here. Pride in being a citizen of this great nation, and gratitude towards Sardar Vallabhbhai Patel, who united more than 550 princely states after Independence. With an iron resolve, he bound this nation together in the thread of unity. Friends, Freedom is essential for any nation. When a nation is free, it determines its own future. But freedom receives the right direction through a republic. A republic shows us the path of taking decisions within the framework of the Constitution. The Constitution keeps us accountable to the people. And unity gives us the strength to move forward as one nation. That is why in our country, Independence Day, Republic Day, and Unity Day are all celebrated with great enthusiasm as national festivals. Friends, Once again today, in Ekta Nagar, we have witnessed the grand vision of India's unity. This year's National Unity Parade has also been extraordinary. This parade is dedicated to the courage and bravery of our police and security forces. Through the tableaux, the nation's heritage and aspirations have been presented before the country. Therefore, Unity Day is no longer limited merely to official events. Through public participation, it has become a celebration of national pride. On this occasion, I extend my greetings to all countrymen on National Unity Day. I bow with deep reverence at the feet of Sardar Vallabhbhai Patel. Friends, Sardar Patel is that great personality in India's history without whom it is difficult even to imagine present-day India. It was Sardar Patel who shaped the India we know today. Even after Partition, the person who kept India secure from the Himalayas to the Indian Ocean was Sardar Patel. Friends, Sardar Patel was able to do this because he understood the character and consciousness of India. He knew that India's diversity was not its weakness. He knew that India's diversity was, in fact, the true foundation of its unity. For thousands of years, we have lived in this manner. Through this very strength, we touched the heights of progress and prosperity. Friends, Sardar Patel once again laid the foundation of that India. He did not merely shape the geography of the nation; he also bound the country together through emotional unity. Through the reconstruction of the Somnath Temple in Gujarat, he also initiated the cultural renaissance of the nation. Friends, This year marks the 75th anniversary of that great work as well. Just as the flag of the Somnath Temple has been flying with pride for a thousand years despite the repeated destruction attempted by aggressors, that same spirit of self-respect inspired us to celebrate the Somnath Swabhiman Parva. And this year, the nation is celebrating that occasion with equal enthusiasm. My dear countrymen, Today, these great ceremonies of national unity, India's rising stature on the global stage, the rapid pace of our economic progress, and the revival of our sacred centres from Kashi Vishwanath to the Ram Temple, all point to the direction in which India is moving. It is a path shown to us by Sardar Patel. And for this, the nation will remain indebted to him forever. Friends, Last year, we celebrated the 150th birth anniversary of Sardar Patel. For two years, programmes and celebrations were organised across the country. We took major resolutions for the nation and pledged to fulfil them. And today, as we celebrate his 151st birth anniversary, we can say with satisfaction that the nation has fulfilled those resolutions within a very short period of time. In these two years, the country has achieved several unprecedented accomplishments. Friends, On the occasion of Sardar Patel's 150th birth anniversary, standing here at his feet, I had said, "Until the country is completely free from Naxalism and Maoist terror, we will not rest." And exactly one year later, as we stand here today, that national resolve has been fulfilled. Today, the backbone of Naxalism has been broken in the country. In 2015, 126 districts of the country were affected by Left-wing extremism. By March 2026, not a single district remained in the country that was still affected by Naxalism. These districts, which suffered for decades from violence and bloodshed, are now becoming part of the mainstream of development. Today, roads are expanding in these areas. Mobile networks are reaching people. Educational and healthcare facilities are increasing. Youth who once faced uncertainty now have new opportunities for education, employment, and progress. Friends, This time, at this Unity Day celebration, families from Naxal-affected regions have joined us here as special guests. I welcome all of them and wish them a bright future. Today, I also pay my respects to all those brave security personnel who made the supreme sacrifice to establish peace in these regions of the country. Friends, Today, India is more secure than before. Today, India is more determined than before. And one major reason for this is the increased confidence of our citizens. Confidence comes from achievements. Confidence comes from the sensitivity of the government and the system. Today, I would certainly like to speak about one such major national achievement. Friends, Talks of removing poverty in India have been going on since Independence. Votes were sought in the name of eradicating poverty. But we all know what the actual condition of poverty in the country was. Those who raised the slogan of removing poverty themselves kept becoming richer. Change in circumstances began to come in recent years. Just a few days ago, a World Bank report was released. The report states that in the ten to twelve years before 2024, fifty crore Indians defeated poverty. That means where earlier 27 out of every 100 people, more than one-fourth of the population, were trapped in extreme poverty, today that number has fallen to less than 3 out of every 100 people. Friends, The entire world is acknowledging that this is a major achievement. But how did this happen? It happened because for the past twelve years, the country has prioritised the welfare and empowerment of the poor. During these twelve years, the nation received a leak-proof system. Through DBT, every rupee sent from Delhi reached the poor. Free ration and free healthcare helped poor families save money. Crores of permanent houses, crores of toilets, and smoke-free kitchens made life easier for the poor. Crores of people received the protection of insurance, which became a source of support during times of crisis. Before 2014, even 25 crore people in the country did not have any social security coverage. Today, nearly 100 crore people in the country have been connected to a social security safety net. Friends, Today, when the entire nation is remembering and paying tribute to Sardar Saheb, his soul must surely be blessing us. Through such efforts, we will eliminate poverty from the country and build a Developed India. Friends, National unity guarantees a nation's stability and success. And in times of global instability such as these, the importance of unity has increased even more. You all know that today the world is burning in the fires of unrest. This is among the most difficult periods of the past several decades. Wars are taking place between many countries around the world. Major economies are struggling with challenges. The crisis of resources continues to grow. Global supply chains are becoming fragmented. At such a time, India's unity, the unity of all Indians, is our greatest strength. Because it is this unity that will help us overcome every storm. Brothers and sisters, In recent months, we have seen the world struggling with an energy crisis. In some countries, limits were even imposed on the purchase of petrol and diesel. It was being said that India would face the biggest crisis because it has such a large population and does not produce sufficient petroleum. Amid these concerns, anti-India forces also became active. But friends, This is India's strength. We did not allow the impact of this global energy crisis to fall upon our citizens. This is certainly a diplomatic and strategic success for the country, but the unity and solidarity of our people played an equally important role. While the nation was looking for solutions to external challenges, certain forces within the country were busy spreading lies and rumours in an attempt to create instability. But the people of India stood shoulder to shoulder with the nation. This is the greatest strength of this country. This is the strongest foundation of our confidence. Friends, When great leaders such as Sardar Patel and Babasaheb Ambedkar created the Constitution of India, they established the system of a ruling side and an opposition side. They also explained the importance of this arrangement. In a democracy, the responsibility of the opposition is to oppose government policies. But unfortunately, in today's India, the opposition has abandoned its duty and has transformed itself from an opposition into an obstruction. In its frustration and despair, it has begun placing obstacles in the path of the country's development. This is not opposition; this is obstruction. They have made it the basis of their politics. They are engaged in tearing apart the dignity and spirit of the Constitution. Friends, These forces are continuously engaged in conspiracies to divide the nation. Attempts are being made to divide and pit against one another the children of Mother India. Due to more than a decade of frustration and disappointment, these people have now come out in the open. We must identify who is secretly meeting whom abroad, which anti-India forces are supporting whom, and who is speaking the language of India's enemies. We must recognise these patterns. We must unite and confront them. Especially the way social media is being used as a weapon, the way rumours are being spread, and the efforts being made to damage India's image, all citizens must come together to deal with these challenges. We have ended Naxalism in remote regions. But the threat of intellectual Naxalism has not yet disappeared from the country. The nation must always remain vigilant against these intellectual Naxals. Friends, No matter how difficult the circumstances may be, we must not allow this grand mission of national unity and this journey towards a strong and capable India to stop. We must now work towards the next major goals. We must secure the nation's borders completely. We must stop infiltration and demographic changes caused by it. We must also move forward towards a Secular Civil Code. Friends, Whenever a nation takes great resolutions, whenever it takes major decisions, obstacles will arise, difficulties will appear, and storms will have to be faced. But India is no longer going to stop. The mantra of "Ek Bharat, Shreshtha Bharat" and the goal of a Developed India have now become India's unwavering resolve. We will push ourselves to the limits of hard work. We will confront every storm with determination. But our eyes will remain fixed on one objective alone: building a strong, capable, and developed India by 2047, when the nation completes 100 years of independence. This passion must be present in every effort we make and in every breath we take. This idea must beat with every heartbeat in our hearts. With this resolve, I once again extend my heartfelt greetings to all of you on National Unity Day. Bharat Mata Ki Jai! Bharat Mata Ki Jai! Bharat Mata Ki Jai! Vande Mataram. Vande Mataram. Union Cabinet colleagues Ashwini Vaishnaw ji, Jitin Prasada ji, all industry leaders, CEOs, delegates, and virtually connected hundreds of universities and institutes - the future of India, my young companions. And here present, the large number of enthusiastic youth, ladies and gentlemen! This is the fifth edition of Semicon India. I welcome you all. I extend a special welcome to the guests who have come from abroad. Friends, Today is also Vishwakarma Jayanti. I extend my best wishes to all countrymen on Vishwakarma Jayanti. In our tradition, Lord Vishwakarma is considered the symbol of the power of construction and creation. And see the coincidence - Semicon India begins today itself. On one side, our centuries‐old tradition of creation, and on the other side, the technology and vision of the 21st century - this is our inspiration for self‐reliant India, for developed India. Friends, Every citizen is committed to building a developed India. If we look at just the past 15 days - I draw your attention only to 15 days - we can see the direction and speed with which India is moving forward. In this September, just 10 days ago, the Global Fintech Fest was held in Mumbai. There was discussion on inclusive finance through agentic AI, tokenization, and quantum technologies. During the same period, nearly 3,000 kilometers of Dedicated Freight Corridor became fully operational in India. I am talking about September's 10 days, not long ago. This will speed up freight movement in the country, improve logistics, and give new strength to manufacturing. Friends, In this same period, in this September itself, India's quarterly GDP report came. And even in this challenging period, India achieved 7.8 percent growth. And during the same time, Japan's credit rating agency upgraded India's sovereign rating. And this happened after three decades - nearly 35 years. That means, on one side India's economy is growing, and on the other side, the world's trust in India is also increasing. Friends, Another big picture of this trust was seen just three days ago at the BRICS Summit. India successfully hosted this summit. The unanimous adoption of the New Delhi Declaration is another big proof of the world's trust in India. Friends, Amidst these achievements of India, this Semicon India event has become even more important. Its first edition was in 2022. Since then we meet every year, but every year the subject of our discussion has changed. In the first edition, we talked about policy. In the next editions, the discussion moved forward. We began talking about projects - from signing files to laying foundation stones. Last year we began discussing pilot lines and test chips. How the first samples began to come out of India's units - this was discussed. And this year, we are talking about commercial production. Now chips are coming out of India's plants and reaching customers in India and the world. And just now, in Mohali and Surat, commercial production has started in two more plants. I congratulate them as well. Friends, For any country, reaching here in such a short time is not easy. This is not a sector where you set up a factory and the work is done. It takes decades to build technological capabilities. Wherever semiconductor ecosystems have been built in the world, they have been prepared after decades of effort. But India, with your cooperation, has worked simultaneously on chip design, manufacturing, equipment manufacturing, materials ecosystem, testing capability - and has shown success. In this journey, you industry leaders have been the major stakeholders. You are the pillars of India's success, you are the partners in our journey. Friends, In the semiconductor industry, there is a word - "tape‐out." This is the day when a chip, after design completion, goes into production. But work does not stop after tape‐out. On that very day, work begins on the next version. And India is doing the same. India has started the second phase of the Semiconductor Mission. The size of our first phase was about 8 billion dollars, while the second phase we have set at 13.5 billion dollars. Moving on the Reform Express, we have made further reforms in this sector. Now international companies can also collaborate with our startups, Indian design companies, and OCI‐owned companies. That means whether it is policies, funds, regulatory frameworks - whatever is needed for the success of your projects, the Government of India is taking every step. Friends, Our effort is that fabless companies grow in India, and intellectual property creation also happens in India. Whether it is making different types of compound semiconductors, making silicon photonics part of the ecosystem, or enabling infrastructure for AI - all this is necessary. And in this direction, we must move forward together. The foundation needed for this has already been prepared. India is also focusing greatly on strengthening supply chains. Many reforms have been made for this. To the leaders of equipment companies present here, I say - do not lag behind in taking advantage of this. For leaders in chemicals, gases, and materials too, golden opportunities await in India. We want the entire ecosystem to grow together. For this, the demand pipeline is also ready. So far, 12 projects have been approved, and in the next phase this scope will expand further. Friends, Another advantage for all of you in India is youth - talented, skilled manpower. Today we have a very large pool of engineering talent. India has thousands of engineering colleges, and every year India produces lakhs of engineers. Once, a gentleman from an American company came to meet me. He said, "If we want to recruit engineers in our country, we call them, and they all fit in one room. But in India, if we call engineers, even a football field is too small." This is India's strength. And Friends, For chip designing, large‐scale skilling work is underway. Just before coming here, I met some young companions from industry on this very campus - all students. These students gifted me chips they had designed themselves. Our government has set a target to train one lakh engineering students for the semiconductor industry. And as you were told, we have already trained more than 70,000 youth. Such a large talent pool is nothing less than a gold mine for all of you. Friends, Amidst these government efforts, today I also appeal to the youth - young talents who are doing research in India should come forward. Those youth who are doing research abroad should also connect with the development of advanced technologies in India. I urge industry leaders to lead R&D in the country. This benefits industry, and it also strengthens India's youth power. Friends, The advancing semiconductor industry also faces some challenges, which you are all familiar with. For years, people in your industry talked about transistor size. As transistors became smaller, computing became faster and more efficient. Even today this question is important. But now, with the arrival of Artificial Intelligence, some new questions have arisen before this industry. In the past two years, the global semiconductor market has grown by more than 20 percent. And a very big part of this growth is due to demand created by AI. That means, today if you are working on AI, the challenges are not only about what is inside the chip, but also about what is happening around it. Bringing memory closer to the processor, connecting many chips in such a way that they work together as one system, transferring huge amounts of data from here to there without delay, managing heat and power - these are many challenges before the semiconductor industry. Amidst these challenges, the world most needs new and reliable manufacturing locations. And India - I assure you - India is continuously preparing itself for this. Friends, Another major issue before today's semiconductor industry is energy security. The chips being made for AI are used in AI data centers. In these data centers, thousands of high‐performance processors work together, processing huge amounts of data. As AI expands, the need for computing power will increase at the same pace. And as computing power increases, the need for electricity will also increase greatly. Therefore, investment in the power sector is very important. Where our plants are located, electricity must be available on time, in the required quantity when needed - this is very important. That is why India is making very large investments in the power sector today. In the past decade, our installed solar energy capacity has increased from about 2 gigawatts to more than 160 gigawatts - from 2 to 160. In nuclear energy too, we are moving forward rapidly. This sector has been opened to private players. We are also working on small modular reactors. In the past decade, India has also expanded its transmission network. Friends, This expansion of power infrastructure in India is also a great strength for our semiconductor ecosystem. India is taking every step needed to make itself a preferred destination for the semiconductor industry. Friends, India is the Mother of Democracy. Just a few years ago, our Constitution completed 75 years. When such a country succeeds in a critical industry, it strengthens the entire world's supply chain. And today, when supply chains are being weaponized, its importance increases even more. We must see the developments happening in India from this larger perspective. India's aspirations are before you, and the new opportunities being created in India are ready to welcome you. Once again, I extend many congratulations to all of you for this event. Thank you very much.
Lieutenant Governor Shri V. K. Saxena ji, all senior officials of the BRO and the Airport Authority, all officials of the Union Territory, other distinguished guests, my dear brothers and sisters. "Ladakh si Mi-Mangs Kunla, Jullay!" Today, another chapter is being added to the progress and development of Ladakh. I am happy that I am becoming a part of this occasion through technology. Today, several important development projects have been inaugurated in Ladakh. Through these projects, travel will become easier for many people, electricity facilities will improve in some places, water availability will increase in others, and the conservation of our ancient heritage will also receive new strength. Just now, I was watching that there was snowfall in Zanskar, and I was seeing the enthusiasm of the people of Zanskar, their excitement in waving the Tricolour even while the snow was falling. Friends, This is my Leh, my Ladakh. I extend my heartfelt congratulations to all the people of Ladakh for these projects. I also congratulate all the engineers, workers, and local companions involved in these projects. I especially appreciate the companions of the BRO. Friends, Some time ago, I had the privilege of laying the foundation stone for the terminal building of the Kushok Bakula Rimpochee Airport in Leh. And it is my good fortune that today I have also had the privilege of inaugurating this important project. This terminal, built at a cost of about six hundred and fifty crore rupees, will increase the airport's annual passenger capacity from one million to three and a half million. The number of flights here can also increase by up to three times. This terminal has another special feature. Keeping Ladakh's harsh climatic conditions in mind, geothermal technology has been used in it. Solar energy has also been incorporated. This is the country's first airport terminal equipped with geothermal heating facilities in India. Congratulations to all of you. Our effort is that modern infrastructure should develop in Ladakh while also ensuring complete care for the environment here. This airport terminal will also provide new energy to the tourism sector in Ladakh. I extend my heartfelt congratulations to all my brothers and sisters of Ladakh for this modern airport terminal. Friends, The other projects inaugurated today are also of great importance. Today, the Nimmu-Padum-Darcha road has been dedicated to the people of Ladakh. About two thousand five hundred crore rupees have been spent on it. A journey that earlier took around twelve hours can now be completed in approximately three hours. This means that not only will travel time be saved, but this road will also greatly support trade and commerce. Friends, Earlier, I had the privilege of laying the foundation stone and inaugurating the power transmission line connecting Srinagar and Leh. Today, the Hanu hydroelectric project and its associated transmission line have also been inaugurated. We all know that a reliable electricity system brings not only light, but also illuminates lives. It brings major changes to life. Many new activities become possible in villages. Children's education, healthcare services, and small local businesses, every sector will benefit from it. Work is also progressing on a modern electricity generation project in the Puga Valley. Friends, Today, forty reservoirs built under Project Amrit Him Sarovar have also been dedicated to the people of Ladakh. These will help farmers obtain irrigation water whenever needed. Friends, It has always been said that life in Ladakh is very difficult because of nature. Natural conditions do present their own challenges. But unfortunately, despite these circumstances, previous governments did not give priority to the development of Ladakh. Today, however, the development of Ladakh is a major priority for the central government. Since becoming a Union Territory, even greater attention has been given to its development needs. Until 2019, Ladakh had about 1,800 kilometres of road network. Today, it has increased to more than 4,000 kilometres. Earlier, there were nineteen major bridges here. Today, their number has increased to about seventy-five. The number of helipads has also increased from seven to more than forty. Earlier, only about three and a half percent of houses in Ladakh had access to tap water. Today, nearly 99 percent of homes have access to tap water. The number of beds in Ladakh's hospitals has also doubled. Along with this, work on important projects such as the Zojila Tunnel is progressing very rapidly. Once completed, it will provide Ladakh with improved year-round connectivity to the rest of the country. Friends, The central government is also focusing on creating new avenues of prosperity in Ladakh. Today, opportunities in tourism are increasing here. Winter tourism and adventure tourism are being promoted. Under the Vibrant Villages Programme, the central government and we are developing more than forty border villages in Ladakh, and we call them the "first villages" of India. This is creating employment opportunities even in remote areas. Friends, Earlier, Ladakh had around one hundred and fifty homestays. Today, their number has increased to three thousand. Greenhouse farming has also been consistently promoted in Ladakh. Today, there are eleven thousand greenhouses here. These have created new possibilities for increasing farmers' incomes. Friends, The demand for a High Court Bench in Ladakh had also existed for a long time. Our government has fulfilled that demand as well. In August this year, approval was granted for the establishment of a High Court Bench in Ladakh. This will make both administration and the justice system more accessible. Friends, Today, digital technology is also bringing many new opportunities closer. Whether it is hotels, restaurants, homestays, or local products, online business is opening new avenues of income. That is why we have expanded BharatNet and high-speed internet in Ladakh and other remote areas. Continuous work is also being done to ensure easier access to government services, to enable youth to study online, and to connect local entrepreneurs to national and global markets. Friends, We are paying special attention to the new generation of Ladakh. The youth here should receive quality education, opportunities to learn new technologies, and should not have to depend on other cities. Therefore, a major Centre of Excellence is being established in Leh. Proposals are also being advanced for satellite campuses of IIT Jammu and IIM Jammu. These will create better opportunities for higher education, research, and advanced skills for the youth of Ladakh within their own region. Friends, Ladakh also has enormous potential in sports. The youth here have created a distinct identity in areas such as ice hockey, winter sports, and mountaineering. I am happy that efforts are being made to promote sporting talent through Ladakh's new sports policy. Our effort is to ensure that young people here receive better training and facilities so that they can achieve new milestones at national and international levels. Friends, The products that all of you create in Ladakh through your hard work are truly excellent. You already know that whenever I meet foreign guests, I often present products made in Ladakh as gifts and also tell them about all of you. I have gifted products made in Ladakh to the Chancellor of Germany, the Prime Minister of Sweden, and the President of Kyrgyzstan. The pashmina of this region, sea buckthorn, local handicrafts, and fruits such as apricots all carry immense commercial potential. That is why we granted a GI tag to Ladakh's apricots. Today, their demand is increasing across the world. Our effort is to ensure that these products find new markets, receive better branding, and that the benefits reach the producers directly. We are also paying special attention to the pastoralists of the Changpa community. Efforts are being made to further strengthen the identity of the pashmina associated with their hard work and traditional skills. Commercial cultivation of flowers in high-altitude regions is also being promoted in Ladakh. Friends, The cultural heritage of Ladakh is a priceless treasure of our nation. The ancient monasteries here, the Buddhist traditions, and the local festivals must be preserved and safeguarded for future generations. In this direction, the contribution of the revered Kushok Bakula Rimpochee ji is a great inspiration for all of us. Our government's effort is that people across the world become familiar with the culture and traditions of Ladakh. Keeping this mission in mind, India's first Petroglyph Conservation Park has been inaugurated in Leh today. This will further strengthen efforts to preserve Ladakh's ancient heritage. Future generations will be able to understand this heritage, and visitors from around the world will also become acquainted with Ladakh's rich history. Friends, I am confident that together we will take Ladakh to new heights of development. With this, I once again extend my heartfelt congratulations to all of you for these many development works. Thank you very much. Jullay!
Bharat Mata ki Jai. Bharat Mata ki Jai. Governor of Gujarat Acharya Devvrat ji, the popular Chief Minister of Gujarat Shri Bhupendra Bhai Patel, many distinguished leaders from other states who have joined us online - Maharashtra Governor Jishnu Dev Varma ji, Chief Minister Devendra Fadnavis ji, Deputy CM Eknath Shinde ji, Deputy CM Sunetra Pawar ji, other ministers, MPs, MLAs, and the lakhs of citizens gathered there. On stage here, my colleagues from the Union Cabinet Ashwini Vaishnaw, Deputy CM and young leader Harsh Sanghvi, ministers of the Gujarat government, other public representatives present, and my dear brothers and sisters of Gujarat. And especially, because this Maharaja Sayajirao University ground of Vadodara is hosting companions from different fields together, this festival of development is happening here all at once. Friends, Today I have come among you to give more speed to the journey of a developed India. Just a short while ago, projects worth thousands of crores of rupees connected to the country's development were inaugurated and foundation stones laid here. I extend heartfelt congratulations to the people of the nation. On this festival of development, the way you all have carried out a cleanliness and welcome campaign across Vadodara city with a spirit of service, I heartily congratulate every citizen of Vadodara and appreciate them. And the color you have shown - yesterday I was watching a video, my chest swelled with pride. Wow! Is this my Vadodara? And now preparations for Ganeshotsav, later Navratri. During Ganeshotsav and Navratri, the whole of Gujarat's attention is on Vadodara. And today I saw, for the past week, every family has created an atmosphere of cleanliness. I feel that this time when Lord Ganesh arrives, all blessings will shower upon Vadodara. Friends, This year from the Red Fort I called for the Saptdharas (Seven Streams) of resolve for a developed India. One of these streams is Gati Shakti. For a developed India we need connectivity that is smooth and also fast according to the times. Vadodara is the city of Gati Shakti University. Today, the last links of the Dedicated Freight Corridor have also been joined from here. Today, the project of more than 2,800 kilometers of Dedicated Freight Corridor has been completed. This is a very historic achievement for 21st‐century India. Friends, This Dedicated Freight Corridor is proof of how the work culture of the country has changed in the past 12 years. The idea of this corridor began in 2004. That means, I see all the youth here - when this idea began, many of you had not even been born. In 2006, a special company was created to carry out this work, and then until 2014, the files just kept moving from here to there. Files were traveling on tables, and unfortunately, even those files did not get a dedicated corridor. Files were stuck, hanging, wandering. And in 2014, the people of Vadodara, Gujarat, and the nation sent me from Gujarat to Delhi. The earlier government remained until 2014. You sent me, and they departed. The truth was that in seven-eight years - my young friends, listen carefully and remember - in seven-eight years not even one kilometer of the freight corridor was completed. If the country had continued at their pace, perhaps even after three-four generations it would not have been done. This was the condition they had left of a project meant to transform India's logistics ecosystem. Friends, After 2014, the education and lessons you had given me here, what this land had taught me, the lessons I carried with me to Delhi - as soon as we reached there, we began to give speed to this project. Today, the 2,800‐kilometer corridor has been completed. And today the Eastern and Western Dedicated Freight Corridors are becoming the main foundation of the country's trade and commerce. Friends, The Dedicated Freight Corridor is the lifeline of rapidly developing India. It has transformed India's transportation sector. Earlier, passenger trains ran on the same tracks as freight trains. That meant both travel and transport were slow. But the Dedicated Freight Corridor has changed this situation. Today, on the Eastern and Western freight corridors, more than 430 freight trains run every day - 430 freight trains daily. Earlier, a freight train took six and a half hours to cover 100 kilometers. Now the same freight train covers 100 kilometers in less than half that time. Where sending goods by truck normally took 30 hours, on the Dedicated Freight Corridor the same distance is completed in 10-12 hours. That means, with freight trains running on this corridor, fuel is saved compared to trucks and trains, freight costs are reduced, time is saved, and the environment benefits. That means, investment was made on one track, and the benefits have been many. Friends, Through this freight corridor, the produce of farmers from eastern and western India is now reaching big markets more quickly. Many varieties of flowers, vegetables, fruits - items that spoil quickly depending on the season - now have a way to reach mandis and consumers at the right time. This has greatly benefited farmers. Friends, This freight corridor is remarkable for another reason. Recently, from JNPT Mumbai port to Vadodara, a double‐stack container freight train was operated. That means one container was placed on top of another, and the train ran. With this train, in one trip, goods equal to more than 250 trucks were transported from Mumbai to Vadodara - 250 trucks. Now, the "angry uncle" types will shout, "What will happen to the truck owners? Where will the trucks go? Those who bought trucks, what will happen to them?" So they have found new work. But in reality, now large quantities of goods can move quickly from one corner of the country to another. Friends, Along with the freight corridor, railways are now reaching places where they had not reached for decades. In Gujarat, Madhya Pradesh, Maharashtra - the tribal areas here are also being connected to rail through this project. In earlier governments, in the budget, one or two trains would be announced, and they would beat drums about it for a whole year. Whereas today, throughout the year, one new train after another is being launched. Even in today's program, several new trains have started. The new train will bring great convenience to the people of Vadodara, Chhota Udaipur, and Alirajpur. Friends, Roads, railways, metros, airports, gas pipelines, homes for the poor - when these things are built for crores of people in the country, life becomes easier, and at the same time, millions of employment opportunities are created for workers, from small to large. Take railways alone - this year's rail budget has been more than 2.75 lakh crore rupees, which is many times higher compared to 12 years ago. That means the expenditure on railway modernization is creating thousands upon thousands of new jobs. And these jobs are created during construction itself. Once roads, railways, pipelines, ports, airports are built, then new industries start coming up around them, new businesses arrive, and these create new jobs. Friends, I have placed before the country the vision of Saptdharas (seven streams) of resolve for a developed India. One of these streams is manufacturing. And Vadodara city knows and understands this strength very well. How education, skill, and industry collaborate - Vadodara has reflected this for nearly 150 years. Decades ago, the journey here began with textiles and tiles manufacturing, which later advanced with oil and petrochemicals. In the past two-two and a half decades, Vadodara has become a major hub of MSMEs. And I remember, when I had just become Chief Minister, Vadodara always had a special claim on me. Every week some delegation would come, saying, "Do something for manufacturing in Vadodara." Because when you came here, you would see Jyoti, LMB, GSFC - that was all, nothing else was visible. Today, how much has changed! Now this city is also known for the country's first Made in India military transport aircraft. The C‐295 aircraft's first flight has already been seen in the skies here. I heartily congratulate the people of Vadodara for attaining this pride. Friends, When we talk about the country's manufacturing, I want to share another figure. In the past decade, India has become a major manufacturer of rail coaches, metro coaches, and rail engines. Since 2014, India has manufactured nearly 50,000 modern railway coaches. Listen to this figure - since 2014, India has made 50,000 modern coaches. The earlier government could not even build 50,000 toilets in 10 years. We have made 50,000 modern coaches, and nearly 250,000 wagons for freight trains have been added to Indian Railways. Friends, Now India is writing a new saga of manufacturing - from chip to ship. That means the parts are ours and the products are ours; a complete chain of self‐reliance will be built within India itself. We are seeing this in the case of solar power. For example, the floating solar plant project at Kadana Dam. A few years ago, setting up solar projects in the country was very expensive, because PV modules and all types of hardware had to be imported. But in recent years we have started manufacturing PV modules in India itself. Today we have developed a capacity of more than 200 gigawatts. And because of such self‐reliance, the PM Surya Ghar Free Electricity Scheme is expanding. So far, 5.5 million families in the country have installed solar plants on their rooftops. Among them, 1.1 million families are from Gujarat. So many families here have become electricity producers, and because of this, from production to installation and service, countless youths have got employment, and many new vendors have emerged. Friends, Those who spread the "echo of lies" can be found at every crossroads, but India's youth walk with the roar of truth, move with the roar of truth, and live for the roar of truth. Our young companions are seeing how today the government is working with focus in every sector - solar, wind, hydro, nuclear. Friends, In the coming era, every modern sector will run on electricity, will depend on the electric ecosystem. For example, the chip industry, data centers, and industries connected to AI - their food and water is electricity. Therefore, if we want to become a superpower in AI before the world, the country must prepare and build its ecosystem. This is exactly what India is doing on the ground. That is why agreements are being made with some countries for uranium, with others for critical minerals and rare earth minerals. All this effort is being made day and night to secure the future of the country's youth - for your future. Friends, The world is changing rapidly, so the needs of the world are also changing. And in the same way, our methods of study and learning are also changing. Earlier the focus was only on degrees, but now along with degrees, the focus is also on skills. The importance of skill is increasing. It has become a major need that youth should have skills, and those skills should be upgraded from time to time. That is why today we are reshaping our education policy, our teaching and training institutions in a new form. And with this spirit, from the Red Fort I also made two major announcements for youth power. The first announcement is connected with the country's traditional need. You all know that in our country millions of youth prepare for competitive exams. Every year, a large part of the income of our middle‐class families is spent on coaching for these children. Our effort is to save the money of such poor and middle‐class families. That is why now the government is moving forward to provide students with free online coaching arrangements. And coaching will be given by India's best teachers. For our youth who are preparing, the government itself will take care of their coaching. Friends, It is equally important for India's youth to be proficient in AI. We want our young generation to be prepared for AI. It is not that the need for AI learners is only in the IT sector. If someone knows this skill, he can create new AI solutions for agriculture. He can develop technology in the field of healthcare. He can increase productivity in manufacturing. He can start his own startup in robotics. Therefore, it is necessary that we have youth who understand AI and use AI for the industries of the country and the world. The government is going to launch a major campaign for this. Friends, This period of India is unprecedented. This is the era of the rise of India's youth power. This time will not return again in India's life. Therefore, we must keep the goal of a developed India in 2047 as supreme. We must make India developed. With this call, once again I extend many congratulations to you all for the development works. Now I have one task for the people of Vadodara - will you do it? Surely? You have made Vadodara so clean, it took so much effort. I saw our youth working day and night in cleaning. But let us decide once that now we will not allow Vadodara to become dirty again. If each of us takes the pledge, "I will not create dirt myself," then will Vadodara become dirty? Will Vadodara become dirty? Then Diwali will shine brilliantly, Navratri will be celebrated splendidly, Ganeshotsav will be adorned with glory. So this much work will surely be done. Well done. Thank you. Bharat Mata ki Jai! Bharat Mata ki Jai! Vande Mataram! Vande Mataram! Vande Mataram! Vande Mataram! Vande Mataram! Vande Mataram! Vande Mataram! Vande Mataram! Vande Mataram! Vande Mataram! Vande Mataram!
Your Excellency, President Marcos, Your Majesty, Excellencies, Namaskar! I warmly congratulate President Marcos for the excellent organisation of the East Asia Summit. Friends, Since the COVID pandemic, the world has been facing one crisis after another. Conflicts are putting increasing pressure on food, fuel, and fertiliser supplies. Ships and seafarers are also being targeted on international waterways. Alongside the serious threat of terrorism, new forms of cross-border crime, such as cyber fraud and online scams, are also on the rise. Friends, Today, what the world needs most is peace, stability, and predictability. For many years, the East Asia Summit has served as an important platform for finding solutions to shared challenges and strengthening mutual cooperation. This very spirit of dialogue, mutual respect, and cooperation is the cornerstone of India's foreign policy. ASEAN unity and centrality are at the heart of our Act East Policy. We are committed to a Free, Open, Inclusive, and Rules-based Indo-Pacific. Whether it is Ukraine or West Asia, we will continue to support every effort towards peace. Friends, Our seas are the lifelines of our shared prosperity. Ensuring their security and freedom of navigation is the responsibility of us all. In this direction, we are launching a Seafarers' Safety Initiative, under which we will enhance cooperation in joint training and emergency response. Friends, Ensuring trust and security in the digital world is equally essential. At the AI Impact Summit held in India this year, we emphasized the development of human-centric, inclusive, and safe AI. We also emphasized the need for technologies such as digital watermarking to combat deepfakes and misinformation. We welcome the Joint Statement issued today on combating online scams. To effectively address this growing threat, we propose hosting the East Asia Summit Cyber Crime Conference at India's National Forensic Sciences University. Friends, The foundation of our relationship lies in a shared heritage that goes back thousands of years. The National Maritime Heritage Complex, being developed at Lothal in India, will soon showcase this shared heritage to the world. To further strengthen our historical and cultural ties, we will also host the East Asia Summit Maritime Heritage Festival at Lothal. Next year, we will host the East Asia Summit University Heads Conclave at Nalanda, which will provide fresh impetus to our cooperation in the fields of education and research. Friends, This year, we are commemorating the 50th anniversary of the historic Treaty of Amity and Cooperation in Southeast Asia. Fifty years ago, this region gave the world an important message, the message of peace, respect for sovereignty, and dialogue. Today, that message has become even more relevant. Our shared future will be shaped not by conflict, but by mutual respect and cooperation. With this vision, India will continue to stand shoulder to shoulder with ASEAN, remaining committed to regional peace, stability, and shared prosperity. Thank you very much.
President of Kyrgyzstan, Excellencies, Namaskar! It is a matter of great pleasure for me to be here amongst all of you on this special occasion of the 25th anniversary of the SCO. I sincerely thank President Japarov for the warm welcome and gracious hospitality extended to us in Bishkek. We had the privilege of being here yesterday, on the occasion of Kyrgyzstan's Independence Day. I once again extend my warmest congratulations to the President and the people of Kyrgyzstan. And today, on the occasion of Uzbekistan's Independence Day, I extend my warmest congratulations to the President and the people of Uzbekistan as well. During the spectacular Opening Ceremony of the World Nomad Games, we all caught a glimpse of Kyrgyzstan's rich culture and our shared heritage. This shared heritage and our deep people-to-people connections are among the great strengths of the SCO family. Friends, Over the past twenty-five years, the SCO has developed a strong tradition of dialogue and cooperation across the vast expanse of Eurasia. Together, we have made valuable contributions not only to the well-being of our people, but also to the advancement of humanity. Over these twenty-five years, we have laid a strong foundation for cooperation. In the next twenty-five years, our goal should be to translate this cooperation into tangible outcomes. At a time when the world is passing through uncertainty and instability, we must transform our shared geography into shared opportunities. And through our efforts, we must bring about positive change in the lives of our people. Friends, At the previous meeting, I had outlined the three main pillars of India's vision for the SCO: S - Security, C - Connectivity, and O - Opportunity The time has come to move these three pillars beyond vision and translate them into concrete outcomes. The first and foremost requirement for this is peace and security. Terrorism continues to pose a serious challenge to humanity as a whole. In the fight against terrorism, we cannot remain confined to an action-reaction approach. We must dismantle the entire ecosystem of terrorist financing, recruitment, radicalisation, and safe havens. We must speak with one voice and make it clear that there can be no place for double standards on this serious issue. We must send a strong message to countries that use terrorism as an instrument of policy and provide safe haven and support to terrorists that terrorism can never be a strategic asset for anyone. Our shared aspiration for a peaceful and secure Eurasia is also closely linked to peace and stability in Afghanistan. India has been contributing to the development and provision of humanitarian assistance to the people of Afghanistan and will continue to do so. In today's interconnected world, the scope of security has become even broader. The crisis in West Asia has demonstrated that a conflict in one region does not remain confined to that region. Its impact is felt across the world, affecting energy security, maritime trade, and supply chains. The countries of the Global South bear the greatest brunt of such disruptions. Therefore, India has consistently called for all tensions and conflicts to be resolved not on the battlefield, but through dialogue and diplomacy. Drug trafficking is not merely a crime; it is a serious threat to our younger generation and regional security. The Centres being established under the SCO to address challenges such as security threats, organised crime, information security, and narcotics are a positive step. We must make these Centres an effective instrument for practical coordination and timely action. Friends, The second pillar of our shared development is strong and reliable connectivity. India supports all efforts that connect markets, facilitate trade, and open up new avenues for development. However, it is equally essential that such efforts respect the sovereignty and territorial integrity of all countries. This is also the fundamental spirit of the SCO Charter. Going forward, the scope of connectivity will become even broader. Along with roads, railways, and air corridors, we must simplify customs processes, promote digital documentation, and make logistics networks more efficient. Friends, The third pillar is - Opportunity. It means that the benefits of SCO cooperation should reach our people directly. Our greatest strength is the connections between our people. For India, the SCO is a confluence of great civilisations, cultures, and ideas. Over the next twenty-five years of the SCO, people-to-people ties should remain at the heart of our cooperation. The true measure of our success should be how many new opportunities our efforts create for young people, how many new markets open up for our entrepreneurs, how much our farmers benefit from technology, and how much better the lives of our citizens become. This is the true meaning of "Security, Connectivity, and Opportunity." Our cooperation in the SCO should not be merely government-driven; it should also be people-driven. I am pleased that, under Kyrgyzstan's chairmanship, special emphasis has been placed on youth engagement within the SCO. India has taken initiatives such as the SCO Start-up Forum, Young Authors' Conclave, and Young Scientists' Forum. Last year, India proposed the SCO Civilizational Dialogue Forum. I am pleased that its first session will be held in India this year. I am confident that with the active participation of all of you, this forum will become a strong platform for vibrant dialogue within the SCO. Friends, Kyrgyzstan's great literary figure, Chingiz Aitmatov, gave the world a beautiful and profound thought. He said that we often focus more on the things that divide us, and less on the things that unite us. This message is equally relevant for the SCO today. Our diversity is our identity, and our shared purpose is our strength. From Bishkek, we should move forward with this resolve: From shared geography to shared opportunities, from vision to outcomes, and from government-driven cooperation to people-driven partnership. With the same belief and commitment, India is ready to contribute to the SCO family's shared journey. Thank you very much.
